## Execution Windows (V1 Carve-Out)

Plan-008 ships in two windows — a **Tier 1 bootstrap-deliverable** (tRPC server skeleton + `sessionRouter` + SSE substrate) that unblocks [Plan-001](./001-shared-session-core.md) Phase 5, and a **Tier 5 remainder** that completes the relay/presence/invite surface. The split is documented authoritatively in [cross-plan-dependencies.md §5 Plan-008 Bootstrap-vs-Remainder Carve-Out](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#plan-008-bootstrap-vs-remainder-carve-out-tier-1--tier-5); this section is the plan-side restatement so engineers reading Plan-008 in isolation see the split.

The carve-out follows the **substrate-vs-namespace decomposition rule** documented authoritatively in [Plan-007 §Execution Windows](./007-local-ipc-and-daemon-control.md#execution-windows-v1-carve-out) — the _transport substrate_ (tRPC server + sessionRouter + SSE plumbing) is what Plan-001 Phase 5 consumes; the _relay/presence behavior_ is what Plan-008 owns canonically. Substrate ships first.

### Tier 1 — Plan-008-Bootstrap (tRPC server + `sessionRouter` + SSE substrate)

Lands alongside Plan-001 to unblock Plan-001 Phase 5 (`sessionClient` over the control-plane transport). Scope:

- **tRPC v11 server skeleton** — Cloudflare Workers host (entry point: `fetchRequestHandler` from `@trpc/server/adapters/fetch`) + tRPC v11 router registration scaffolding per [ADR-014](../decisions/014-trpc-control-plane-api.md). Local development uses the workerd runtime via `wrangler dev` (Miniflare v3); Tier 5 production deploys to Cloudflare Workers without runtime change. Bootstrap ships only the skeleton; relay broker / presence register / invite handlers are Tier 5.
- **`sessionRouter` HTTP handlers** — typed tRPC procedures for `SessionCreate`, `SessionRead`, `SessionJoin` exposing the existing `packages/control-plane/src/sessions/session-directory-service.ts` (already shipped in Plan-001 Phase 4). The router wraps the service; it does not re-implement directory logic.
- **SSE substrate for `SessionSubscribe`** — `SessionSubscribe` is request-only on the wire — the response is an `AsyncIterable<EventEnvelope>` SSE stream per `packages/contracts/src/session.ts#SessionSubscribeRequest`. The Tier 1 bootstrap ships the SSE transport plumbing (tRPC `subscription` procedure + Server-Sent-Events HTTP framing) that `sessionClient.subscribe` consumes from the control-plane side. Event sourcing into the stream remains Plan-006's domain; Plan-008-bootstrap supplies only the transport.

### Tier 5 — Plan-008-Remainder (relay + presence + invite acceptance)

Lands at Plan-008's original Tier 5 slot once Plan-002 (invite/presence) is complete. Tier 5 placement is unchanged because relay coordination depends on Plan-002. Scope:

- **Relay broker** (`relay-broker-service.ts`) — pairwise X25519 + XChaCha20-Poly1305 negotiation, relay sharding, WebSocket Hibernation per the original [§Implementation Steps](#implementation-steps) below.
- **Presence register fan-out** — Plan-008-remainder adds a **sibling** hosted-relay-presence-fan-out file under Plan-002's `packages/control-plane/src/presence/` directory. Plan-002 owns `presence-register-service.ts` (shipped NS-26 / PR #108); Plan-008-remainder EXTENDS the **directory**, never the file (CP-008-6; F-008r-D).
- **Invite-acceptance handoff** — wires Plan-002 invite resolution into the Tier 1 `sessionRouter`. **Widened 2026-08-11 (BL-133 amendment, CP-002-9):** the handoff leg also owns the daemon-side invite surface — the daemon IPC handlers for invite create/accept/revoke/preview, the live control-plane caller that replaces the deps-injected seam, and the `membershipClient.preview` client-SDK method — consumed from Plan-002's contracts (`Plan-002 §Cross-Plan Obligations` CP-002-9); Plan-002 ships the control-plane router + service, this leg ships the daemon/client consumption.
- **Reconnect association + relay negotiation** — `RelayNegotiation`, `SessionResumeAfterReconnect`, presence re-association.
- **Postgres tables** — `session_directory` and `relay_connections` per [Shared Postgres Schema](../architecture/schemas/shared-postgres-schema.md).
- **Client surfaces** — `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/session-join/`, `apps/cli/src/session-join/`, and `packages/client-sdk/src/sessionJoinClient.ts`.

## Goal

Implement authenticated shared-session join, presence registration, and relay negotiation without shifting execution authority into the control plane.

## Scope

This plan covers join APIs, invite acceptance handoff, presence registration, relay negotiation, reconnect association, and client surfaces for shared-session join.

## Non-Goals

- Local IPC implementation
- Runtime-node attach internals
- Provider transport protocols

## Invariants

The following invariants are **load-bearing** and MUST be preserved across all Plan-008 PRs and downstream extensions. They split by execution window because Plan-008's scope expands materially between Tier 1 bootstrap and Tier 5 remainder.

### I-008-1 — Tier 1 bootstrap is operator-development-only behind a feature flag

Plan-008-bootstrap (Tier 1) ships the tRPC v11 server skeleton + `sessionRouter` + SSE substrate to unblock Plan-001 Phase 5. **It does not constitute a production-ready control-plane host.** The bootstrap MUST be gated behind a feature flag (or equivalent operator-only entry point) and MUST NOT be exposed on a non-loopback bind path or routed to from production deployments until Plan-008-remainder lands at Tier 5.

**Why load-bearing.** `Spec-027 §Required Behavior` governs the daemon-side bind-time secure-defaults surface (Plan-007's domain), but the SaaS Cloudflare Workers host that Plan-008-bootstrap creates is **explicitly outside Spec-027 scope** per [ADR-020](../decisions/020-v1-deployment-model-and-oss-license.md) (the SaaS deployment model is operator-provisioned hosting; Spec-027 governs self-host operator-facing daemon defaults). Production auth, rate-limiting (Plan-021), PASETO v4 token validation (Plan-018), and relay encryption (Plan-008-remainder) are all **NOT YET PRESENT** at Tier 1 — exposing the bootstrap to production traffic would bypass those surfaces entirely.

**Verification.** I-008-1 has TWO gates that BOTH must be enforced:

1. **Feature-flag gate.** Tier 1 PR must wire the bootstrap behind a feature flag (e.g., `CONTROL_PLANE_BOOTSTRAP_ENABLED=1`) that defaults off; production deployment configurations MUST NOT set the flag until Tier 5. Negative-path test: handler refuses to serve when the flag is unset in a production `wrangler` environment.
2. **Approved-dev-environment allow-list gate (per F-008b-1-04 — Workers-runtime equivalent of the original loopback-bind gate; allow-list pivot per Codex PR #20 round 4).** The Worker handler MUST refuse to serve any request unless the deployed environment is the explicit local-development marker (`env.ENVIRONMENT === 'development'`), returning HTTP 503 immediately before any router dispatch even when the feature flag is set. This is **allow-list semantics**, not deny-list — the gate refuses on `undefined`, `'production'`, `'staging'`, `'test'`, `''`, and any value other than the explicit `'development'` marker. The allow-list framing closes the round-4 exposure path Codex flagged: a deny-list `!== 'production'` gate would still pass on a default `wrangler deploy` Worker (no `--env`) where `ENVIRONMENT` is unset (because `ENVIRONMENT="development"` is also moved out of top-level `[vars]` to `.dev.vars` per the round-4 amendment — see T-008b-1-1), even after `wrangler secret put CONTROL_PLANE_BOOTSTRAP_ENABLED 1` (which targets the same default top-level Worker). Allow-list semantics require the `ENVIRONMENT` value itself to be explicitly present, so the only path to satisfying both gates on a deployed Worker is adversarial-admin behavior (`wrangler secret put ENVIRONMENT development` against a deployed Worker), which is outside I-008-1's accidental-exposure threat model. Cloudflare Workers do not expose a host-bind surface (the runtime maps requests via Cloudflare's edge routing per [ADR-014](../decisions/014-trpc-control-plane-api.md)), so the original Fastify-era loopback-bind gate translates to environment-scoped allow-list refusal. Positive-path test: `wrangler dev` (workerd locally) serves with flag set because `.dev.vars` supplies `ENVIRONMENT=development`. Negative-path test: table-driven over `undefined`, `'production'`, `'staging'`, `'test'`, `''` — handler refuses with 503 in every row even with flag set. Coordinate language with Spec-027's bind-time secure-defaults pattern (Plan-007's domain) — Spec-027 governs the daemon-side bind surface; I-008-1 is the SaaS-host parallel for the control-plane bootstrap. **Tier 5 gate-semantics obligation:** at Tier 5, the allow-list MUST widen to include the explicit `'production'` marker once relay/presence/invite surfaces ship and PASETO/rate-limiting (Plan-018, Plan-021) gate production traffic — tracked as part of I-008-2 Tier 5 wiring.

### I-008-2 — Tier 5 production invariants apply once their substrates land

The production-grade invariants that apply **at Tier 5** — PASETO v4 token validation on every endpoint (per [ADR-010](../decisions/010-paseto-webauthn-mls-auth.md) + [Plan-018](./018-identity-and-participant-state.md), the co-tier Tier 5 PASETO issuer), relay-broker encryption (pairwise X25519 + XChaCha20-Poly1305 per [ADR-010](../decisions/010-paseto-webauthn-mls-auth.md)), and invite-acceptance handoff to [Plan-002](./002-invite-membership-and-presence.md) (Tier 2, shipped NS-14/NS-26) — decompose into the concrete, individually-testable guarantees **I-008-4 … I-008-11** below. Plan-008-remainder MUST gate those surfaces on their dependencies being satisfied per [cross-plan-dependencies.md §5](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#5-canonical-build-order).

**Rate-limiting is NOT a Tier-5 surface (mis-tiering correction).** The original I-008-2 listed "rate-limiting middleware (per Plan-021)" among the surfaces that "apply at Tier 5 once … Plan-021 … substrates land" — but [Plan-021](./021-rate-limiting-policy.md) is **Tier 6, strictly downstream of Plan-008** (per [Plan-021 §Tier Placement](./021-rate-limiting-policy.md#tier-placement): "Strictly downstream of Plan-008 — this plan consumes Plan-008's tRPC router and WS frame hook"). Plan-021's substrate cannot "land at Tier 5"; it lands at Tier 6 and **mounts its `rateLimitProcedure` middleware onto Plan-008's `packages/control-plane/src/server/` host** (per [cross-plan-dependencies.md §2 `packages/control-plane/src/server/` row](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#2-package-path-ownership-map): "Plan-021 (Tier 6 — rate-limiter middleware mount)"). Plan-008-remainder therefore exposes a stable mount surface but does **not** wire rate-limiting — exactly mirroring [Plan-002 CP-002-3](./002-invite-membership-and-presence.md#cross-plan-obligations) (invite-endpoint rate-limit deferred to Tier 6 post-Plan-021, [BL-120](../backlog.md)). The reciprocal obligation is **CP-008-5** below.

**Why load-bearing.** Shipping Tier 5 surfaces without their dependencies would either silently no-op (auth bypass) or hard-fail at runtime. The dependency tier graph is the only way to keep the substrate composition correct.

**Verification.** Tier 5 PRs must reference and wire the actual Plan-018 (PASETO issuer) + Plan-002 (invite/presence) surfaces — not stubs. Integration tests must exercise the full PASETO-auth + relay-encryption stack end-to-end before Tier 5 PRs merge (concrete per-surface assertions: I-008-4 … I-008-11). Rate-limit wiring is verified at **Tier 6** by Plan-021 against this plan's mount surface (CP-008-5), not here.

### I-008-3 — Substrate wraps, never re-implements, the directory service

Plan-008-bootstrap's `sessionRouter` and `session-router.ts` MUST wrap the existing `packages/control-plane/src/sessions/session-directory-service.ts` shipped by Plan-001 Phase 4. Re-implementing directory logic in the router would fork the lock-ordering invariant (per [Plan-001 §Invariants I-001-1](./001-shared-session-core.md#invariants)) and break the `Querier`-driven test pattern Plan-001 Phase 5 strengthens.

**Why load-bearing.** Plan-001 owns the directory service. Plan-008 owns the HTTP transport. Conflating the two violates the Plan-001 ownership row in [cross-plan-dependencies.md §2 `packages/control-plane/src/sessions/`](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#2-package-path-ownership-map).

**Verification (per F-008b-1-05 resolution).** Three concrete enforcement mechanisms — all three MUST land in the Phase 1 PR:

1. **Constructor injection.** `session-router.ts` exports a factory function that accepts a `SessionDirectoryService` instance: `createSessionRouter(directoryService: SessionDirectoryService): TRPCRouter`. The router's procedures call `directoryService.*` methods exclusively; the factory does NOT instantiate a `Querier` or `pg.Pool` directly. Same pattern for `session-subscribe-sse.ts`: `createSessionSubscribeSse(directoryService: SessionDirectoryService): SseHandler`.
2. **ESLint `no-restricted-imports` rule.** Forbid `pg`, `pg-pool`, and `@databases/pg` imports from `packages/control-plane/src/sessions/session-router.ts` and `packages/control-plane/src/sessions/session-subscribe-sse.ts`. Rule lives in the package-level ESLint config; CI enforces it.
3. **Unit-test introspection assertion.** Test asserts (via TypeScript symbol introspection or a small AST walker) that the two files' exported symbols call only `directoryService.*` methods at the top-level execution path. The integration-test layer reuses Plan-001 Phase 4's `Querier`-driven test pattern — the router inherits I-001-1 lock-ordering by routing through the directory service.

### Tier 5 production invariants (decomposition of I-008-2): I-008-4 … I-008-11

The following decompose the I-008-2 umbrella into the concrete, individually-testable production guarantees the Tier 5 remainder MUST hold. Each cites its Spec-008 / ADR-010 source and is proven by a named surface in the [§Test And Verification Plan](#test-and-verification-plan). Kept compact deliberately — the umbrella invariants (I-008-1/2/3) carry the verbose rationale; these are the enforceable leaves.

### I-008-4 — PASETO v4 validation on every Tier-5 endpoint

Every control-plane Tier-5 endpoint (`SessionJoin`, `PresenceRegister`, `RelayNegotiation`, `SessionResumeAfterReconnect`), **the three authenticated invite procedures** (`invite.create`, `invite.accept`, `invite.revoke` — reachable through the merged host once [Plan-002](./002-invite-membership-and-presence.md) T2.7 ships the invite router; added 2026-08-11 per the BL-133 amendment, the same gated-endpoint-set completion the runtimenode procedures then took a day later), **the five `runtimenode.*` procedures** (`runtimenode.attach`, `runtimenode.heartbeat`, `runtimenode.capabilityupdate`, `runtimenode.detach`, `runtimenode.roster` — added 2026-08-12 per the BL-141 amendment: the Plan-003 caller-authorization guard consumes the verified principal this middleware resolves, so the `callerParticipantId` [ADR-025](../decisions/025-runtime-node-control-plane-caller-authorization.md) D2 forwards into every runtime-node service call is contractually required here rather than incidentally supplied. This row binds R1's endpoint set, not present-day enforcement: until R1 lands, `resolveCurrentParticipantId` throws and the surface admits no untrusted traffic; the wiring that flips these routes live is owned by T-008r-1-5's host-wiring leg — the throwing resolver swapped for the middleware-derived verified principal, each `runtimenode.*` route exercised under a verified token — which is what lets ADR-025 D2 promise the Plan-003 guard itself goes production-live with zero further guard-code change), **and** the relay WSS handshake MUST validate a PASETO v4.public token before any dispatch ([Spec-008 §Relay Connection Lifecycle](../specs/008-control-plane-relay-and-session-join.md#relay-connection-lifecycle) `Sec-WebSocket-Protocol: paseto-v4, <base64url(token)>`; `Spec-008 §Interfaces And Contracts`). Validation consumes the Tier-1-shipped `@ai-sidekicks/crypto-paseto` `verify` primitive (PR #92) against the [Plan-018](./018-identity-and-participant-state.md)-published issuer key (CP-008-4). No endpoint serves on an absent / expired / invalid token. **`invite.preview` is deliberately excluded, and the exclusion is part of this invariant's record (2026-08-11):** preview is anonymous by design — the previewer may hold no registered identity yet, which is the point of a deep-link preview (`Spec-002 §Interfaces And Contracts`) — and its protection is the 256-bit `v4.local` token entropy plus the [Spec-021 §Canonical Endpoint Group Registry](../specs/021-rate-limiting-policy.md#canonical-endpoint-group-registry) anonymous rows (`invite.preview` per token-hash, `invite.redeem_ip` stacked per IP); a future reader must not "complete" the gated list over it — that would regress the deep-link flow into requiring authentication before the confirmation step it exists to render. Rate-limit middleware is a separate Tier-6 layer (CP-008-5), not part of this invariant.

### I-008-5 — Relay is zero-knowledge (E2EE; never plaintext)

The relay broker MUST forward only opaque per-recipient `{sender_ephemeral, recipient_id, seq, nonce, ciphertext+tag}` envelopes and MUST NOT decrypt, inspect, or persist plaintext payloads (`Spec-008 §Relay Encryption`, `Spec-008 §Relay Connection Lifecycle`, `Spec-008 §Trust Properties` — honest-but-curious relay threat model; the broker routes on `recipient_id` alone and forwards `sender_ephemeral`/`seq`/`nonce`/`ciphertext+tag` opaquely). Relay sharding and WebSocket Hibernation operate on ciphertext frames only.

### I-008-6 — Session-granularity forward secrecy via ephemeral-key zeroization

Each participant generates an ephemeral X25519 key pair **per session** (not per message); the secret key and all derived pairwise session keys MUST be zeroed in memory at session end (`Spec-008 §Relay Encryption`, `Spec-008 §Relay Connection Lifecycle`, `Spec-008 §Trust Properties`). Compromise of the long-term Ed25519 identity key MUST NOT reveal past session keys.

### I-008-7 — Control-plane `SessionKeyBundle` admission is identity-bound and single-use

Before distributing any `SessionKeyBundle` the control plane MUST: (a) verify the Ed25519 signature over `session_id ‖ ephemeral_x25519_public` (`Spec-008 §Relay Encryption`, `Spec-008 §Relay Negotiation`); (b) reject any bundle whose Ed25519 signing key is not bound to a registered participant identity (`Spec-008 §Trust Properties`); and (c) reject reused ephemeral X25519 public keys across distinct sessions (`Spec-008 §Relay Negotiation`). A compromised control plane cannot forge valid Ed25519 signatures.

### I-008-8 — In-session replay protection via AEAD-bound monotonic sequence

Per-sender monotonic sequence numbers MUST be bound into the AEAD associated data (alongside `recipient_id`) **and** carried in the envelope in the clear so the receiver can reconstruct the associated data; a tampered `seq` fails the AEAD tag. Recipients MUST reject out-of-order / replayed frames within a session (`Spec-008 §Relay Encryption`, `Spec-008 §Trust Properties`).

### I-008-9 — Reconnect re-associates, never duplicates, participant identity

Switching between direct and relay connectivity, or reconnecting within the grace window, MUST re-associate the same participant presence rather than mint a second session identity (`Spec-008 §Fallback Behavior`; **`Spec-008 §Acceptance Criteria` (AC2)**). `SessionResumeAfterReconnect` accepts the prior participant / client handle (`Spec-008 §Interfaces And Contracts`).

### I-008-10 — Control plane is never the execution authority

Control-plane join / relay / presence MUST NOT become the source of truth for run-execution state, and relay connectivity MUST NOT be treated as proof of execution authority (`Spec-008 §State And Data Implications`, `Spec-008 §Pitfalls To Avoid`; **`Spec-008 §Acceptance Criteria` (AC3)**).

### I-008-11 — Join is a membership action separable from relay connectivity

`SessionJoin` (membership) MUST succeed independently of relay (connectivity): a relay-setup failure leaves the join valid with degraded remote connectivity, never a failed join (`Spec-008 §Fallback Behavior`, `Spec-008 §Implementation Notes`; **`Spec-008 §Acceptance Criteria` (AC1)**). The V1 pairwise participant cap (≤ 10, `Spec-008 §Relay Encryption`) is the **same** cap Plan-001's join already enforces (`resource.limit_exceeded`, [Spec-001 §Resource Limits](../specs/001-shared-session-core.md#resource-limits)) — the relay inherits it; no separate relay-side cap is enforced.

### I-008-12 — Client event-stream resume is floor-checked, gap-safe, and at-most-once-applied

Campaign-B12 consumer-contract invariant (it enforces the consumer rule [Plan-006 T4.3](./006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#t43--replay-servicets-for-eventreadaftercursor--eventreadwindow) states producer-side; it is **not** part of the I-008-4 … I-008-11 decomposition of I-008-2). Any Plan-008 client surface that resumes a session event stream MUST: **(a)** resume from `acknowledged ?? earliest` (`SessionReadResponse.timelineCursors`, [api-payload-contracts.md §Tier 1: Plan-001 — Shared Session Core (Task 4.2)](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md#tier-1-plan-001--shared-session-core-task-42)); **(b)** treat the gap predicate `decodeEventCursor(acknowledged) < decodeEventCursor(earliest)` — and its two converging siblings, an unresolvable `acknowledged` cursor (the typed `event.cursor_unresolvable`) and a sequence discontinuity in the resumed stream (a live-stream gap, a non-contiguous drained page — the surface a retention pass leaves when it advances `earliest` between the floor check and the first read, closing the check-to-read race — or a drain exhausting below the snapshot `latest`, the total-purge witness shape; each detected by the consumer's own checks with the evidence in hand — the floor-initialized contiguity check for gaps, the drain-exhaustion witness comparison for the purge shape; an untyped iterator rejection from the subscription is terminal, never a reset trigger) — as events-lost: reset the projection — reinitializing the apply high-water-mark together with it, so the rebuilt projection re-applies the replayed prefix instead of discarding it against a stale mark — and resume from `earliest`, while a clean floor (`decode(acknowledged) === decode(earliest)`) MUST NOT trigger a reset (the T4.3 no-false-resync rule), and a settled reset also invalidates the caller's acknowledged floor — reset settlement means the caller has durably recorded the rebuild in its projection's commit domain (stripping `acknowledged` through the injected `readSession` seam until the rebuilt projection re-acks), so a cycle dying after a settled reset re-floors at `earliest`, never at a stale ack past the cleared prefix; **(c)** apply each sequence at most once **within a projection epoch** — the interval from cycle start, or from a settled `onProjectionReset` (which clears the projection and begins a new epoch the re-drained prefix re-applies into — the reset callback is the epoch boundary a caller with non-resettable external side effects must anchor to), to the next reset — a high-water-mark of the last applied `sequence` dedupes the drain/live overlap and in-stream reconnect redelivery (SSE `Last-Event-ID` resumption floors replay at the last-delivered cursor, so redelivery of already-applied events is the expected steady state), and the mark advances only after the apply callback settles successfully — an in-flight or rejected apply never advances it, so the mark never claims unapplied work; **across cycles** the floor is the caller's persisted `acknowledged` cursor, so events applied but not yet acknowledged when a cycle dies re-deliver on the next invocation — the deliberate at-least-once residual, bounded by ack cadence: a caller that advances `acknowledged` only after its durable apply gets effective exactly-once, and the decorator persists no second position of its own (the ack floor stays the single durable authority — a decorator-durable watermark would mint a competing source of truth); and **(d)** when `earliest` is absent (a pre-widening responder, version skew), neither trust `acknowledged` unchecked nor destroy local state: a responder that predates the T4.3 `earliest` widening necessarily predates Plan-006 Phase 4's entire event-replay surface (T4.3, T4.5's subscription, and T4.7's `event.*` entry points land together), so the conservative cursorless full replay T4.3 sketches is unreachable through the very surface it names — the consumer MUST refuse the resume cycle before any projection reset or subsequent replay/subscribe wire call (a typed SDK-local unsupported-responder rejection issued on the cycle's own `session.read` snapshot — the sole wire call the refused cycle makes; the projection and `acknowledged` floor stay intact), surfacing the incompatibility as degraded timeline state and re-probing on a later invocation (the responder may have upgraded).

**Why load-bearing.** A resume that skips the floor check silently loses events whenever the acknowledged position has fallen below the surviving floor — every downstream projection (timeline rendering, recovery replay, rollback anchors) is corrupted with no diagnostic. A resume without the high-water-mark double-applies under at-least-once delivery, corrupting projections in the opposite direction. Plan-006 T4.3 states this contract from the producer side; I-008-12 is the consumer-side enforcement Plan-008's client surfaces carry (CP-008-10), and it is what keeps the `Spec-008 §Default Behavior` "live replay cursors as part of successful join" promise honest end-to-end.

**Verification.** Tests T-008r-4-T21..T29 (T-008r-4-13 — nine named tests per the [campaign design §4 W2 B12 row](../superpowers/specs/2026-07-01-capability-enhancements-design.md)), plus the composition-point integration arm T-008r-4-T13 (T-008r-4-10 — the three-leg reconnect drives `resumeAndSubscribe` in production).

### I-008-13 — Backfilled history is origin-verified, entitlement-scoped, and never silently empty

Peer history backfill is the only V1 path by which a late joiner acquires session history (`Spec-008 §Peer History Backfill On Join (V1)`; the control plane holds no session events per [ADR-017](../decisions/017-shared-event-sourcing-scope.md) and pre-join relay ciphertext is undecryptable to a new member by design). Every event a Plan-008 surface re-publishes or accepts through that path MUST satisfy all three legs:

**(a) Origin-verified before append.** The joiner verifies each backfilled event's `daemon_signature` against the **originating** daemon's Ed25519 public key — resolved by origin `NodeId` from the session participant roster ([Security Architecture §Per-Event Daemon Signature](../architecture/security-architecture.md#per-event-daemon-signature)) — over the **origin's own** canonical bytes ([Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#canonical-serialization-rules)), **before** appending. A verification failure REFUSES the row (never appends it) and surfaces `audit_integrity_failed` ([Spec-006 §Integrity Protocol](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#integrity-protocol)). A re-publishing peer never re-signs and never re-canonicalizes what it forwards — re-publication is transport, never authorship. That makes serving **possession-scoped**: `sequence` is a signed canonical member and a receiver appends under its own, so a received copy is re-signed and unservable, and a source therefore serves only the rows for which it holds **origin-signed** bytes — its own authored rows, plus a predecessor `NodeId`'s rows where its member's store was carried forward, served as transport under the origin's roster key. A copy offered from received relay traffic is necessarily re-signed bytes and is refused on this same leg, not treated as a special case. Compacted-region rows arrive as their audit stubs and verify on the anchor-plus-`stub_signature` path ([Spec-006 §Post-Compaction Integrity](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#post-compaction-integrity)), never as reconstructed full rows. The append itself follows ADR-017's unchanged receive semantics (the joiner's own `sequence`, chain, and signature), so the retained row attests receipt, not origin authorship — the named residual `Spec-008 §Peer History Backfill On Join (V1)` records, not a gap this invariant closes.

**(b) Entitlement-scoped.** A source serves only rows of the session in which the requester holds active membership. Four exclusions are closed and MUST hold on the **source** side (a joiner-side filter is not sufficient — the leak happens on the wire): sentinel-bound daemon-scope / participant-scope rows are never served ([Spec-006 §Daemon-Scope Event Binding And Node-Scope Anchoring](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#daemon-scope-event-binding-and-node-scope-anchoring) — they have no owning session and belong to the source daemon's own posture and account-level history); the `pii_payload` column is never carried in any chunk (it is outside the canonical form, so omitting it costs no verifiability, while shipping it would fan a partition sealed under another participant's content key — [Spec-022 §Shred Fan-Out](../specs/022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md#shred-fan-out) — to a member who holds neither that key nor any part of its erasure path; the signed `pii_ciphertext_digest` / `pii_participant_id` members stay in the payload so the joiner's row stays honest about what it lacks); no other session's rows are served; and **no row of a membership-restricted channel is served to a requester outside that channel's member set** (`Spec-008 §Per-Channel Recipient Scoping (V1)`, the serving half of I-008-14 — V1's one restricting kind is [Spec-016](../specs/016-multi-agent-channels-and-orchestration.md)'s `direct` two-human channel, whose immutable pair reduces the rule to "a `direct` channel's events are served only to its pair"). Within the joined session the entitlement is role-independent across `viewer` / `collaborator` / `runtime contributor` ([Spec-002 §Required Behavior](../specs/002-invite-membership-and-presence.md#required-behavior)) — the fourth exclusion narrows by **channel membership**, never by role, and admits no `owner` override. A source whose entitled set for a requester is **empty** has still answered: it is never outstanding, its own origin is credited as claimed, and no withholding is ever surfaced as withholding, so the entitlement narrowings stay invisible to coverage rather than reported as gaps (surfacing them would disclose the withheld channel's traffic pattern to exactly the participant the scoping excludes) — with the one cause-opaque asymmetry `Spec-008 §Peer History Backfill On Join (V1)` names: an entirely-withheld predecessor origin stays unclaimed, indistinguishable to the joiner from an uncarried store, because crediting an unevidenced foreign claim would let one member silently retire another's history.

**(c) Honest about coverage.** Coverage is tracked over **origins and sources**, not spans — a joiner cannot enumerate events it has never seen — on the two axes `Spec-008 §Peer History Backfill On Join (V1)` defines: every **other** current member of the session is either answered or outstanding (the joiner is its own source by construction — own holdings local, own origin credited from the local log, no pairwise self endpoint to request over; it is never held outstanding against itself — 2026-08-11 round-2 fold) — the membership record retains `suspended` / `revoked` rows, but a departed member is never a requestable source (its history rides the origin axis), and **answered** means the response's terminal marker arrived **verifying** — its responder signature checked under the claimed `NodeId`'s roster key, the proof the pairwise channel's `ParticipantId`-grained authentication cannot supply — and every declared chunk ordinal is held with distinct verified entry `id`s reconciling to the signed `totalEntries` (round-3 fold), never merely that some chunk arrived — every origin `NodeId` in the register-once signing-key roster is either claimed by an answered response (a self-claim on an answer whose terminal marker proves the claimed `NodeId` by that responder signature; a foreign claim only with at least one row verifying under that origin's key — both claim classes evidence-backed, key possession and row evidence respectively) or unclaimed, and any outstanding source or unclaimed origin surfaces the explicit `pending_backfill` state naming it (`Spec-008 §Fallback Behavior`), never an empty or complete-looking timeline. The arms differ: an outstanding current member is transient and clears on its return; an unclaimed origin is transient while any member is outstanding and terminal in V1 once every other current member has answered without claiming it — the departed member being the canonical instance — and stays named. The typed `session.history_backfill_unavailable` code names the response-level refusal (transient arm) and is never the coverage state itself. Repeated deliveries — retry, restart re-request, presence-transition re-request — converge idempotently on the canonical `id`, with the request-scoped correlation identifier serving completion accounting only (chunk ordinals plus the terminal entry-count marker; `Spec-008 §Interfaces And Contracts`), never convergence: a source serves one representation per `id` per response (full row if held, else the audit stub), first delivery wins, and no appended row is ever rewritten — a stub never replaces an appended full row, and a full row never supersedes an appended stub, because supersession would rewrite a committed row mid-chain; a stub pinned by the compaction race is the accurate record of a source that never reconstructs what it no longer holds.

**Why load-bearing.** Each leg fails silently if unenforced. Without (a), a peer re-publishing an edited version of a third party's history launders tampered rows into the joiner's log **under the joiner's own signature** — the joiner's chain would then attest content no daemon ever authored, and every later reader of that log would verify it green. Without (b), the same mechanism that hands a joiner the session's history also hands them the source daemon's sessionless posture chain, a PII partition sealed to someone else, and the contents of every two-human `direct` channel in the session — converting a session-membership grant into a cross-boundary read, and silently re-opening on the serving path exactly the confidentiality the live path spends a narrowed fan-out to hold (the fourth exclusion is the leg that keeps re-publication from becoming the leak). Because the exclusions must hold source-side, a joiner-side filter would leave the bytes already on the wire. Without (c), the honest failure (peers offline) is indistinguishable from the honest success (nothing has happened in this session yet), and a joiner acts on a timeline it believes is complete — the exact silent-emptiness the `Spec-014 §Cross-Node Artifact Relay (V1)` `artifact.no_access_key` posture exists to prevent for artifacts.

**Verification.** Origin-verification and refusal arms MUST drive the production verifier rather than re-implement its predicate in the test, and one arm feeds a **validly re-signed** copy of another daemon's event (correct bytes, a legitimate non-origin key) to assert it is refused for resolving under the wrong `NodeId` — the arm a signature-only fixture passes vacuously; entitlement arms feed a sentinel-bound row, another session's row, and a row bound to a `direct` channel the requester is **not** a member of through the **source-side** chunk selector and assert all three absent from the emitted chunk, with the same selector call emitting a `pii_payload`-bearing row — its entry bytes carrying the signed digest and owner-claim members with no ciphertext anywhere in the chunk, because the `pii_payload` exclusion strips a **column** that was never inside the canonical bytes, never the row (dropping the row would be the complete-looking partial timeline this invariant forbids) — and emitting a `direct`-channel row for a requester who **is** in the pair (firing negative controls plus positive controls, not a clean-by-construction fixture — a selector that emitted nothing would pass the exclusion arms vacuously), asserted **total over both retention classes**: a compacted `direct`-channel stub, read through the signed stub projection's `channelId`, withholds and serves by pair membership exactly as live rows do, and a compacted received row is never served; a stub arm serves a compacted row's entry carrying its origin's covering anchor record and asserts the joiner verifies the anchor's **claim signature** under the origin roster key — coordinates and root together per `Spec-006 §Anchoring Cadence`'s 2026-08-11 amendment, with the relabeled-anchor negative control: a carried record whose span coordinates are rewritten to cover the stub fails the claim signature and the entry is refused (under a root-only preimage that control would pass, the round-2 finding) — the stubbed row's span coverage, and the `stub_signature` before append, with an anchor-less stub entry, and one under a failing anchor, refused and never appended; a provenance-write arm asserts the joiner's append path writes neither PII column on any backfill-appended row (the write-side half of the Spec-006 amendment's require-absent arm); convergence arms assert idempotent re-delivery by `id` and that no appended row is rewritten in either direction (a stub offered after an appended full row is discarded; a late pre-compaction full row offered after an appended stub is discarded — the compaction-race order); a possession arm serves a predecessor-`NodeId` row from a carried-forward store and asserts it verifies under the **origin's** roster key while an empty foreign claim is **not** credited (the origin stays unclaimed); the degraded arms assert `pending_backfill` naming every unanswered source on an all-peers-offline join **and** an unclaimed origin still named after every current peer has answered (the terminal arm, which a transient-only fixture never reaches); completion arms drop a response's terminal chunk — and separately one declared ordinal — and assert the source stays outstanding until both arrive, with an entitled-empty source answering as a single zero-entry terminal chunk and credited answered, and a departed (`revoked`) member never enumerated as a requestable source while its origin stays coverage-tracked; a terminal-authentication arm (2026-08-11 round-2 fold) asserts a terminal marker signed under the responder's roster key is credited — self-claim included — while an unsigned marker, one under a wrong key, and one whose signed member set was altered after signing (a stripped or padded `servesForOrigins`) each leave the source outstanding and the self-claim uncredited, and a self-exclusion arm asserts no self-request is ever minted while the joiner's own origin is credited claimed from its local log; a bridge arm drives the engines end-to-end through the production CP-008-14 `session.*` methods — serve streaming chunks whose terminal marker is signed with the daemon-held key, deliver appending through the joiner engine, and a live relayed event appending through `session.relayEventDeliver` into the same engine — with a maximal-canonical-size entry (at the `Spec-006` `EVENT_CANONICAL_BYTES_MAX` bound) asserted to encode into a single legal frame, **plus the round-3 arms**: an origin-compacted stub delivered through the bridge appends as the received-stub row (origin projection bytes verbatim, origin `stub_signature` retained, marker stamped, local `sequence`) and re-verifies at rest on `Spec-006 §Post-Compaction Integrity`'s received-as-stub dispatch, a cross-class flip (marker cleared, or planted on a locally-compacted stub) failing fail-closed, and a maximal bound-sized stub entry likewise encodes into one legal frame. Named tests (T-008r-4-14, authored 2026-08-11 by the restoring readiness-audit delta): `T-008r-4-T33` / `T-008r-4-T34` / `T-008r-4-T35` (origin-verification, tampered-refusal, and the validly-re-signed wrong-`NodeId` refusal), `T-008r-4-T36` (the source-side entitlement exclusions with the pii-served and `direct`-pair positive controls), `T-008r-4-T37` (idempotent convergence, no rewrite in either direction), `T-008r-4-T38` (the possession arm and the uncredited empty foreign claim), `T-008r-4-T39` (the transient and terminal degraded arms), and `T-008r-4-T40` (completion accounting) — with `T-008r-4-T41` / `T-008r-4-T42` covering the serving selector's possession bound and the provenance-marker mechanics the same task ships, `T-008r-4-T44` the response-level refusal arm (a `session.history_backfill_unavailable` refusal changes no coverage state — the refused source stays outstanding — and a later successful retry converges the same rows idempotently), `T-008r-4-T45` the stub anchor-carriage arm with the relabeled-anchor negative control, `T-008r-4-T46` the joiner-side PII-column require-absent arm, `T-008r-4-T47` the stub-class entitlement and possession arms, and — the round-2 fold's pair — `T-008r-4-T48` the terminal-authentication and self-exclusion arms, `T-008r-4-T49` the bridge integration and frame-fit arms.

### I-008-14 — Channel-scoped relay traffic is member-scoped on the live path and on the serving path

A session event scoped to a **membership-restricted** channel reaches that channel's members and no one else (`Spec-008 §Per-Channel Recipient Scoping (V1)`). V1 has exactly one restricting kind: [Spec-016](../specs/016-multi-agent-channels-and-orchestration.md)'s `direct` two-human channel, whose member pair is fixed at creation and immutable thereafter. Every Plan-008 surface that addresses or serves session events MUST satisfy all three legs:

**(a) Live addressing is an intersection, never the full recipient set.** For an event whose `payload` carries a `channelId` binding resolving to a membership-restricted channel, the sending surface derives its envelope recipient set as the **channel's member set intersected with the session's current recipient set** and seals one envelope per member of that intersection (T-008r-4-10, over the T-008r-4-6 per-recipient encrypt). The predicate is **structural** — a payload-carried channel binding whose resolved record restricts membership — and MUST NOT be an enumerated event-type list: the canonical envelope's top-level member set is closed ([Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#canonical-serialization-rules)), so the binding necessarily rides the payload, and a hand-maintained type list would fail **open** on the first taxonomy addition it missed. The predicate is **total**: the bootstrap `main` channel has no channel row and resolves unrestricted by construction ([Spec-016 §Interfaces And Contracts](../specs/016-multi-agent-channels-and-orchestration.md#interfaces-and-contracts)), so it is never an unresolvable binding. A binding that genuinely cannot be resolved **refuses the publication** — never falls back to the unscoped session recipient set. The intersection composes with the membership epoch as a further narrowing, and because a `direct` pair is immutable, no epoch transition can widen that channel's recipients.

**(b) Serving applies the same predicate at selection time.** The backfill half is the fourth exclusion of I-008-13(b) — stated once there and bound here to the same structural predicate and the same fail-closed disposition (an unresolvable binding withholds the row). It is a distinct enforcement point rather than a restatement: a `HistoryBackfillChunk` is addressed to the requester alone, so there is no second recipient set to narrow and the only lever is what the source **selects**.

**(c) The guarantee is over daemons, not agents — and is stated as such.** This invariant bounds which participants' **daemons** ever hold the channel's ciphertext. It does **not** exclude agents: a member's own daemon holds the plaintext by design, and keeping that content out of any agent's context is [Spec-016](../specs/016-multi-agent-channels-and-orchestration.md)'s daemon obligation at agent-context assembly, discharged through [Plan-016 §Cross-Plan Obligations](./016-multi-agent-channels-and-orchestration.md#cross-plan-obligations) CP-016-14 and **not owned here**. Any Plan-008 surface, comment, or error message that describes recipient scoping as agent exclusion is a defect against this leg.

**Why load-bearing.** (a) fails open in the direction that cannot be undone: a fan-out that wrongly includes a non-member hands that daemon ciphertext it can decrypt — a disclosure no later membership change, key rotation, or retraction recalls, and one that leaves no trace at the sender. The two disposition rules follow from that asymmetry: a wrong narrowing is a delivery failure the sender observes and retries, so refusal beats widening on an unresolvable binding, and a structural predicate beats an enumerated list because the list's failure mode is silent inclusion. Without (b), the confidentiality (a) buys is spent the first time a joiner asks for history and a pair member re-publishes its `direct` channel to them. Without (c) stated explicitly, a reader reasonably infers that cryptography is doing the agent-exclusion work, and the audience filter that actually does it looks redundant — the precise misreading that gets a defense-in-depth layer deleted as dead code.

**Verification.** The live arm drives the **production** recipient-set derivation (never a re-implemented predicate in the test) and asserts that a three-plus-participant session publishing a `direct`-channel event seals envelopes to the pair only — with a **firing negative control**: the same fixture publishing an unrestricted-channel event seals to every session recipient, so a derivation stuck at "narrow everything" or "narrow nothing" fails one arm or the other. A further arm asserts the fail-closed disposition (an unresolvable channel binding refuses publication and emits no envelope at all, rather than falling back to the session set). A third arm covers the seam itself: constructing the surface with **no** resolver bound is refused, which is what keeps CP-008-12's build-incomplete rule detectable — every other arm binds one, so an admit-all default would pass them all. The serving arm rides I-008-13's entitlement arms above. Named tests: T-008r-4-T30 / T-008r-4-T31 / T-008r-4-T32 (live path, T-008r-4-10); the serving-path tests are `T-008r-4-T36` (the source-side channel exclusion with its `direct`-pair positive control), `T-008r-4-T41` (the possession-bound serving selector), and `T-008r-4-T47` (the stub-class arms — the projection-read channel predicate and the stub-class possession bound), authored on T-008r-4-14 (2026-08-11).

Re-opened 2026-08-03: the V1 product-vision reconciliation amendment mints **I-008-13** + **I-008-14** and **CP-008-11** + **CP-008-12**, grows T-008r-4-10 with the live-path scoped fan-out over a forward-declared `ChannelRecipientScopeResolver` seam (CP-008-12; the CP-008-8 injected-seam shape, deliberately **not** an in-DAG dependency on Tier-6 [Plan-016](./016-multi-agent-channels-and-orchestration.md)), re-derives the Tier-5 test census 97 → 100, and schedules the R4 backfill task carrying both the origin-verification legs and leg 2's serving-side channel exclusion — audit-relevant scope growth the NS-17 audit never saw. **Owed scope widened 2026-08-11 (BL-133 amendment, PR #322):** the Plan-002 restoring readiness-audit delta lands two further growths on this already-open flip — the I-008-4 gated-endpoint-set completion (the three authenticated invite procedures join the invariant, with `invite.preview`'s anonymous exclusion recorded in the invariant text) and the Tier-5 invite-acceptance-handoff scope widening (daemon invite IPC handlers + live control-plane caller + `membershipClient.preview`, consuming Plan-002 CP-002-9) — both audited by the same targeted readiness-audit delta named in the paired-spec box above, which authors the R4 backfill task, re-checks this box, and restores `approved`. **Audited and re-checked 2026-08-11** — that delta (PR #323, §6 node NS-58) judged the full open-flip scope: the 2026-08-03 growth (I-008-13 / I-008-14, CP-008-11 / CP-008-12, the T-008r-4-10 live-path fan-out) plus the two PR #322-landed widenings taken as in-scope routed growth (the I-008-4 gated-endpoint-set completion and the Tier-5 invite-acceptance-handoff scope), authored T-008r-4-14 with CP-008-13 / CP-008-14 and its seventeen named tests, and re-derived the Tier-5 census 100 → 117 by direct count. That leg was routed to this plan by the ADR's §Mandated Work and was **not** carried by the 2026-08-11 delta above; it lands instead in the Plan-003/Spec-003 restoring targeted readiness-audit delta, which audits it in the same commit — so this box is re-opened and re-checked within one commit, the marker never rests un-checked in a committed state, and Plan-008 is flip-and-restored `approved` in the same swap.
