No re-audit of the Tier-1 or Tier-5 body is implied.

Target paths below assume the canonical implementation topology defined in [Container Architecture](../architecture/container-architecture.md).

## Cross-Plan Obligations

The Tier 1 bootstrap-deliverable Plan-008-bootstrap ships at Phase 1 carries reciprocal obligations to downstream consumers. This section makes those obligations visible to a Plan-008 reviewer without requiring them to read every consuming plan first. Mirrors the bidirectional-citation pattern established by [Plan-001 §Cross-Plan Obligations](./001-shared-session-core.md#cross-plan-obligations) (CP-001-1 / CP-001-2) and [Plan-007 §Cross-Plan Obligations](./007-local-ipc-and-daemon-control.md#cross-plan-obligations) (CP-007-1..5).

### CP-008-1 — `sessionRouter` HTTP transport + SSE substrate owed to [Plan-001](./001-shared-session-core.md) Phase 5

Plan-008-bootstrap Phase 1 ships the tRPC v11 `sessionRouter` HTTP handlers + SSE substrate that Plan-001 Phase 5's control-plane-side `sessionClient` consumes. The contract surface includes (a) the per-procedure tRPC shapes (`SessionCreate` mutation / `SessionRead` query / `SessionJoin` mutation + Zod schema imports — F-008b-1-03 closed 2026-04-30 per [api-payload-contracts.md §Tier 1 (cont.): Plan-008](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md#tier-1-cont-plan-008--plan-008-bootstrap-control-plane-trpc--sse-substrate)), (b) the SSE wire frame primitive (Content-Type, `data:` encoding, `id:`/`retry:`/`Last-Event-ID` semantics — F-008b-1-01 closed 2026-04-30 per [api-payload-contracts.md §SSE Wire Frame (Tier 1 Ratified)](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md#sse-wire-frame-tier-1-ratified)), and (c) the production-deployment + feature-flag gate (per I-008-1) so Plan-001 Phase 5 integration tests against a `wrangler dev` bootstrap (workerd local environment with `env.ENVIRONMENT === 'development'`).

**Why bidirectional.** Plan-001 Phase 5 (line ~261-274) names "the Plan-008 bootstrap-deliverable substrate" as its precondition; without CP-008-1 on the Plan-008 side, the obligation is one-directional.

### CP-008-2 — `runtimeNodeAttach` registration surface owed to [Plan-003](./003-runtime-node-attach.md)

Plan-003 (line ~79) cites the Plan-008 bootstrap's tRPC v11 host as the registration surface for runtime-node-attach procedures. Tier 1 ships only the host + sessionRouter; the runtime-node-attach surface registers against the same host at its own tier. Plan-008-bootstrap MUST expose a stable router-mounting surface so Plan-003 can attach without forking the host topology.

**Why bidirectional.** Plan-003 derivatively cites Plan-008's host runtime declaration. Per BL-104 / C-4 resolution (2026-04-30), the runtime is Cloudflare Workers via `@trpc/server/adapters/fetch`; Plan-003's CP-003-2 reciprocal text was updated in lockstep with this amendment so both plans cite the same runtime authoritatively.

### CP-008-3 — `EventEnvelope` schema reciprocity with [Plan-006](./006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md) (Tier 4)

Plan-008-bootstrap's SSE substrate consumes `EventEnvelope` from `packages/contracts/src/event.ts`. Per the Plan-001 F-001-2-06 forward-declaration pattern, Plan-001 Phase 2 ships a placeholder shape that Plan-006 widens at Tier 4 with the canonical event-taxonomy registry. CP-008-3 declares Plan-008-bootstrap's obligation to rebind onto the Plan-006 EventEnvelope shape when Tier 4 lands — the SSE wire frame body changes without changing the wire framing itself.

**Why bidirectional.** Plan-006 reviewers must see that Plan-008-bootstrap is a forward-declared consumer; Plan-008 reviewers must see that the placeholder shape is provisional and the Plan-006 widening is required at Tier 4.

### CP-008-4 — PASETO issuer + authenticated-identity context owed by [Plan-018](./018-identity-and-participant-state.md) (co-tier Tier 5)

Plan-008-remainder **consumes** two surfaces from Plan-018 (the co-tier Tier-5 identity plan): (a) the **PASETO v4.public issuer key** that I-008-4 validates every endpoint / relay-handshake token against (the verify primitive itself is the Tier-1-shipped `@ai-sidekicks/crypto-paseto`, PR #92; Plan-018 publishes the issuer key per [cross-plan-dependencies.md §3 Plan-018 row](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#3-inter-plan-dependency-graph)); and (b) the **authenticated-identity context** that resolves `ControlPlaneSessionJoinRequest.identityHandle` → a registered participant identity — the same `AuthenticatedIdentityContext` named in [Plan-018 CP-018-3 / D-018-1](./018-identity-and-participant-state.md) (first-sighting auth-context producer). Plan-018's **ratified** resolution (c) (D-018-1) makes Plan-018 own the context shape and producer plans populate it; Plan-008-remainder's join path is one such producer. This is a **non-secret** tuple — no per-participant secret crosses the seam (the former "shared `ikm`" framing is dissolved; see Plan-018 D-018-1 / Plan-022 D-022-2).

A **third consumed surface — (c) relay connection-token issuance (R3 OD-3-3, ratified OD-008r-3)** — is owed by Plan-018. `RelayNegotiationResponse.connectionToken` is a short-lived PASETO v4.public (TTL 300s, `Spec-008 §Relay Negotiation`); minting a `v4.public` is an Ed25519 **sign** under the issuer key, which MUST NOT execute inside the zero-knowledge relay broker (R3 is verify-only — holding Plan-018's signing key in the broker would breach the trust boundary I-008-5). Plan-018 therefore additionally owes a **connection-token issuance surface** — a constructor-injected `RelayConnectionTokenIssuer` whose signing key + custody stay entirely with Plan-018 (the same custody-stays-with-producer seam as the **CP-008-8** injected Ed25519 signer). R3's `RelayNegotiation` endpoint (T-008r-3-6) **consumes** this issuer and authors no sign; the exact issuer-surface shape is Plan-018-owned (ratified OD-008r-3; the reciprocal `provides` note lands on Plan-018 at swap).

A **fourth consumed surface — (d) the participant identity-key roster backing `RegisteredIdentityResolver` (registered 2026-08-15, Plan-018 NS-62 promotion pass)** — widens this entry's owed set from three to four. T-008r-3-1's bundle-admission resolver maps `ed25519_identity_public` → `ParticipantId` (I-008-7b); that resolution is a key-roster read over Plan-018's `participant_identity_keys` store ([Plan-018 CP-018-13](./018-identity-and-participant-state.md#cross-plan-obligations) consumer (c) — Plan-018 T5.1/T5.3), a surface this plan named with no store behind it until that pass authored one. Same co-tier ordering as (a)–(c): Plan-018's store + roster land before the R3 broker consumes them (F-008r-C).

**Intra-Tier-5 ordering edge (F-008r-C).** All **four** surfaces require Plan-018 to land **before** the Plan-008-remainder surfaces that consume them — the R1 auth-middleware (issuer key + identity context, T-008r-1-5; the R1 widening recorded at the W2.5 promotion) and R2/R3 — within Tier 5, but [cross-plan-dependencies.md §5](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#5-canonical-build-order) named only "once Plan-002 (invite/presence) is complete" — it did **not** establish the Plan-018 → Plan-008-remainder intra-tier edge. **Resolution (executed at swap):** §5 (Tier 5 row + §3 graph) is amended to record Plan-018-before-Plan-008-remainder, with Plan-018's reciprocal (issuer-key publication + `AuthenticatedIdentityContext` shape + `RelayConnectionTokenIssuer` surface) confirmed on its side. R1/R2/R3 tasks consuming the PASETO issuer / identity context / token-issuer carry an explicit `Consumes:` clause naming Plan-018 (intra-tier precondition), not an in-DAG dependency — and the R1 phase Precondition carries the enforceable merge gate.

### CP-008-5 — Rate-limit mount surface owed to [Plan-021](./021-rate-limiting-policy.md) (Tier 6, downstream)

Plan-008-remainder owns `packages/control-plane/src/server/` and MUST expose a stable middleware-mount surface so that Plan-021 (Tier 6) can mount its `rateLimitProcedure` middleware **onto** Plan-008's host without forking the host topology (per [cross-plan-dependencies.md §2 `packages/control-plane/src/server/` row](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#2-package-path-ownership-map): "Plan-021 (Tier 6 — rate-limiter middleware mount)"). Rate-limiting is **not** wired by Plan-008-remainder (the I-008-2 mis-tiering correction) — it is a Tier-6 forward obligation, exactly mirroring [Plan-002 CP-002-3](./002-invite-membership-and-presence.md#cross-plan-obligations) / [BL-120](../backlog.md).

**Why bidirectional.** Plan-021 reviewers see that the rate-limiter mounts onto Plan-008's host (Plan-021 already declares Plan-008 a dependency — [cross-plan-dependencies.md §3 Plan-021 row](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#3-inter-plan-dependency-graph)); Plan-008-remainder reviewers must know NOT to author a rate-limit middleware task and to keep the mount surface stable.

### CP-008-6 — `packages/control-plane/src/presence/` directory EXTEND owed to [Plan-002](./002-invite-membership-and-presence.md)

Plan-002 Phase 3 (Tier 2) **CREATED** `packages/control-plane/src/presence/presence-register-service.ts` (shipped NS-26 / PR #108). Plan-008-remainder **EXTENDS the `presence/` directory** with a sibling hosted-relay-fan-out file — it does **not** create or own `presence-register-service.ts` — per [Plan-002 CP-002-1](./002-invite-membership-and-presence.md#cross-plan-obligations) ("`presence/` … EXTENDED by Plan-008-remainder (Tier 5) for hosted-relay fan-out … downstream extenders add sibling files under the same directory"). **F-008r-D:** [cross-plan-dependencies.md §2 `packages/control-plane/src/presence/` row](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#2-package-path-ownership-map) planning-time-attributes `presence-register-service.ts` to Plan-008 (overtaken by the NS-26 ship — same staleness Plan-018 CP-018-4 already records); the §2 row owes a fix to read Plan-002-owns-file / Plan-008-extends-directory.

### CP-008-7 — Invite-acceptance handoff consumes [Plan-002](./002-invite-membership-and-presence.md) invite resolution (Tier 2, shipped)

Plan-008-remainder's `SessionJoin` invite-based path (`ControlPlaneSessionJoinRequest.inviteToken`) consumes Plan-002's invite-acceptance surface (`invite-service.ts` + `session_invites` resolution, Plan-002 Phase 2, Tier 2 shipped). The handoff resolves an `inviteToken` to a membership grant; the membership write itself follows the Plan-001 lock-ordering invariant I-001-1 (`sessions` → `session_memberships`) inherited via the directory service. Provider Plan-002 is a lower tier (Tier 2) → resolves as a Phase Precondition (path c).

### CP-008-8 — Participant Ed25519 identity-signer injection (custody owned by [ADR-021](../decisions/021-cli-identity-key-storage-custody.md) / [ADR-010](../decisions/010-paseto-webauthn-mls-auth.md) + [Plan-023](./023-desktop-shell-and-renderer.md))

R4's participant relay crypto (Phase R4-A) consumes an **injected** long-term Ed25519 identity signer — used only to sign each session's ephemeral X25519 public key (`session_id ‖ ephemeral_x25519_public`, `Spec-008 §Relay Encryption`, `Spec-008 §Relay Negotiation`). R4 owns the **signing operation**, never key custody. Custody is **another plan's territory**: CLI at-rest storage per [ADR-021](../decisions/021-cli-identity-key-storage-custody.md); desktop WebAuthn-PRF derivation per [ADR-010](../decisions/010-paseto-webauthn-mls-auth.md) ([Plan-023](./023-desktop-shell-and-renderer.md), Tier 8). R4 takes the signer as a constructor-injected dependency (the same `KeyRing`-style seam Plan-002 `invite-service` uses), so the participant crypto stays buildable + unit-testable with a fixture signer ahead of the custody producers. **The concrete signer-provider surface is not invented here** — it is forward-declared, deferred to ADR-021 / Plan-023 (ratified OD-008r-4). One-sided consume today → the reciprocal `provides` note on the custody producer is a fix-in-place obligation at swap (anti-rigid-ownership norm).

### CP-008-9 — Per-frame admission seam owed to [Plan-021](./021-rate-limiting-policy.md) (Tier 6, downstream; minted at the Tier-6 audit, D-021-2)

R3's zero-knowledge DO relay broker MUST expose a **per-decoded-frame admission seam** — an injectable hook invoked once per decoded inbound frame, **before** fan-out: `onFrame(connection, frame) → { proceed: true } | { proceed: false, sendFrame: <in-band error frame> } | { proceed: false, close: { code, retryAfter } }`. The hook receives **ciphertext-envelope metadata + the authenticated connection principal only** — never plaintext payload — preserving I-008-5 (the broker stays zero-knowledge; rate-limit accounting needs frame counts and principals, not content). The hook returns the outcome; the **broker** performs the resulting send/close (single-signal contract — the hook never writes to the connection). Default when no hook is injected: admit-all (R3 ships with the seam unbound; Plan-021's Phase 3 binds `wsRateLimit` into it).

**Why bidirectional.** Plan-021's WS enforcement (`ws.message`, 60/min/participant per [Spec-021 §Canonical Endpoint Group Registry](../specs/021-rate-limiting-policy.md#canonical-endpoint-group-registry)) cannot exist without a hosted per-frame interception point, and Spec-021's drop-frame overflow semantics (in-band `rate_limited` frame on counter trip; close `4029` only on active block, `4003` on ban — Plan-021 D-021-9, [api-payload-contracts.md §Relay Rate-Limit Signalling](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md#relay-rate-limit-signalling)) constrain the seam's outcome shape. R3 reviewers must know the seam is load-bearing for Tier 6 and keep it stable; Plan-021 consumes it as [CP-021-5](./021-rate-limiting-policy.md#cross-plan-obligations). The self-host relay path is independent (Plan-025 wires `wsRateLimit` into its own frame handler — no seam consumption).

### CP-008-10 — `EventClient` replay-cursor consumer surface consumed from [Plan-006](./006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md) (Tier 4; campaign B12)

T-008r-4-13's `ResumingEventClient` decorator consumes three Plan-006-owned surfaces: **(a)** the `EventClient` interface + `createDaemonEventClient` factory ([Plan-006 T4.7](./006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#t47--client-sdk-eventclientts-typed-entry-points)); **(b)** the T4.3 cursor codec (`encodeEventCursor` / `decodeEventCursor` beside `EventCursorSchema` in `packages/contracts/src/session.ts`) + the `timelineCursors.earliest` widening + the typed `event.cursor_unresolvable` error ([Plan-006 T4.3](./006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#t43--replay-servicets-for-eventreadaftercursor--eventreadwindow)); and **(c)** the subscribe replay-then-live monotonic-handoff guarantee ([Plan-006 T4.5](./006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#t45--eventsubscription-ssejson-rpc-notification-stream), I-006-4-05). Consumption is **compose-only**: per the [cross-plan-dependencies.md §2 `packages/client-sdk/src/` row](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#2-package-path-ownership-map), Plan-006's T4.7 `sessionClient.ts` seam is the sole in-place cross-plan file edit under that directory — Plan-008 adds its own sibling file + barrel line and MUST NOT edit `eventClient.ts`. Provider Plan-006 is a lower tier (Tier 4) → resolves as a Phase Precondition (path c), the CP-008-7 pattern.

**Why bidirectional.** Plan-008 reviewers must see the consume is compose-only and lower-tier-gated; Plan-006 reviewers must see the campaign-B12 consumer leg exists. Reciprocity on Plan-006's side: T4.3's own text states the consumer rule this plan's I-008-12 enforces ("resume from `acknowledged ?? earliest`", the gap predicate, the three converging failure modes) — its absent-`earliest` arm carries a non-normative doc-only cross-reference (added at the W2.5 closing PR #239) marking the full-replay sketch superseded by this plan's I-008-12(d) refusal, the normative home (the B13 one-clause reciprocal precedent; no Plan-006 behavior re-opened) — and the [cross-plan-dependencies.md §3 Plan-008 row](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#3-inter-plan-dependency-graph) carries the graph edge added by this amendment.

### CP-008-11 — Roster-resolved daemon verification key consumed from [Plan-006](./006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md) (Tier 4) for peer history backfill

I-008-13(a) requires the joiner to verify each backfilled event against its **originating** daemon's key. The resolution surface is Plan-006-owned: the register-once control-plane `daemon_signing_public_keys` roster written by `runtimenode.signingkeyregister` and read through `runtimenode.signingkeyroster` ([Plan-006](./006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md) T4.10, CP-006-7 leg B). Plan-008's backfill surface **consumes the read half only** — it registers no key, authors no signature over a peer's bytes, and holds no daemon signing key. Provider Plan-006 is a lower tier (Tier 4) → resolves as a Phase Precondition (path c), the CP-008-7 / CP-008-10 pattern.

**Why bidirectional — and why no Plan-006 edit is owed.** The reciprocal is already in the corpus and needs no amendment: [Security Architecture §Per-Event Daemon Signature](../architecture/security-architecture.md#per-event-daemon-signature) already names "**peer daemon verifying relayed events**" as a first-class consumer of exactly this roster lookup, alongside local replay and forensic export. Backfill is that consumer, deferred in time rather than new in kind — it verifies relayed events that arrive late instead of live — so this obligation records an existing published surface's second call site rather than re-opening Plan-006 behavior (the CP-008-10 doc-only-reciprocal precedent). Plan-008 reviewers must see the consume is read-only and lower-tier-gated; Plan-006 reviewers must see that the backfill leg is a named consumer of T4.10's roster read. The [cross-plan-dependencies.md §3](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#3-inter-plan-dependency-graph) Plan-008 row needs no new edge either: it already carries Plan-008 → Plan-006 (the T3.3 anchor-router tRPC mount per CP-006-3, and the campaign-B12 `EventClient` consume per CP-008-10), so this obligation is a third consume across an existing edge rather than a new one — the contrast with CP-008-10, whose amendment added the edge itself.

### CP-008-12 — Per-channel recipient scoping **provided (forward)** to [Plan-016](./016-multi-agent-channels-and-orchestration.md) / [Spec-016](../specs/016-multi-agent-channels-and-orchestration.md) (Tier 6, downstream), over a forward-declared resolver seam

**Direction: provide (forward).** [Spec-016 §Interfaces And Contracts](../specs/016-multi-agent-channels-and-orchestration.md#interfaces-and-contracts)'s `direct` two-human channel names confidentiality from non-member **humans** as a relay-layer property it "depends on rather than defines", and [Plan-016](./016-multi-agent-channels-and-orchestration.md)'s D-016-21 row records it as "consumed here, defined there". Plan-008 is the _there_: I-008-14 defines it and `Spec-008 §Per-Channel Recipient Scoping (V1)` is its contract. Plan-016 consumes a **property**, not a symbol — it imports nothing from Plan-008 — so the obligation exists to keep a Plan-016 reviewer from re-deriving the mechanism and a Plan-008 reviewer from treating the narrowed fan-out as unmotivated.

**The channel record is not read directly — it arrives through an injected resolver.** The scoping decision needs the channel's kind and, for a `direct` channel, its immutable member pair. That record is **Plan-016-owned** and Plan-016 is Tier 6, strictly downstream of the Tier-5 remainder; a hard consume would invert the build DAG. Plan-008 therefore takes a constructor-injected `ChannelRecipientScopeResolver` — one total async operation resolving a `ChannelId` to `{ kind: "unrestricted" }` or `{ kind: "restricted"; members: readonly ParticipantId[] }`, rejecting on a binding it cannot resolve (I-008-14(a)'s fail-closed arm) — and a `FixtureChannelRecipientScopeResolver` keeps the surface buildable and unit-testable ahead of the producer. **This is the CP-008-8 shape exactly: Plan-008 owns the scoping OPERATION, never the channel record.** The concrete provider is **forward-declared, not an in-DAG dependency** — the plan header's Dependencies and the R4 `Consumes:` line are deliberately unchanged, exactly as CP-008-8 leaves custody out of them — and it is supplied by the plan that owns the channel record when that plan lands. Until then the resolver is unbound in production wiring, and an unbound resolver is a **build-incomplete** state, never an admit-all default: admit-all would silently mean full-session fan-out for `direct` channels, the one outcome I-008-14(a) exists to prevent (contrast CP-008-9's deliberate admit-all default, which fails **safe** because an unbound rate limiter only forgoes throttling). **That rule is enforced, not merely asserted:** the resolver is a **required** constructor parameter and the coordinator **refuses construction** without one — verified by `T-008r-4-T32`, because every other test on this surface binds a resolver, so an admit-all default would be indistinguishable from a correctly bound one across the entire rest of the suite.

**Why bidirectional — and where the reciprocals landed.** Plan-008 reviewers must see that the narrowed fan-out discharges a named downstream dependency rather than inventing policy; Plan-016 reviewers must see that the dependency now has a definition to point at. The two reciprocal edits live on surfaces **this plan does not own**, and both landed alongside this amendment (same PR): the [cross-plan-dependencies.md §3](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#3-inter-plan-dependency-graph) Plan-016 row now carries the Plan-016 → Plan-008 edge this obligation needed (the CP-008-10 edge-adding shape, unlike CP-008-11, which rode an existing edge), and Plan-016's own D-016-21 row records the property as "consumed here, defined there" over this obligation's `ChannelRecipientScopeResolver` seam. Nothing further is owed on either surface.

### CP-008-13 — Received-row provenance: the column ships here, the semantics live in Spec-006, and the receive-append seam edits inside Plan-006's `events/` module are sanctioned (Tier 4/5, registered 2026-08-11)

**Direction: mixed (provide the column; consume the semantics and the append seam).** The 2026-08-11 [Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#canonical-serialization-rules) received-row provenance amendment defines the marker `Spec-008 §Peer History Backfill On Join (V1)` pins: `session_events.received_from_node_id` (`TEXT` nullable — NULL = origin-authored, non-NULL = the origin `NodeId` whose roster key verified the bytes at receive) plus the conditional local-only `receivedFromNodeId` canonical member inside the receiver-signed canonical bytes of received rows (never on the wire; forging the marker in either direction fails `signature_mismatch` under the production verifier). The split is deliberate and this obligation is its registration: **Plan-008 owns the durable column** — T-008r-4-14 ships the additive daemon migration ([cross-plan-dependencies.md §1](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#1-table-ownership-map) contested-column row), because backfill is the surface that needs it as the possession-scoped serving selector (`received_from_node_id IS NULL`, I-008-13(a)) — while **Spec-006 owns the semantics** and Plan-006's shipped surfaces enforce them (the T4.1 verifier's provenance dispatch of both PII binding modes — the four-state compare on origin rows, the require-absent arm on received rows; the canonical-member byte contract; the stub-projection carriage under rule 4's scalar binding).

**The seam: every Plan-008 receive path supplies the verified origin `NodeId` into Plan-006's append, and the marker survives compaction.** The conditional-member emission lives in Plan-006's shipped `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/canonicalizer.ts`; the append path that must accept an optional received-origin parameter and stamp the column is Plan-006's shipped `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/event-log-service.ts`; and the stub-projection preservation of the two backfill-relevant conditional fields — `receivedFromNodeId` and `channelId` per [Spec-006 §Compacted Event Format](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#compacted-event-format) — lives in Plan-006's shipped `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/compactor.ts`. All three edits land with T-008r-4-14 as **sanctioned seam edits under this obligation** — the [CP-003-5](./003-runtime-node-attach.md#cross-plan-obligations) per-file-crossing shape: additive, byte-governed by the Spec-006 amendment (no Plan-006 design is re-decided; an origin-authored append with no parameter is byte-identical to today's output, and a stub of a row carrying neither field projects byte-identically to today's), and registered here plus in Plan-006's §Cross-Plan Amendments entry so neither plan's reviewer reads the crossing as unsanctioned. Contrast CP-008-10, which is compose-only — this obligation exists precisely because the emission, stamping, and projection points cannot be composed from outside their modules.

**Why bidirectional.** A Plan-006 reviewer must see why a Plan-008 task edits `canonicalizer.ts` / `event-log-service.ts` / `compactor.ts` (the Spec-006 amendment is the byte contract; this CP is the sanction); a Plan-008 reviewer must see why the backfill engine never re-implements canonicalization, verification, or projection (it imports Plan-006's production verifier and drives Plan-006's append and compaction seams — transport, stamping, and preservation only, never authorship). The reciprocal registrations landed in the same PR: Plan-006's §Cross-Plan Amendments entry records the two-leg amendment with the column pinned **Plan-008-owned** and carries the owning side's record of this sanction (naming CP-008-13 and all three seam edits), and the [cross-plan-dependencies.md §2](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#2-package-path-ownership-map) `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/` row names the three sanctioned seam edits as the module's one sanctioned in-place extender. Nothing further is owed on either surface.

### CP-008-14 — Daemon JSON-RPC bridge for relay-decrypted payloads: four `session.*` method registrations on Plan-007's registry substrate (Tier 5, registered 2026-08-11 — Codex PR #323 round 2)

**Direction: consume (the registry substrate); provide (the bridge methods).** Pairwise decryption is client-side where bundle custody lives (the T-008r-4-10 coordinator), while roster-resolved verification and log append are daemon authority (the T-008r-4-14 engines) — so without a named client↔daemon bridge the two halves of `Spec-008 §Peer History Backfill On Join (V1)` are unreachable from each other: `packages/runtime-daemon` and `packages/client-sdk` share no dependency edge in either direction, and the daemon-side engines would ship with no production caller. This obligation registers the bridge: **four daemon JSON-RPC methods** — `session.historyBackfillStart` / `session.historyBackfillServe` / `session.historyBackfillDeliver` (T-008r-4-14) and `session.relayEventDeliver` (T-008r-4-10's inbound leg, the live-relay sibling riding the same verify-and-append engine) — as handler files under `packages/runtime-daemon/src/ipc/handlers/`, an EXTEND of the Tier-1 `session.*` namespace root against Plan-007's registry substrate, the same root-extension class as Plan-024's `session.takeControl` / `session.releaseControl` lease pair (CP-024-4) and Plan-016's `session.goalUpdate` / `session.goalClear` pair (D-016-2 / campaign B6): Plan-007 owns the registry surface and the namespace root, never these handlers; Plan-008 adds exactly these handler files and modifies no substrate file. `session.historyBackfillServe` is streaming (subscribe-init ack + chunk notifications over Plan-007's `streaming-primitive.ts`, the `presence.subscribe` / I-007-7 shape); the other three are request/response. Method surface and semantics live at [api-payload-contracts.md §History-Backfill Bridge Method Registry](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md#history-backfill-bridge-method-registry-tier-5-plan-008-t-008r-4-14); the [cross-plan-dependencies.md §2](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#2-package-path-ownership-map) `packages/runtime-daemon/src/ipc/` row carries the extender registration. **Why this is a registration and not a new transport:** the methods ride the established Spec-007 local IPC surface — the same trust boundary every client-initiated daemon action already crosses — and nothing new rides the relay or the control plane (the prior api-payload comment claiming "no daemon JSON-RPC method" was the round-2 finding: it held the relay-payload shapes to a rule that left the engines caller-less).

### CP-008-15 — Store-lineage watermark on [Plan-006](./006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md)'s `daemon_signing_keys` table (Tier 4/5, registered 2026-08-12 — Codex PR #323 round 3)

**Direction: extend (a Plan-006-owned table gains Plan-008-registered lineage columns).** `Spec-008 §Peer History Backfill On Join (V1)`'s carried-forward serving arm requires the source to label each origin-authored (`received_from_node_id IS NULL`) row with the `NodeId` whose roster key verifies it — but a carried-forward store leaves predecessor-authored and current-daemon rows equally unmarked, and no other author-node record exists locally (the round-3 finding). This obligation registers the durable mapping: [`daemon_signing_keys`](../architecture/schemas/local-sqlite-schema.md#audit-log-crypto-tables-plan-006) re-keys `(session_id)` → `(session_id, node_id)` and gains `superseded_at_sequence INTEGER` (NULL = active row) — one row per `NodeId` the store has ever been registered under, the succession watermark stamped by the successor daemon's admission-time registration (the Spec-008 §Named-gap registration hook, which is already the discrete moment a fresh `NodeId` takes over the store) as the store's highest `session_events.sequence`, so a `NULL`-marker row's origin resolves by watermark-window lookup and `servesForOrigins` derives from the same lineage rows. Plan-006 owns the table and its migration `0005-daemon-signing-keys.ts`; Plan-008's T-008r-4-14 ships the **additive lineage migration** (SQLite table rebuild widening the PK; `node_id` backfilled to the daemon's current `NodeId`) and the selector's watermark labeling, the CP-003-5 per-file-crossing shape CP-008-13 already uses for the `events/` seam edits. The reciprocal record lives in Plan-006 §Cross-Plan Amendments; the [cross-plan-dependencies.md §1](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#1-table-ownership-map) `daemon_signing_keys` entry carries the contested-extension note.

### CP-008-16 — Envelope-interior application-payload discriminator: the `RelayApplicationPayload` union and its consumer registration seam (Tier 5, ratified 2026-08-12 by the Plan-014 relay-scope targeted readiness-audit delta)

**Direction: provide (forward).** `Spec-008 §Peer History Backfill On Join (V1)` places backfill payloads inside the existing pairwise ciphertext envelope with **no new frame message type**, citing `Spec-014`'s artifact-key attestations as riding the same seam — and Spec-014 says the same in the other direction. Two message families therefore share one plaintext envelope and neither spec defined the field that separates them on receipt, leaving the T-008r-4-10 coordinator with no input to demultiplex on and Plan-014's Task 7 attestation leg unbuildable. This obligation registers the discriminator as **Plan-008-owned**: `packages/contracts/src/session-join.ts` gains `RelayApplicationPayload`, a Zod `discriminatedUnion` over a **required string tag member `kind`** whose vocabulary is the `RelayApplicationPayloadKind` enum — the same file and the same "the values are a wire-protocol fact contracts must own" ground on which the NS-19 Tier-7 audit hoisted the `RelayFrameType` 1-byte frame enum here (D-025-1), and one level below it: `RelayFrameType` separates frames, `RelayApplicationPayload` separates application payloads **inside** one frame's decrypted plaintext. Plan-008's three arms are `history_backfill_request` (`HistoryBackfillRequest`), `history_backfill_chunk` (`HistoryBackfillChunk`), and `relayed_session_event` (`RelayedSessionEventPayload` — the tagged wrapper nesting exactly one `HistoryBackfillEntry` verbatim, typed at the Codex PR #326 fold because a Zod `discriminatedUnion` needs an object schema per arm and prose is not one; CP-008-14's `session.relayEventDeliver` row consumes it, forwarding the nested entry into the CP-008-13 verify-and-append engine so the live and backfill paths share one entry type). `HistoryBackfillEntry` is chunk-interior and carries no tag — the tag sits on the wrapper. **Unknown-kind is fail-closed:** the coordinator's demux drops a payload whose `kind` is absent or unrecognized with a logged refusal and never dispatches it to a bridge method — a forward-compatible peer's unknown family is discarded, never mistaken for a registered one. Adding `kind` to `HistoryBackfillChunk` additionally pulls the tag into the responder-signed preimage (`responderSignature` covers the chunk's other members), so the tag is unstrippable in transit rather than advisory. **Why bidirectional, and where it lands.** The union block lands with the existing `session-join.ts` contract tasks — CREATEd at **T-008r-1-1**, the union block added alongside the relay wire-frame codec at **T-008r-1-3**, the same family and same Tier-5 remainder as the `RelayFrameType` block — and is consumed by the coordinator demux at **T-008r-4-10** and the bridge engines at **T-008r-4-14**; no existing Plan-008 task is renumbered or re-scoped. Consumers do **not** mint competing tags: each registers its own arm through the seam and keeps its payload schema in its own contract domain, the established `SessionEventSchema` union-registration shape recorded on the [cross-plan-dependencies.md §2](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#2-package-path-ownership-map) `packages/contracts/src/` row. **First registrant: Plan-014**, whose `artifact_key_attestation` arm and `packages/contracts/src/artifacts/`-resident payload schema are recorded at [Plan-014 CP-014-4](./014-artifacts-files-and-attachments.md#cross-plan-obligations-provided) / D-014-5. The registry of kinds and the fail-closed rule live at [api-payload-contracts.md §Envelope-Interior Application-Payload Kind Registry](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md#envelope-interior-application-payload-kind-registry-tier-5-plan-008-t-008r-1-3). **Status unaffected:** everything registered here is additive and unshipped on both sides — a new union, a new enum, and one new required member on two never-implemented interfaces — so no version bump is owed, Plan-008 and Spec-008 both keep `approved`, and **no Spec-008 body text was edited**; a consumer-plan delta registering an additive cross-plan row plus additive unshipped exports into another plan's surface without flipping that plan is the CP-003-5 precedent.

## Ratified Design Decisions (Tier 5 audit, 2026-05-30)

The Tier-5 plan-readiness audit (NS-17) authored the un-tasked Plan-008 remainder (R1–R4) and surfaced four named Open Decisions plus twelve per-phase ODs (the latter author-resolved in-band — see the §Decision Log NS-17 rows). All four named decisions were ratified at the user-review pause and are folded here as the plan's design of record; the cross-cutting corpus amendments they entail (`error-contracts.md`; `shared-postgres-schema.md`; `cross-plan-dependencies.md §2/§3/§5`) are executed at swap alongside this `approved → review` flip. **No per-participant secret crosses from Plan-018** — Plan-008's consumed surfaces are the non-secret PASETO issuer key, the `AuthenticatedIdentityContext` tuple, and the injected `RelayConnectionTokenIssuer`; none is the dissolved "shared `ikm`" (see Plan-018 D-018-1 / Plan-022 D-022-2).

- **OD-008r-1 — three relay error codes registered (Type 1).** `relay.bundle_rejected` (403; R3 bundle-admission of an unregistered identity — OD-3-1), `relay.replay_rejected` (R4 monotonic-seq replay — I-008-8), and `relay.bundle_signature_invalid` (R4 client-side peer-bundle re-verify) are registered in `error-contracts.md` at swap. None reuses the handshake-scoped `relay.authentication_failed`.
- **OD-008r-2 — durable `relay_seen_ephemeral_keys` store (Type 2).** The I-008-7(c) cross-session ephemeral-reuse guard has no storage home in the frozen per-session-DO schema (discard-on-close structurally cannot observe cross-session reuse), so a durable uniqueness-indexed `relay_seen_ephemeral_keys` store is added to the R1 relay migration + `shared-postgres-schema.md` at swap. **Retention is full-horizon by design, not an open sliver.** The dedup key (the ephemeral X25519 public key) is client-minted with no server-anchored birth time, so no bounded TTL can enforce the I-008-7(c) cross-session-reuse guard without reopening the `Spec-008 §Relay Negotiation` forward-secrecy hole — a windowed store forgets a key the client can then re-present under a fresh timestamp. The store therefore remembers every key for the (session × participant) lifetime; growth is an ops concern (time-partition, never `DROP`), decoupled from the security property. The audit ratifies the store as durable + uniqueness-indexed + **full-horizon** (the committed `shared-postgres-schema.md` `relay_seen_ephemeral_keys` design, NS-17 round-6); any future bounding is a separate Spec-008 Type-2 decision, not a V1 open question.
- **OD-008r-3 — inject Plan-018 `RelayConnectionTokenIssuer` (CP-008-4 surface (c); Type 1).** The 300s `RelayNegotiationResponse.connectionToken` is a PASETO v4.public **sign** that MUST NOT run inside the verify-only zero-knowledge broker (I-008-5). R3's `RelayNegotiation` endpoint (T-008r-3-6) consumes a constructor-injected issuer whose signing key + custody stay entirely with Plan-018; R3 authors no sign. The issuer-surface shape is Plan-018-owned.
- **OD-008r-4 — participant signer-provider deferred to ADR-021 / Plan-023; `client-sdk` module placement (Type 1).** R4 owns the Ed25519 signing _operation_ via an injected sign-only `Ed25519IdentitySigner` (CP-008-8); concrete key custody is deferred to ADR-021 (CLI at-rest) / ADR-010 + Plan-023 (desktop WebAuthn-PRF). A `FixtureEd25519IdentitySigner` keeps R4 buildable + unit-testable ahead of the custody producers. The participant relay crypto lands as a `packages/client-sdk/src/relay/` **module** (not a new `packages/crypto-relay/` package — no second cross-package consumer forces one, and minting a package would be unauthorized scaffolding). The reciprocal `provides` note on the custody producer is a fix-in-place obligation at swap.

## Target Areas

- `packages/control-plane/src/server/` — Tier 1 bootstrap-deliverable: Cloudflare Workers host (entry point: `fetchRequestHandler` from `@trpc/server/adapters/fetch`) + tRPC v11 router registration scaffolding (per [ADR-014](../decisions/014-trpc-control-plane-api.md))
- `packages/control-plane/wrangler.toml` — Tier 1 bootstrap-deliverable: workerd local-dev + Cloudflare Workers deployment configuration. Top-level `[vars]` is intentionally OMITTED — neither the bootstrap feature flag nor the development environment marker is placed in any deployable wrangler-config surface, per the I-008-1 allow-list gate semantics established in Codex PR #20 round 4 (see `.dev.vars` bullet for rationale). Only `[env.production.vars]` is set, carrying `ENVIRONMENT = "production"` for `wrangler deploy --env production`; this is intentionally never paired with a bootstrap flag (and gate #2 allow-list rejects `'production'` until Tier 5). The deployable surface carries no security-load-bearing keys, by design. See T-008b-1-1 for the full layout
- `packages/control-plane/.dev.vars` — Tier 1 bootstrap-deliverable: local-only secrets file (gitignored per Cloudflare guidance) supplying BOTH `ENVIRONMENT=development` AND `CONTROL_PLANE_BOOTSTRAP_ENABLED=1` for `wrangler dev` workerd local development. `wrangler deploy` does NOT read this file (per [Cloudflare Workers Secrets docs](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/configuration/secrets/)), so neither key can reach a published Worker via the default deployment path. The `ENVIRONMENT` key is itself security-load-bearing under the I-008-1 allow-list gate (`env.ENVIRONMENT === 'development'`), so co-locating it with the bootstrap flag preserves the structural separation between local-dev secrets and deployable config. A committed `.dev.vars.example` documents the expected keys; root `.gitignore` adds `.dev.vars` + `.dev.vars.*` + `!.dev.vars.example` (the negation pattern exempts the committed template from the ignore glob — without it, contributors would need `git add -f` to update the template)
- `packages/control-plane/src/sessions/session-router.ts` — Tier 1 bootstrap-deliverable: typed tRPC procedures wrapping Plan-001's `session-directory-service.ts`
- `packages/control-plane/src/sessions/session-subscribe-sse.ts` — Tier 1 bootstrap-deliverable: SSE transport plumbing for `SessionSubscribe`
- `packages/contracts/src/session-join.ts` — Tier 5 remainder (including the `HistoryBackfillRequest` / `HistoryBackfillEntry` / `HistoryBackfillChunk` application-payload schemas per [api-payload-contracts.md §Plan-008](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md#plan-008--control-plane-relay-and-session-join), T-008r-4-14 — payloads inside the pairwise ciphertext envelope, never a method registration)
- `packages/control-plane/src/sessions/session-join-service.ts` — Tier 5 remainder
- `packages/control-plane/src/relay/relay-broker-service.ts` — Tier 5 remainder
- `packages/control-plane/src/presence/` — Tier 5 remainder **EXTEND** (sibling hosted-relay-presence-fan-out file; Plan-002 owns `presence-register-service.ts` per CP-008-6 — **F-008r-D** ownership correction: do not create or edit `presence-register-service.ts`)
- `packages/control-plane/src/server/` — Tier 5 remainder **EXTEND**: the cross-cutting **PASETO v4.public validation middleware** (I-008-4 — foundational; consumed by the join / presence services AND the relay WSS handshake) plus relay-broker host wiring. The Tier-1 `host.ts` is not re-architected; Tier 5 adds sibling middleware / wiring files on the same stable mount surface Plan-021 EXTENDS at Tier 6 (CP-008-5)
- `packages/control-plane/src/migrations/` — Tier 5 remainder **EXTEND**: a numbered forward migration creating `session_directory` + `relay_connections` (uncontested Plan-008 tables, [cross-plan-dependencies.md §1](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#1-table-ownership-map)) per [shared-postgres-schema.md §Session Directory and Relay (Plan-008)](../architecture/schemas/shared-postgres-schema.md#session-directory-and-relay-plan-008). The `migrations/` directory is Plan-001-owned — Plan-008 adds a forward migration file, never edits `0001-initial.ts`
- `packages/runtime-daemon/src/backfill/` — Tier 5 remainder **CREATE** (T-008r-4-14, 2026-08-11 restoring delta): the peer-history backfill engine — `history-backfill-source.ts` (the possession-scoped entitled-chunk selector over `received_from_node_id IS NULL` with the four I-008-13(b) exclusions applied at selection time) + `history-backfill-joiner.ts` (per-source request minting, verify-before-append through the per-row origin-bytes signature and anchor-plus-stub checks of Plan-006's production verifier, marker stamping, `id`-keyed first-delivery-wins convergence, two-axis `pending_backfill` coverage). Registered at [cross-plan-dependencies.md §2](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#2-package-path-ownership-map)
- `packages/runtime-daemon/src/migrations/` — Tier 5 remainder **EXTEND** (T-008r-4-14): the additive `0NNN-received-row-provenance.ts` daemon migration adding `session_events.received_from_node_id` (`TEXT` nullable; Plan-008-owned column, Spec-006-owned semantics — [cross-plan-dependencies.md §1](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#1-table-ownership-map)), with the same-commit guarded-block registration in `packages/runtime-daemon/src/session/migration-runner.ts` (the §2 daemon-migrations mirror seam). The directory is Plan-001-owned — Plan-008 adds a forward migration file, never edits an existing one
- `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/` — Tier 5 remainder: three **CP-008-13-sanctioned seam edits** inside Plan-006's directory (T-008r-4-14 — the conditional `receivedFromNodeId` canonical-member emission in `canonicalizer.ts`, the optional received-origin parameter on `event-log-service.ts`'s append seam, and the conditional `receivedFromNodeId` / `channelId` stub-projection preservation in `compactor.ts`), byte-governed by [Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#canonical-serialization-rules) and [Spec-006 §Compacted Event Format](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#compacted-event-format) — never a re-architecture, and everything else in the module is consumed by import only
- `packages/client-sdk/src/relay/` — Tier 5 remainder **CREATE**: participant pairwise-crypto + relay-WSS transport module (the participant side of the zero-knowledge channel R3 brokers — ECDH/HKDF/AEAD/zeroization with an injected Ed25519 signer, CP-008-8). Placement as a `client-sdk` **module** (not a dedicated `packages/crypto-relay/` primitives package) is **ratified** (OD-008r-4 — see §Ratified Design Decisions). The same module holds the coordinator's outbound recipient-set derivation and its injected `ChannelRecipientScopeResolver` seam (I-008-14(a), CP-008-12 — the operation is owned here, the channel record is not)
- `packages/client-sdk/src/sessionJoinClient.ts` — Tier 5 remainder
- `packages/client-sdk/src/resumingEventClient.ts` — Tier 5 remainder **CREATE** (campaign B12): the P1-10 replay-cursor consumer — a `ResumingEventClient` decorator over Plan-006's `EventClient` exposing `resumeAndSubscribe` (T-008r-4-13; compose-only per CP-008-10 — never edits Plan-006's `eventClient.ts`)
- `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/session-join/` — Tier 5 remainder (renderer subtree per [cross-plan-dependencies.md §2 `apps/desktop/src/renderer/` row](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#2-package-path-ownership-map); the substrate is created by Plan-023 Tier 1 Partial per BL-101 (a) resolution, so this surface ships at Tier 5 alongside the rest of Plan-008-remainder)
- `apps/cli/src/session-join/` — Tier 5 remainder

## Data And Storage Changes

- Add shared join, reconnect, and relay-negotiation records needed to correlate membership, presence, and relay attempts.
- Extend presence history with reconnect-association metadata where needed for duplicate suppression.
- See [Shared Postgres Schema](../architecture/schemas/shared-postgres-schema.md) for column definitions.
- **The 2026-08-03 amendment adds no storage on either leg** (I-008-13, I-008-14). Peer history backfill reads a source's existing per-daemon `session_events` log and appends into the joiner's own under [ADR-017](../decisions/017-shared-event-sourcing-scope.md)'s unchanged receive semantics, with coverage held as derived in-session state (no cursor column). Per-channel recipient scoping derives its recipient set per publication from inputs already durable and event-carried on the Plan-016-owned channel record — the channel kind and a `direct` channel's immutable member pair — so no table, column, or store is added or owned here (`Spec-008 §State And Data Implications`).
- **The 2026-08-11 restoring delta adds exactly one column and no table**: `session_events.received_from_node_id` (`TEXT` nullable; NULL = origin-authored) — the received-row provenance marker the [Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#canonical-serialization-rules) amendment defines and the backfill serving selector reads (`received_from_node_id IS NULL`, I-008-13(a)), shipped by T-008r-4-14 as a Plan-008-owned additive daemon migration on Plan-001's `session_events` ([local-sqlite-schema.md §Session Events](../architecture/schemas/local-sqlite-schema.md#session-events-plan-001-extended-by-plans-006-008-015); ownership row at [cross-plan-dependencies.md §1](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#1-table-ownership-map), seam sanction at CP-008-13). Coverage state stays derived in-session — still no cursor column.

## API And Transport Changes

- Add `SessionJoin`, `PresenceRegister`, `RelayNegotiation`, and `SessionResumeAfterReconnect` to shared contracts and client SDKs.
- Ensure relay negotiation only exposes the minimum transport data required to establish connectivity.
- **The 2026-08-03 amendment adds no wire surface on either leg.** `HistoryBackfillRequest` / `HistoryBackfillEntry` / `HistoryBackfillChunk` ride the existing pairwise ciphertext envelope as application-level payloads (typed since 2026-08-11 at [api-payload-contracts.md §Plan-008](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md#plan-008--control-plane-relay-and-session-join), T-008r-4-14 — no new frame message type, no `SessionKeyBundle` change, no relay message class, and no control-plane procedure; the local client↔daemon boundary is a different matter, crossed by the four CP-008-14 `session.*` JSON-RPC bridge registrations per the 2026-08-11 round-2 fold; a response is split at the application layer wherever a chunk would exceed the 64 KB frame ceiling), and per-channel recipient scoping constrains only which envelopes a sender produces, leaving the R1 frame codec, the R3 broker, and the receiving path untouched — no relay wire method, frame type, envelope member, or error code (I-008-14).

## Implementation Steps

- Contracts: See [API Payload Contracts](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md) for typed schemas this plan consumes.
- Tier markers below correspond to §Execution Windows (V1 Carve-Out): `[Tier 1]` = ships in Plan-008-bootstrap; `[Tier 5]` = ships in Plan-008-remainder. The renderer subtree ships at Tier 5 alongside the rest of Plan-008-remainder (the `apps/desktop/src/renderer/` substrate is created by Plan-023 Tier 1 Partial per BL-101 (a) resolution).
- Plan-008's invariants govern tier scoping — see §Invariants I-008-1 (bootstrap is operator-development-only behind a feature flag), I-008-2 (Tier 5 production invariants), I-008-3 (substrate wraps, never re-implements directory service).

1. **[Tier 1: tRPC server skeleton + sessionRouter + SSE substrate; Tier 5: relay-negotiation + presence + reconnect contracts]** Define authenticated join, presence, reconnect, and relay-negotiation contracts. Include the relay wire format for relay messages. Per F-008b-1-07 expansion, the Tier 1 portion breaks into 4 sub-actions:
   - **(a)** Register tRPC v11 router via `@trpc/server/adapters/fetch` (Cloudflare Workers per ADR-014 + BL-104 resolution). The Worker's default `fetch` export delegates to `fetchRequestHandler({ endpoint, req, router, createContext })`; no separate adapter package beyond `@trpc/server/adapters/fetch` is required.
   - **(b)** Wire the I-008-1 dual-gate enforcement at the request-entry site: feature-flag check (`CONTROL_PLANE_BOOTSTRAP_ENABLED === '1'` from `env`) AND approved-dev-environment allow-list check (`env.ENVIRONMENT === 'development'` per F-008b-1-04 Workers reformulation + Codex PR #20 round 4 allow-list pivot) — both refusal paths return HTTP 503 immediately before any router dispatch.
   - **(c)** SSE subscription handling for `SessionSubscribe` is provided natively by tRPC's shared HTTP resolver (`resolveResponse.ts` substrate) which all adapters — including the fetch adapter on Workers — delegate to for subscription procedures. The substrate detects `proc._def.type === 'subscription'`, sets SSE headers, and wraps async generators / observables into a `ReadableStream`. No separate SSE adapter required. The canonical wire-frame primitive (Content-Type / `data:` encoding / `id:`/`retry:` semantics) is ratified at [api-payload-contracts.md §SSE Wire Frame (Tier 1 Ratified)](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md#sse-wire-frame-tier-1-ratified) (closed 2026-04-30).
   - **(d)** Inject `SessionDirectoryService` per I-008-3 enforcement mechanism #1 (constructor injection): factory function takes the directory-service instance and returns the typed tRPC router.
2. **[Tier 5]** Implement control-plane join and presence services with membership verification and invite-acceptance handoff. Requires Plan-002 (invite/presence) substrates per I-008-2.
3. **[Tier 5]** Implement relay broker flows and reconnect association logic without coupling them to execution authority. V1 relay encryption uses pairwise X25519 ECDH + XChaCha20-Poly1305 (via `@noble/curves` and `@noble/ciphers`) with Ed25519 signature verification over each participant's ephemeral X25519 key bundle per [ADR-010](../decisions/010-paseto-webauthn-mls-auth.md). Relay sharding targets 25 connections per data DO using WebSocket Hibernation for Durable Objects. Relay authentication uses PASETO v4 tokens (per ADR-010, requires Plan-018 issuer surface — I-008-2).
4. **[Tier 5]** Add desktop and CLI shared-session join surfaces plus typed client SDK integration. CLI surface ships at Tier 5 alongside the join service; desktop renderer subtree at `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/session-join/` ships at Tier 5 (per [cross-plan-dependencies.md §2 row for `apps/desktop/src/renderer/`](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#2-package-path-ownership-map); substrate created by Plan-023 Tier 1 Partial per BL-101 (a) resolution).

## Tier 1 Bootstrap PR Sequence

The Tier 1 bootstrap-deliverable (per §Execution Windows above) lands as **1 small PR**. The Tier 5 remainder PR breakdown is deferred to plan-execution time when Tier 5 begins.

### Phase 1: Bootstrap (tRPC v11 server + `sessionRouter` + SSE substrate)

**Goal:** Plan-008-bootstrap ships end-to-end behind passing tRPC + SSE integration tests. `sessionRouter` exposes Plan-001 Phase 4's `session-directory-service.ts` over HTTP; `SessionSubscribe` SSE substrate is functional. Plan-001 Phase 5 unblocks on this PR's merge (in conjunction with Plan-007 partial Phase 3).

**Spec-008 AC coverage (per F-008b-1-06).** Phase 1 covers NO Spec-008 AC. The substrate-only deliverable is a Tier 1 unblock for Plan-001 Phase 5; [Spec-008](../specs/008-control-plane-relay-and-session-join.md) lines 257-259 (3 ACs: authenticated session join, direct↔relay switching, control-plane-not-execution-authority) all require Tier 5 surfaces (relay broker, presence register, invite handoff) and land in Plan-008-remainder. The §Done Checklist explicitly enforces this — the gate cannot be flipped to `completed` on the basis of skeleton-only delivery.

**Precondition:** [Plan-001](./001-shared-session-core.md) Phase 4 merged (the bootstrap wraps that PR's `packages/control-plane/src/sessions/session-directory-service.ts` — already shipped 2026-04-27 per [GitHub PR-#10](https://github.com/Sawmonabo/ai-sidekicks/pull/10)). Plan-001 Phase 2 schemas (`packages/contracts/src/session.ts` + `packages/contracts/src/event.ts`) merged. ADR-014 runtime authorization reconciled per §Preconditions (BL-104 resolved 2026-04-30 — Cloudflare Workers via `@trpc/server/adapters/fetch`).

```yaml
  - { type: audit_status, status: substrate_exempt, carve_out_ref: "Plan-008 Bootstrap-vs-Remainder Carve-Out" }
```

The `audit_status: substrate_exempt` declaration is documentary — Phase 1 already shipped via PR #21 (2026-04-30) and preflight Gate 3 filters it. The YAML closes `Plan-001 §Preconditions`'s named governance debt for Plan-008 alongside the per-phase audit semantics introduced in [the readiness-audit runbook §Per-Phase Audit Semantics](../operations/plan-implementation-readiness-audit-runbook.md#per-phase-audit-semantics). Plan-008's plan-level audit checkbox is checked in §Preconditions — the Tier 5 remainder audit completed 2026-05-30 (NS-17); the box row itself was added by the W2.5 targeted re-audit closing PR (#239), which found the swap had never added it.

- `packages/control-plane/src/server/` — host + tRPC v11 router registration scaffolding per [ADR-014](../decisions/014-trpc-control-plane-api.md). **Runtime: Cloudflare Workers via `@trpc/server/adapters/fetch`** (BL-104 resolved 2026-04-30 — see §Decision Log). Local development uses workerd (`wrangler dev`); production deploys to Cloudflare's edge. Skeleton only — relay broker / presence register / invite handlers ship in Plan-008-remainder at Tier 5. Per §Invariants I-008-1, the bootstrap MUST be gated behind a feature flag (e.g., `CONTROL_PLANE_BOOTSTRAP_ENABLED=1`) defaulting to off AND must enforce the approved-dev-environment allow-list gate (per I-008-1 verification gate #2 + F-008b-1-04 Workers reformulation + Codex PR #20 round 4 allow-list pivot — `env.ENVIRONMENT === 'development'`); deployable wrangler-config surfaces MUST NOT carry the flag NOR the `ENVIRONMENT=development` marker until Tier 5.
- `packages/control-plane/src/sessions/session-router.ts` — typed tRPC procedures wrapping Plan-001 Phase 4's `session-directory-service.ts`. The router does NOT re-implement directory logic per §Invariants I-008-3 (constructor-injection enforcement). Per F-008b-1-03 resolution, the procedures are:

  | tRPC procedure | Procedure type | Input schema (from `packages/contracts/src/session.ts`) | Output schema | Directory-service method called | Tier 1 middleware |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | `session.create` | `mutation` | `SessionCreateRequestSchema` | `SessionCreateResponseSchema` | `directoryService.createSession(...)` | request-id stamping; Querier-injection context (no PASETO/rate-limit at Tier 1 — those land at Tier 5 per I-008-2) |
  | `session.read` | `query` | `SessionReadRequestSchema` | `SessionReadResponseSchema` | `directoryService.readSession(...)` | request-id stamping; Querier-injection context |
  | `session.join` | `mutation` | `SessionJoinRequestSchema` | `SessionJoinResponseSchema` | `directoryService.joinSession(...)` | request-id stamping; Querier-injection context (Tier 1 stub: rejects non-self joins until invite/presence land at Tier 5 — see I-008-2) |

  The procedure type assignments follow the tRPC convention: read-only operations use `query` (HTTP GET-like, idempotent); writes/state-changes use `mutation` (HTTP POST-like, non-idempotent). Closed 2026-04-30: procedure-type assignments and canonical `dotted-camelCase` method-name strings (`session.create`, `session.read`, `session.join`) are ratified at [api-payload-contracts.md §Tier 1 (cont.): Plan-008](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md#tier-1-cont-plan-008--plan-008-bootstrap-control-plane-trpc--sse-substrate) (cross-transport consistency with Plan-007 JSON-RPC IPC per the same source).

- `packages/control-plane/src/sessions/session-subscribe-sse.ts` — SSE transport plumbing for `SessionSubscribe`. The contract is request-only on the wire; the response is an `AsyncIterable<EventEnvelope>` SSE stream per `packages/contracts/src/session.ts#SessionSubscribeRequest`. Bootstrap supplies only the transport — event sourcing into the stream remains Plan-006's domain. Wire frame ratified at [api-payload-contracts.md §SSE Wire Frame (Tier 1 Ratified)](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md#sse-wire-frame-tier-1-ratified) (closed 2026-04-30): `Content-Type: text/event-stream; charset=utf-8`; `Cache-Control: no-store`; `X-Accel-Buffering: no`; one `EventEnvelope` per SSE event encoded as `data: <single-line JSON>`; `id:` carries the `EventCursor` value from Plan-006 (or placeholder string at Tier 1 pending Plan-006 widening); `retry: 5000`; on reconnect with `Last-Event-ID`, server emits all events strictly after that cursor; `event: heartbeat\ndata: {}\n\n` every 15s. **SSE adapter selection is settled by BL-104 resolution:** tRPC v11's shared HTTP resolver (`packages/server/src/unstable-core-do-not-import/http/resolveResponse.ts` upstream) detects subscription procedures and produces the SSE-streaming `Response` natively when invoked through `@trpc/server/adapters/fetch`'s `fetchRequestHandler` on Cloudflare Workers. No separate SSE adapter is required.

#### Tasks

- **T-008b-1-1** (Files: `packages/control-plane/src/server/host.ts` (CREATE) + `packages/control-plane/src/server/feature-flag-gate.ts` (CREATE) + `packages/control-plane/src/server/dev-environment-gate.ts` (CREATE) + `packages/control-plane/wrangler.toml` (CREATE) + `packages/control-plane/.dev.vars.example` (CREATE) + `<repo-root>/.gitignore` (EXTEND with `.dev.vars` + `.dev.vars.*` + `!.dev.vars.example` — negation pattern exempts the committed template from the ignore glob per round-5a Codex finding); Verifies invariant: I-008-1 dual-gate; Spec coverage: per F-008b-1-06, NO Spec-008 AC at Tier 1) — Implement the tRPC v11 host scaffolding (Cloudflare Workers via `@trpc/server/adapters/fetch`'s `fetchRequestHandler`, per BL-104 resolution) behind I-008-1 dual-gate enforcement. The feature-flag gate (`env.CONTROL_PLANE_BOOTSTRAP_ENABLED === '1'` checked at request-entry, refuse-with-503 before router dispatch when unset) AND the approved-dev-environment allow-list gate (`env.ENVIRONMENT === 'development'` per F-008b-1-04 Workers reformulation + the Codex round-4 allow-list pivot, refuse-with-503 on `undefined`/`'production'`/`'staging'`/`'test'`/`''`/anything else even with flag set) BOTH ship in this task.

  **`wrangler.toml` layout.** Top-level `[vars]` is **OMITTED** — neither the bootstrap feature flag nor the `ENVIRONMENT` marker is placed in any deployable wrangler-config surface. Only `[env.production.vars]` is present, setting `ENVIRONMENT = "production"` for `wrangler deploy --env production`. This `'production'` value never satisfies the allow-list gate (`=== 'development'` only), so even pairing it with a hypothetical bootstrap flag in `[env.production.vars]` cannot satisfy gate #2 — the deployable surface carries no security-load-bearing keys by design. The omission of top-level `[vars]` is deliberate per Wrangler's `vars` optionality (per [Cloudflare Workers Wrangler config docs](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/wrangler/configuration/) — "vars: object optional"); a default `wrangler deploy` (without `--env`) publishes a Worker with no `ENVIRONMENT` set and no bootstrap flag, so even `wrangler secret put CONTROL_PLANE_BOOTSTRAP_ENABLED 1` against that Worker still fails gate #2 because `ENVIRONMENT` is `undefined` (allow-list rejects it).

  **Bootstrap flag + `ENVIRONMENT` marker location.** Both keys are supplied locally via `packages/control-plane/.dev.vars` — literal contents:

  ```
  ENVIRONMENT=development
  CONTROL_PLANE_BOOTSTRAP_ENABLED=1
  ```

  `wrangler dev` reads `.dev.vars` automatically for workerd local development per [Cloudflare Workers Secrets docs](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/configuration/secrets/) ("Put secrets for use in local development in either a `.dev.vars` file or a `.env` file, in the same directory as the Wrangler configuration file"). `wrangler deploy` does NOT consult `.dev.vars` (deployed Workers receive secrets only via `wrangler secret put`, the dashboard, or `--secrets-file`), so neither key can reach a published Worker through the default deployment path. The live `.dev.vars` MUST be gitignored — root `.gitignore` extends per Cloudflare's explicit guidance ("Do not commit secrets to git. The `.dev.vars` and `.env` files should not committed to git") with the following block (literal contents):

  ```
  # Cloudflare Workers local-development secrets — never commit live values
  .dev.vars
  .dev.vars.*
  # Exempt the committed example template from the wildcard above
  !.dev.vars.example
  ```

  The `!.dev.vars.example` negation is mandatory: without it, `.dev.vars.*` matches `.dev.vars.example` and Git ignores the committed template, forcing contributors to use `git add -f` to update it (per the round-5a Codex finding). A committed `packages/control-plane/.dev.vars.example` mirrors the expected keys (same literal contents above) and instructs implementers to copy it to `.dev.vars` for local `wrangler dev` runs.

  **Why both keys live in `.dev.vars` (not just the flag).** Under allow-list gate semantics, `ENVIRONMENT` itself is security-load-bearing: gate #2 passes only when `ENVIRONMENT === 'development'`. Co-locating both keys in `.dev.vars` — the only Wrangler surface that `wrangler deploy` does not read — preserves the structural separation between local-dev secrets and deployable config. If `ENVIRONMENT="development"` lived in top-level `[vars]` (deployable), a default `wrangler deploy` followed by `wrangler secret put CONTROL_PLANE_BOOTSTRAP_ENABLED 1` would satisfy both gates on a publicly reachable Worker — exactly the round-4 exposure path Codex flagged.

  Tests: T-008b-1-T1 handler refuses without flag (I-008-1 gate #1); T-008b-1-T2 handler refuses on every non-`'development'` `ENVIRONMENT` value even with flag set (I-008-1 gate #2 allow-list — table-driven over `undefined`, `'production'`, `'staging'`, `'test'`, `''`); T-008b-1-T3 handler serves with both keys set under `wrangler dev` (`.dev.vars` supplies `ENVIRONMENT=development` AND `CONTROL_PLANE_BOOTSTRAP_ENABLED=1`). (Audit trail for prior `[env.dev]`, top-level-`[vars]`-flag, and deny-list-gate task drafts: see §Decision Log.)

- **T-008b-1-2** (Files: `packages/control-plane/src/sessions/session-router.ts` (CREATE) + `packages/control-plane/src/sessions/session-router.factory.ts` (CREATE); Verifies invariant: I-008-3 enforcement #1 (constructor injection); Spec coverage: §Cross-Plan Obligations CP-008-1) — Implement the typed tRPC router via `createSessionRouter(directoryService: SessionDirectoryService): TRPCRouter` factory. The 3 procedures (per the table above) bind to `directoryService.createSession` / `readSession` / `joinSession` exclusively; the factory does NOT instantiate `Querier` or `pg.Pool` directly. Procedure-type assignments + canonical method-name strings (`dotted-camelCase` `session.create` / `session.read` / `session.join`) are ratified at [api-payload-contracts.md §Tier 1 (cont.): Plan-008](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md#tier-1-cont-plan-008--plan-008-bootstrap-control-plane-trpc--sse-substrate) (closed 2026-04-30). Tests: T-008b-1-T4 round-trip `session.create` end-to-end against `pg.Pool`-backed `Querier` (I-001-1 lock-ordering inherited via directory service); T-008b-1-T5 round-trip `session.read`; T-008b-1-T6 round-trip `session.join` (Tier 1 stub: self-joins succeed; non-self joins reject with `auth.not_authorized` until Tier 5 invite/presence lands).
- **T-008b-1-3** (Files: `packages/control-plane/src/sessions/session-subscribe-sse.ts` (CREATE) + `packages/control-plane/src/sessions/session-subscribe-sse.factory.ts` (CREATE); Verifies invariant: I-008-3 enforcement #1 (constructor injection); Spec coverage: §Cross-Plan Obligations CP-008-1 + CP-008-3) — Implement SSE substrate via `createSessionSubscribeSse(directoryService: SessionDirectoryService): SseHandler` factory. The SSE-streaming `Response` is produced natively by tRPC's shared HTTP resolver when the subscription procedure is invoked through `fetchRequestHandler`; the factory wires the directory-service into the subscription procedure's async-generator body. Wire frame primitive ratified at [api-payload-contracts.md §SSE Wire Frame (Tier 1 Ratified)](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md#sse-wire-frame-tier-1-ratified) (closed 2026-04-30); frame-shaping logic centralized in the factory. Tests: T-008b-1-T7 SSE connection lifecycle (open + send synthetic EventEnvelope + close on disconnect); T-008b-1-T8 `Last-Event-ID` resumption (reconnect with header → server emits events strictly after cursor); T-008b-1-T9 heartbeat cadence (15s `event: heartbeat` frames in absence of data).
- **T-008b-1-4** (Files: `packages/control-plane/.eslintrc.js` (EXTEND with `no-restricted-imports`) + `packages/control-plane/test/sessions/router-no-sql.test.ts` (CREATE); Verifies invariant: I-008-3 enforcement #2 (ESLint rule) + #3 (unit-test introspection)) — Land the I-008-3 enforcement mechanism: ESLint `no-restricted-imports` rule forbidding `pg`, `pg-pool`, `@databases/pg` imports from `session-router.ts` and `session-subscribe-sse.ts`; CI fails on rule violation. Unit-test introspection asserts the two files' exported symbols call only `directoryService.*` methods (TypeScript symbol introspection or AST walker pinned in the test runner). Tests: T-008b-1-T10 ESLint rule trips on direct `pg` import in router; T-008b-1-T11 introspection assertion catches new SQL via direct `Querier` instantiation in router.

T-008b-1-T12 round-trip integration test: a stub `sessionClient.subscribe` (using the contracts-side schema from Plan-001 Phase 2) connects to the Phase 1 SSE substrate, receives a synthetic `EventEnvelope` (sourced via test fixture, since Plan-006 event sourcing is out of Phase 1 scope), and verifies cursor-based resumption via `Last-Event-ID`. Frame shape canonical at [api-payload-contracts.md §SSE Wire Frame (Tier 1 Ratified)](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md#sse-wire-frame-tier-1-ratified) (closed 2026-04-30); test asserts on that ratified shape. **Spec coverage:** Spec-008 §Required Behavior (control-plane transport SSE substrate integration with Plan-001 Phase 5 — cursor-based resumption via `Last-Event-ID`).

## Tier 5 Remainder PR Sequence

The Tier 5 remainder (relay broker, presence fan-out, invite-acceptance handoff, reconnect / relay negotiation, the `session_directory` + `relay_connections` tables, and the desktop / CLI / SDK join surfaces) decomposes into **four phases**, mirroring [§Implementation Steps](#implementation-steps) 1-4 above and the Plan-007-remainder R1/R2/R3 authoring precedent (NS-16 / PR #124). This section closes the original "deferred to plan-execution time" gap — the readiness audit authors the decomposition so the preflight G4 traceability gate has Tasks rows to cite. Task-id namespace: **`T-008r-{phase}-{n}`** (`r` = remainder); tests: **`T-008r-{phase}-T{n}`**.

**Build order within the remainder.** R1 (contracts + schema + the foundational PASETO-validation middleware) is the spine R2/R3/R4 consume — it ships first. R2 (join + presence) and R3 (zero-knowledge relay broker) may proceed in parallel once R1 lands. R4 (client surfaces **+ participant relay crypto**) consumes R3's `SessionKeyBundle`-admission + relay-WSS endpoints and R2/R3 reconnect-association semantics, so it follows R2 + R3 (per [§Parallelization Notes](#parallelization-notes)).

**Cross-tier `Consumes:` provenance** (every out-of-remainder symbol R2/R3/R4 import resolves to one of):

- **(b) Tier-1-shipped** — `@ai-sidekicks/crypto-paseto` verify/sign (PR #92); `@noble/curves` (R3 broker: `ed25519.verify` for bundle admission only; R4 participant: X25519 ECDH) + `@noble/ciphers` XChaCha20-Poly1305 (R4 participant AEAD) — audited libs per [ADR-010 §Decision](../decisions/010-paseto-webauthn-mls-auth.md#decision), `Spec-008 §Relay Encryption`.
- **(c) lower-tier Precondition** — Plan-001 `session-directory-service.ts` + `sessions` / `session_memberships` (Tier 1); Plan-002 `invite-service.ts` + `presence/` directory + `session_invites` (Tier 2, shipped NS-14 / NS-26); Plan-006 `EventClient` (T4.7 `eventClient.ts`) + T4.3 cursor codec + T4.5 replay-then-live handoff (Tier 4, campaign B12 — CP-008-10).
- **(co-tier intra-Tier-5 Precondition)** — Plan-018 PASETO issuer key + `AuthenticatedIdentityContext` (CP-008-4; §5 ordering edge F-008r-C recorded at swap).
- **Not consumed here:** rate-limiting is the Tier-6 forward obligation CP-008-5, not an R-phase consume.

**Each phase lands as one PR** (R3 may split into admission-service / forwarding-DO, and R4 into participant-crypto-module / client-surfaces, if the diff exceeds review scope — decided at plan-execution time per the Tier-1 single-PR precedent).

### Phase R1 — Tier-5 Contracts + Schema + Foundational Auth Middleware

**Goal:** Author the Zod contracts at `packages/contracts/src/session-join.ts` for `ControlPlaneSessionJoin` / `PresenceRegister` / `RelayNegotiation` / `SessionResumeAfterReconnect` + the `SessionKeyBundle` relay-handshake type + the relay binary wire frame, the `session_directory` + `relay_connections` migration, and the cross-cutting **PASETO v4.public validation middleware** (I-008-4) consumed by R2 + R3. **The contract shapes are already ratified** at [api-payload-contracts.md §Plan-008](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md#plan-008--control-plane-relay-and-session-join) (the four request/response interface pairs) and [Spec-008 §Relay Negotiation](../specs/008-control-plane-relay-and-session-join.md#relay-negotiation) (`SessionKeyBundle` fields; wire frame `[4-byte BE length][1-byte type][payload]`); R1 authors Zod schemas **matching** the canonical illustration (code is canonical per the api-payload-contracts §Source-of-Truth Policy), not new shapes.

**Spec-008 AC coverage:** none directly (contracts are substrate); enables AC1/AC2/AC3 at R2/R3/R4.

**Precondition:** Plan-001 Phase 2 contracts package + `sessions` schema (Tier 1, shipped); `crypto-paseto` verify primitive (PR #92); **Plan-018's Tier-5 issuer surface + migration merged** — T-008r-1-5's middleware injects the Plan-018-published PASETO issuer key and consumes the `AuthenticatedIdentityContext` producer shape (CP-008-4, the task's own Consumes line), and T-008r-1-T8's fresh-DB chain assertion requires the Plan-018-owned `participants`/`identity_mappings` migration to precede the relay migration (F-008r-C — widened from the R2/R3-only scoping at the W2.5 promotion; the header Dependencies row and the dep-map §5 Tier-5 cell carry the same widening).

**Target files:** `packages/contracts/src/session-join.ts` (CREATE); `packages/control-plane/src/migrations/` (EXTEND — forward migration); `packages/control-plane/src/server/` (EXTEND — PASETO-validation middleware).

- **T-008r-1-2** (Files: `packages/contracts/src/session-join.ts` (EXTEND — same file as T-008r-1-1, additive `SessionKeyBundle` block); Verifies invariant: I-008-7, I-008-6 (substrate — the `SessionKeyBundle` admission shape the R3 control-plane admission check verifies, Ed25519 sig over `session_id ‖ ephemeral_x25519_public`, plus the per-session ephemeral material the type frames; admission directly verified at R3); Spec coverage: `Spec-008 §Relay Negotiation` (substrate; enables AC2 at R3)) — Author the `SessionKeyBundleSchema` relay-handshake type MATCHING [Spec-008 §Relay Negotiation](../specs/008-control-plane-relay-and-session-join.md#relay-negotiation): `{ ephemeralX25519Public: <32-byte>, ed25519IdentityPublic: <32-byte>, signature: <64-byte> }` where the signature is the Ed25519 signature over `session_id ‖ ephemeral_x25519_public` (`Spec-008 §Relay Negotiation`). Encode the byte-length invariants on the wire shape: the two public keys are 32 bytes each (`Spec-008 §Relay Negotiation`) and the Ed25519 signature is 64 bytes (RFC 8032; pinned by the module-level `SIGNATURE_LENGTH` constant (64) that `packages/crypto-paseto/src/v4-public.ts#verifyV4Public` slices by) — represent as base64url-or-hex bounded strings or fixed-length `Uint8Array` validators consistent with the package's existing byte-field convention, asserting exact decoded length. The attested `participantId` is supplied by R3 from the admission-authenticated identity at distribution time — R1 authors only the record TYPE, and never adds a participant field to the client-signed `SessionKeyBundle` itself. **Scope guard (#1d):** R1 authors the bundle TYPE only — the X25519 ECDH, HKDF-SHA256 derivation (`salt=session_id`, `info="ai-sidekicks/v1/pairwise"`, len 32; `Spec-008 §Relay Encryption`), signature VERIFICATION, single-use admission (`Spec-008 §Relay Negotiation`), and registered-identity admission (`Spec-008 §Trust Properties`) are all R3 crypto behavior, not R1.
