- **T-008r-1-3** (Files: `packages/contracts/src/session-join.ts` (EXTEND — additive relay wire-frame codec block) — OR a sibling `packages/contracts/src/relay-wire-frame.ts` (CREATE) + `packages/contracts/src/index.ts` (EXTEND — `export * from "./relay-wire-frame.js"`) if the codec's imperative read/write helpers warrant separation from the declarative Zod schemas, decided at execution; Verifies invariant: I-008-5, I-008-8 (substrate — the frame treats `[payload]` as an opaque ciphertext envelope the zero-knowledge relay forwards uninspected, and the AEAD-bound replay sequence lives inside that opaque payload never in the frame header; both directly verified at R3); Spec coverage: `Spec-008 §Message Framing` (substrate; enables AC2 at R3)) — Implement the relay binary wire-frame codec MATCHING [Spec-008 §Message Framing](../specs/008-control-plane-relay-and-session-join.md#message-framing): `[4-byte big-endian length][1-byte message type][payload]`. Author `encodeRelayFrame(messageType, payload: Uint8Array): Uint8Array` and `decodeRelayFrame(frame: Uint8Array): { messageType: number; payload: Uint8Array }` as a small encapsulated codec (a class with the two methods, or a frozen module object — OOP-for-stateful-boundary per the calibration rule; no free-floating mutable state). The codec writes/reads the 4-byte big-endian length prefix + 1-byte type byte and slices the opaque `payload` WITHOUT inspecting or decrypting it (I-008-5 honest-but-curious relay boundary). Enforce the **64 KB maximum frame size** (`Spec-008 §Message Framing` — "Messages larger than 64 KB must be chunked at the application layer before encryption") on the **whole wire frame** — the single quantity both directions bound, so the cap and its tests apply to that one value: encode rejects when the frame it would emit (`4 + 1 + payload.length`) exceeds 64 KB; decode reads the 4-byte prefix as `declaredLength` (the type + payload byte count, T-008r-1-T6), validates `declaredLength ≥ 1` and `frame.length === 4 + declaredLength` (so a truncated or over-long frame is rejected rather than silently sliced), and rejects when `frame.length` exceeds 64 KB — mirroring the defense-in-depth body-cap pattern already used in this package (cf. `EVENT_CURSOR_MAX_LEN`, `session.ts`). The 1-byte message-type field's two categories — forwardable pairwise-ciphertext envelopes vs broker-handled control frames — are declared HERE in contracts as the `RelayFrameType` wire enum (NS-19 audit hoist; see §Decision Log 2026-06-15), authored as a cross-package-safe `as const` object + derived union (`export const RelayFrameType = { … } as const; export type RelayFrameType = (typeof RelayFrameType)[keyof typeof RelayFrameType]`) — **not** a `const enum`, which transpile-only / `isolatedModules` builds cannot inline across the package boundary the three consumers (R3 broker T-008r-3-9, R4 client-SDK T-008r-4-8, and the Plan-025 self-host relay-node) import from. `encodeRelayFrame`/`decodeRelayFrame` keep the opaque `number` at the wire boundary; consumers map `number` ↔ `RelayFrameType`. The numeric values are a wire-protocol fact, so contracts (R1) owns them — **superseding** the original author-resolved R1 decision that deferred the assignment to R3 (§Decision Log NS-17 R1); the category semantics + routing are still verified at R3 (T-008r-3-T21/22/23). Tests: T-008r-1-T5 encode→decode round-trip preserves `messageType` + byte-identical `payload`; T-008r-1-T6 big-endian length-prefix correctness across a 1-byte and a multi-KB payload (assert bytes 0-3 are the BE-encoded payload+type length); T-008r-1-T7 reject encode whose resulting frame would exceed 64 KB, and reject decode of a frame whose total size exceeds 64 KB — both assertions bounding the same whole-frame quantity — plus a boundary case: a payload that fills a frame to exactly 64 KB encodes and decodes round-trip (the prior asymmetry — payload-capped on encode, declared-length-capped on decode — mis-handled this boundary).

- **T-008r-1-5** (Files: `packages/control-plane/src/server/paseto-validation-middleware.ts` (CREATE) + `packages/control-plane/src/server/paseto-validation-middleware.factory.ts` (CREATE — DI factory mirroring the `session-router.factory.ts` constructor-injection precedent) + `packages/control-plane/src/server/host.ts` (EXTEND — the 2026-08-12 host-wiring leg below); Verifies invariant: I-008-4 (PASETO v4.public validation on every Tier-5 endpoint AND the relay WSS handshake before any dispatch); Spec coverage: `Spec-008 §Relay Connection Lifecycle` (`Sec-WebSocket-Protocol: paseto-v4, <base64url(token)>` handshake) + `Spec-008 §Interfaces And Contracts` (`SessionJoin` requires authenticated identity)) — Implement the cross-cutting **foundational PASETO v4.public validation middleware** that the R2 join/presence services AND the R3 relay WSS handshake mount on. It validates a `v4.public` token via the Tier-1-shipped `verifyV4Public(token, issuerPublicKey)` primitive from `@ai-sidekicks/crypto-paseto` (`packages/crypto-paseto/src/v4-public.ts#verifyV4Public`; already a `packages/control-plane` dependency) against the **Plan-018-published issuer public key** (CP-008-4), refusing dispatch on an absent / expired / invalid / wrong-issuer token. The middleware also threads an **optional implicit-assertion** to `verifyV4Public(token, issuerPublicKey, footer?, implicitAssertion?)` (the Tier-1 primitive's 4th parameter, per `packages/crypto-paseto/src/v4-public.ts#verifyV4Public`) — which validates the Ed25519 signature over the implicit-assertion-bound payload and **returns the payload bytes without checking registered claims**. The middleware therefore enforces the registered claims **explicitly** on the verified payload, with **per-surface audience separation** so a token minted for one surface cannot replay to another (RFC 8725 "cross-JWT confusion" mitigation — a single issuer mints both relay-connect and endpoint-auth tokens, so each surface MUST validate `aud` and refuse a token carrying a different audience; [RFC 8725](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8725.html)): `exp` (unexpired) on every surface; the R3 relay handshake requires `aud == relay-connect`; the R2 endpoint surfaces — the join / presence / resume endpoints, which authenticate with the participant's endpoint-auth access token — **reject** an `aud == relay-connect` token (`Spec-008 §Relay Negotiation`: relay-connect is the audience "distinguishing it from endpoint-authentication tokens") with the existing `auth.token_invalid` (401) — no new code — closing the replay of a 300-second relay connection-token to an endpoint. (Full positive `aud` allowlisting — each endpoint asserting its OWN named audience — additionally needs a canonical endpoint-auth audience VALUE that the token-mint spec owns (Spec-018 / Plan-018); the reject-relay-connect floor is the I-008-4 replay guard R2 enforces now, independent of that value.) The implicit-assertion defaults to none for the R2 endpoint surfaces (which bind no channel); the R3 relay WSS handshake (T-008r-3-5) supplies the Durable Object's `sessionId` as the implicit assertion, enforcing the [Spec-008 §Relay Negotiation connection-token claim shape](../specs/008-control-plane-relay-and-session-join.md#relay-negotiation). These additions — the implicit-assertion parameter, the R3 `aud == relay-connect` requirement, and the R2 `aud != relay-connect` rejection — are back-compatible with a correctly-minted endpoint-auth access token (which never carries the relay-connect audience); only a mis-audienced or replayed relay-connect token is now refused at R2. Author as an injected class/factory — `createPasetoValidationMiddleware(issuerKeySource): PasetoValidationMiddleware` — that takes the issuer key as a constructor dependency (NEVER generates or hardcodes it), exactly mirroring how `invite-service.ts` injects its `KeyRing` rather than instantiating crypto inline (`packages/control-plane/src/invites/invite-service.ts#InviteService`, the KeyRing dependency-injection notes). On failure, return the EXISTING error codes (do NOT mint new ones): `auth.token_expired` (401) / `auth.token_invalid` (401) for endpoint dispatch, `relay.authentication_failed` (401) for the WSS handshake — all present at `docs/architecture/contracts/error-contracts.md §Auth` + `docs/architecture/contracts/error-contracts.md §Relay`. **Consumes** (intra-Tier-5 Precondition, NOT in-DAG — §5 ordering edge F-008r-C recorded at swap, CP-008-4): Plan-018's PASETO issuer key + the `AuthenticatedIdentityContext` producer shape that resolves `ControlPlaneSessionJoinRequest.identityHandle` → a registered participant identity. **Scope guards:** (a) R1 authors token VALIDATION + the resolved-identity handoff only; the canonical `AuthenticatedIdentityContext` shape is Plan-018-owned (ratified D-018-1) — `Consumes:` it, do NOT author its shape. (b) This is the SAME stable mount surface Plan-021 EXTENDS at Tier 6 for `rateLimitProcedure` (CP-008-5) — R1 does **NOT** author rate-limiting (the F-008r-B mis-tiering correction); keep the mount surface stable. (c) R2/R3 cite this task as "R1 PASETO-validation middleware, I-008-4". **Host-wiring leg (2026-08-12, BL-141 / [ADR-025](../decisions/025-runtime-node-control-plane-caller-authorization.md) — Codex PR #327 round 2):** wire the middleware into `host.ts`'s `productionFetchHandler`, replacing the throwing `resolveCurrentParticipantId` injection with a resolver deriving the canonical `ParticipantId` from the verified token's `sub` claim, so every already-mounted procedure consuming that seam — the five `runtimenode.*` procedures I-008-4 gates, and the invite router once [Plan-002](./002-invite-membership-and-presence.md) T2.7 mounts it — goes production-live on the verified principal through one owned swap; without this leg the middleware would exist while those routes kept throwing the Tier-5 deferral error. Tests: T-008r-1-T11 a well-formed in-validity-window `v4.public` token signed by the injected issuer key passes and yields the resolved identity handle; T-008r-1-T12 absent / malformed-header / wrong-issuer-key tokens each refuse with `auth.token_invalid` before any downstream dispatch; T-008r-1-T12b a validly-signed, unexpired token carrying `aud == relay-connect` presented to an R2 endpoint surface is refused with `auth.token_invalid` before any dispatch (the cross-JWT-confusion guard — the R2 mirror of the R3 `aud != relay-connect` refusal at T-008r-3-T13b); T-008r-1-T13 an expired token refuses with `auth.token_expired`; T-008r-1-T14 the WSS-handshake refusal path surfaces `relay.authentication_failed` (proves the single middleware serves both the endpoint and the `Sec-WebSocket-Protocol: paseto-v4, <base64url(token)>` handshake surfaces per I-008-4); T-008r-1-T11b the verify core, given an implicit assertion, accepts only when the caller supplies the byte-identical assertion and refuses on a mismatch (the channel-binding substrate the R3 WSS handshake uses to reject a cross-session connection-token replay); T-008r-1-T14b with the middleware wired into the host, a valid endpoint-auth token reaching a `runtimenode.*` procedure resolves `resolveCurrentParticipantId` to the token's verified `sub` and the service receives it as `callerParticipantId` (the ADR-025 D2 handoff, asserted end-to-end at the host tier); T-008r-1-T14c an absent or invalid token on each of the five `runtimenode.*` routes refuses with `auth.token_invalid` before any service dispatch (per-route I-008-4 coverage for the runtime-node rows).

### Phase R2 — Membership-verified Join + Presence Fan-out + Invite Handoff

**Goal:** Implement `session-join-service.ts` (membership verification, invite-acceptance handoff to Plan-002, PASETO-gated per the R1 middleware) + the hosted-relay presence fan-out sibling under `presence/` + reconnect re-association. Honors I-008-4 (PASETO every endpoint), I-008-9 (reconnect re-associates, never duplicates), I-008-11 (join separable from relay).

**Spec-008 AC coverage:** AC1 (authenticated join without restarting active runs); AC2 (reconnect / path-switch creates no second identity); AC3 (response-shape half — control plane is not the execution authority, I-008-10 / T-008r-2-7; the relay-not-execution-authority half lands in R3).

**Consumes:** R1 contracts + PASETO middleware; Plan-002 `invite-service.ts` + `presence/` (CP-008-6 / CP-008-7, Tier 2); Plan-018 PASETO issuer + `AuthenticatedIdentityContext` (CP-008-4); Plan-001 `session-directory-service.ts` (I-001-1 lock-ordering inherited).

**Target files:** `packages/control-plane/src/sessions/session-join-service.ts` (CREATE); `packages/control-plane/src/presence/` (EXTEND — sibling fan-out file).

### Phase R3 — Relay Broker (Zero-Knowledge Admission + Opaque Forwarding)

**Goal:** Implement `relay-broker-service.ts` as the **zero-knowledge** broker — it performs **no message encrypt/decrypt** (I-008-5; the pairwise ECDH/HKDF/AEAD is participant-side, R4). It: (1) admits `SessionKeyBundle`s — `@noble/curves` `ed25519.verify` over `session_id ‖ ephemeral_x25519_public` (`Spec-008 §Relay Encryption`, `Spec-008 §Relay Negotiation`), reject-unregistered-identity (195), reject-reused-ephemeral across sessions (183) (I-008-7 a/b/c); (2) serves peers' admitted bundles so each participant can run client-side ECDH; (3) forwards opaque `{sender_ephemeral, recipient_id, seq, nonce, ciphertext+tag}` envelopes by `recipient_id` **without inspecting or persisting plaintext** (I-008-5; `sender_ephemeral`/`seq`/`nonce`/`ciphertext+tag` opaque to the broker), discarding all bundles on session close (I-008-6, broker side); (4) authenticates the WSS handshake with PASETO v4.public (I-008-4); (5) serves `RelayNegotiation` (connection-token TTL 300s) + relay-**transport** key-resume on reconnect (Spec-008 lifecycle step 6 — distinct from R2's participant-identity re-association); (6) shards relay Durable Objects at ≤ 25 connections/DO with WebSocket Hibernation (`Spec-008 §Relay Encryption` + `Plan-008 §Implementation Steps`); (7) consumes the 1-byte wire message-type discriminator — the `RelayFrameType` wire enum declared in contracts at R1 (T-008r-1-3) per the NS-19 hoist (§Decision Log 2026-06-15) — dispatching forwardable ciphertext envelopes from broker-handled control frames (T-008r-3-9). Writes `session_directory` + `relay_connections`.

**Spec-008 AC coverage:** AC2 (direct ↔ relay **transport** switching at the broker, no second identity — the transport half; the client half is R4); AC3 (control plane never the execution authority, I-008-10).

**Consumes:** R1 contracts + `SessionKeyBundle` type + wire-frame codec + PASETO middleware; `@noble/curves` `ed25519.verify` (bundle admission only — **not** the AEAD suite, which is R4); Plan-018 issuer; Plan-001 `session_directory` FK on `sessions`.

**Target files:** `packages/control-plane/src/relay/relay-broker-service.ts` (CREATE); `session_directory` / `relay_connections` writes (via R1 migration).

- **T-008r-3-1** (Files: `packages/control-plane/src/relay/relay-broker-service.ts` (CREATE); Verifies invariant: I-008-7 (a/b/c), I-008-5; Spec coverage: `Spec-008 §Relay Negotiation` (`SessionKeyBundle` requirements, 176-183) + §Trust Properties (195)) — Implement the `RelayBrokerService` class (constructor-injected `Querier`, a `RegisteredIdentityResolver` backed by Plan-018's `participant_identity_keys` roster read — the CP-018-13 consumer-(c) leg, trued 2026-08-15 at the NS-62 pass: resolving `ed25519_identity_public` → `ParticipantId` is a key-roster read over that store, not an auth-context read; the caller-equality comparison below separately reads the posting connection's Plan-018 `AuthenticatedIdentityContext` — and a `SeenEphemeralKeyRegistry` over the ratified durable `relay_seen_ephemeral_keys` store — OD-3-2 / OD-008r-2) with the `SessionKeyBundle` admission method: verify the Ed25519 signature over `session_id ‖ ephemeral_x25519_public` via a **direct `@noble/curves` `ed25519.verify(signature, message, ed25519_identity_public, { zip215: false })`** (I-008-7a, `Spec-008 §Relay Encryption` + `Spec-008 §Relay Negotiation` — NOT the `crypto-paseto` v4.public suite, NOT any AEAD). **`{ zip215: false }` is load-bearing and MUST be passed literally at this call site**: noble defaults its `ed25519` wrapper to `zip215: true` — the consensus-friendly ZIP-215 decode rules, which **skip the small-order public-key rejection** — so against a small-order `ed25519_identity_public` the `[8][k]A` term drops out of the cofactored verification equation and one `(R, S)` pair verifies for _every_ message, a universal forgery of bundle admission. `zip215: false` selects strict RFC 8032 / FIPS 186-5 verification, which additionally rejects non-canonical point encodings (`y >= p`). Noble resolves both `{}` and `{ zip215: undefined }` back to the permissive wrapper default, so hoisting the option into a shared or spread options object silently reinstates the forgery **with no diff at the call site** — the same pin, with its regression guard, is already in evidence at `packages/crypto-paseto/src/v4-public.ts`; reject any bundle whose `ed25519_identity_public` is not bound to a registered participant identity, **and reject any bundle whose resolved participant is not the caller's own authenticated identity** (I-008-7b, `Spec-008 §Trust Properties` — the `RegisteredIdentityResolver` maps `ed25519_identity_public` → a `ParticipantId` that MUST equal the posting connection's `AuthenticatedIdentityContext.participantId`; a registered-but-not-caller bundle is a replay of another participant's signed bundle and is rejected as a confused-deputy / impersonation attempt); reject any `ephemeral_x25519_public` already recorded as used **in a distinct session** (I-008-7c, `Spec-008 §Relay Negotiation` — durable `relay_seen_ephemeral_keys` store, OD-3-2 / OD-008r-2) — the registry check **reads the stored `session_id` and decides** (NOT a blind INSERT): a **distinct-session** record is rejected with `relay.bundle_rejected`, while a **same-session** re-presentation is an **idempotent admit only when the re-presenting participant is the one that first claimed the key** (the reconnect-resume path, `Spec-008 §Relay Connection Lifecycle`) — verified against the broker's **live per-session admission state** (which participant admitted each `ephemeral_x25519_public` this session, held in the in-memory bundle cache T-008r-3-4 discards on close), **failing closed** when that state is unavailable (the broker rejects rather than blind-admit; the client then re-mints a fresh ephemeral per `Spec-008 §Relay Connection Lifecycle`'s restart branch — the normal per-session path). A **different** authenticated participant re-presenting an already-admitted key — even within the same session — is rejected as a same-session peer-key hijack (the same I-008-7b caller-identity binding that guards the signed bundle), never idempotently admitted. The durable `relay_seen_ephemeral_keys` store is unchanged — it stays the cross-session `(key → first session_id)` backstop; the same-session original-claimant check is broker live-layer logic, not a column on that table. The broker performs NO ECDH/HKDF/AEAD — admission is signature-verification only (I-008-5; the pairwise crypto is R4). **Consumes:** R1 `SessionKeyBundleSchema` (32B ephemeral_x25519_public + 32B ed25519_identity_public + 64B signature, lengths enforced) from `packages/contracts/src/session-join.ts`; `@noble/curves` `ed25519.verify` (Tier-1-shipped, `ADR-010 §Decision`). On admission failure the broker raises `relay.bundle_rejected` (403; OD-3-1 / OD-008r-1, registered in `error-contracts.md` at swap; do NOT reuse `relay.authentication_failed`, which is handshake-scoped). Tests: T-008r-3-T1 valid bundle with a correct Ed25519 signature over `session_id ‖ ephemeral_x25519_public` is admitted; T-008r-3-T2 bundle with a tampered signature is rejected (I-008-7a); T-008r-3-T2b a bundle whose `ed25519_identity_public` is a **small-order point**, carrying the matching all-message forged `(R, S)` pair, is rejected — the strict-verification arm of the same I-008-7a signature check, and the only arm that detects a dropped `{ zip215: false }`; the case carries a **negative control** asserting that the identical `(signature, message, key)` triple verifies under a bare `ed25519.verify` at noble's permissive default, so the test cannot go green with the option removed (mirroring `packages/crypto-paseto/src/__tests__/ed25519-strict-verification.test.ts`); T-008r-3-T3 bundle whose `ed25519_identity_public` is not a registered participant is rejected (I-008-7b); T-008r-3-T4 an `ephemeral_x25519_public` reused from a prior distinct session is rejected (I-008-7c); T-008r-3-T4c the same `ephemeral_x25519_public` re-presented within the **same** session **by the original claimant** is idempotently admitted, not rejected (the SELECT-then-decide same-session carve-out, `Spec-008 §Relay Connection Lifecycle`); T-008r-3-T4d a same-session re-presentation of an already-admitted `ephemeral_x25519_public` by a **different** authenticated participant is rejected (the original-claimant binding — a same-session peer-key hijack, distinct from the cross-session T-008r-3-T4 and the different-signer T-008r-3-T3b); T-008r-3-T3b a bundle correctly signed by a registered participant **other than the authenticated caller** (a replayed peer bundle) is rejected on the caller-identity binding — distinct from T-008r-3-T3's unregistered-key rejection (the #1 confused-deputy guard, `Spec-008 §Trust Properties`).

### Phase R4 — Client Surfaces + Participant Relay Crypto (SDK + Desktop + CLI)

**Goal:** Two client-side surfaces. **(A) Participant pairwise-crypto + relay-transport module** at `packages/client-sdk/src/relay/` (CREATE) — the participant side of the zero-knowledge channel R3 brokers: generate the ephemeral X25519 keypair per session; sign its public half with an **injected** long-term Ed25519 identity signer (custody is ADR-021 CLI / Plan-023 WebAuthn-PRF, **not** owned here — CP-008-8); assemble + post the `SessionKeyBundle`; fetch + Ed25519-verify peers' bundles; X25519 ECDH + `HKDF-SHA256(shared, salt=session_id, info="ai-sidekicks/v1/pairwise", length=32)` per-pair derive (`Spec-008 §Relay Encryption`); XChaCha20-Poly1305 per-recipient encrypt with a fresh 24-byte nonce and AEAD AAD = `recipient_id ‖ per-sender monotonic seq`; decrypt + monotonic-seq replay rejection (I-008-8); zeroize the ephemeral secret + derived keys on session close (I-008-6); WSS relay connection (PASETO v4.public handshake via the R3 negotiation token, ping/pong 30s / dead-60s, reconnect-resume). This is the "pairwise encrypted relay payload channel" [Plan-027](./027-cross-node-dispatch-and-approval.md) (Tier 9) consumes; its round-trip + zeroization + HKDF + Ed25519-verify tests satisfy the Plan-008 Done Checklist (`Plan-008 §Done Checklist`). **(B) Join / presence / reconnect surfaces** — `sessionJoinClient.ts` (typed join / presence / relay-negotiation / reconnect-resume) + desktop `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/session-join/` + CLI `apps/cli/src/session-join/`, honoring I-008-9 (reconnect re-association) and I-008-11 (join succeeds; relay degradation does not fail the join — small replay-oriented join response, never a giant state dump, per `Spec-008 §Implementation Notes`). **Plus (campaign B12) the event-stream leg of reconnect:** the `ResumingEventClient` decorator (T-008r-4-13) that resumes the session event stream from `acknowledged ?? earliest` with gap detection and at-most-once apply (I-008-12) — identity re-association (R2), transport re-attach (R3), and timeline resume (B12) are the three distinct legs a full reconnect composes, and the T-008r-4-10 coordinator is the production composition point that drives all three. **Plus (2026-08-03 vision reconciliation) the outbound recipient set:** the same coordinator derives _who_ an event's envelopes are addressed to — the session's current recipient set, intersected with the channel's member set when the event binds to a membership-restricted channel (I-008-14(a), `Spec-008 §Per-Channel Recipient Scoping (V1)`) — over a forward-declared injected resolver (CP-008-12), since the per-recipient encrypt in Part A deliberately decides nothing about the set. **Plus (2026-08-11 restoring delta) Part C — the peer-history backfill engine** (T-008r-4-14, daemon-side at `packages/runtime-daemon/src/backfill/`): the possession-scoped serving selector and the verify-before-append joiner discharging I-008-13 and I-008-14(b) — the serving half of the confidentiality whose live half the coordinator's scoped fan-out holds — riding the same pairwise envelope as an application-level payload, with the `session_events.received_from_node_id` provenance migration and the CP-008-13 receive-append seam, bridged to the client-side coordinator over the four CP-008-14 `session.*` daemon JSON-RPC methods (round-2 fold: decryption is client-side where bundle custody lives, verification and append daemon-side, and without the named bridge the engines have no production caller).

**Spec-008 AC coverage:** AC1 (client joins a live session and reads the existing timeline before deciding whether to attach a runtime node); AC2 (client-side reconnect / path-switch — the participant half of the relay-transport switch); **AC4** (entitled-history recovery on join — I-008-13, T-008r-4-14, tasked 2026-08-11 by the restoring readiness-audit delta); **AC5, both halves** (an event scoped to a membership-restricted channel is sealed to that channel's members only — I-008-14(a), T-008r-4-10 — and never served on backfill outside its member set — I-008-14(b), T-008r-4-14); plus the client-terminated crypto properties I-008-6 (ephemeral zeroization) + I-008-8 (replay rejection). Campaign B12 adds the I-008-12 event-stream resume contract (timeline continuity across reconnect; no new AC).

**Consumes:** R1 contracts + `SessionKeyBundle` type + wire-frame codec; R3 broker `SessionKeyBundle`-admission + relay-WSS endpoints; `@noble/curves` X25519 + `@noble/ciphers` XChaCha20-Poly1305 (the full participant AEAD suite); an **injected** Ed25519 identity signer (custody = ADR-021 CLI / Plan-023 WebAuthn-PRF — CP-008-8; R4 owns the signing operation, never custody); Plan-023 Tier-1-Partial renderer substrate + `window.sidekicks` bridge; Plan-007 CLI scaffold + `@ai-sidekicks/client-sdk` transport (Tier-4 remainder); **Plan-006 Tier 4** — `EventClient` (T4.7) + T4.3 cursor codec/semantics + T4.5 replay-then-live handoff (CP-008-10; path-c lower-tier Precondition, campaign B12); an **injected** `ChannelRecipientScopeResolver` for the I-008-14(a) recipient-set derivation (CP-008-12 — R4 owns the scoping operation, never the channel record; the concrete provider is forward-declared to the plan that owns that record and is **not** an in-DAG dependency, the CP-008-8 pattern, so a `FixtureChannelRecipientScopeResolver` keeps R4 buildable and testable ahead of it); and — for Part C (T-008r-4-14) — **Plan-006 Tier 4's** production integrity verifier (T4.1 — its per-row origin-bytes signature check and its anchor-plus-stub checks, imported never re-implemented; the chain half stays out of scope at this boundary, per the task), the `runtimenode.signingkeyroster` read half (CP-008-11), the append + compaction seams with the Spec-006-governed conditional-member emission and stub-projection carriage (the CP-008-13 sanctioned seams), and **Plan-007's IPC registry + streaming substrate** for the four CP-008-14 `session.*` bridge handler registrations (the Tier-1 `session.*` root-extension class; `session.historyBackfillServe` streams over `streaming-primitive.ts`, the I-007-7 subscribe-init-ack shape).

**Target files:** `packages/client-sdk/src/relay/` (CREATE — participant pairwise-crypto + relay-WSS transport module); `packages/client-sdk/src/sessionJoinClient.ts` (CREATE); `packages/client-sdk/src/resumingEventClient.ts` (CREATE — campaign B12); `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/session-join/` (CREATE); `apps/cli/src/session-join/` (CREATE); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/backfill/` (CREATE — T-008r-4-14) + `packages/runtime-daemon/src/migrations/0NNN-received-row-provenance.ts` (CREATE) + `packages/runtime-daemon/src/session/migration-runner.ts` (EXTEND — same-commit registration) + `packages/contracts/src/session-join.ts` (EXTEND — HistoryBackfill schemas) + the three CP-008-13 seam edits in `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/` (T-008r-4-14) + `packages/runtime-daemon/src/ipc/handlers/` (EXTEND — the four CP-008-14 `session.*` bridge handler files, T-008r-4-14 + T-008r-4-10's inbound leg).

The constructor takes the injected signer + the `SessionId`. On construction it generates a **fresh ephemeral X25519 key pair per session** (not per message; `x25519.utils.randomSecretKey()` / public derive via `@noble/curves`, `Spec-008 §Relay Encryption`, `ADR-010 §Decision`) and retains the secret only in private class state. No bundle assembly, ECDH, or AEAD yet — those are T-008r-4-4..6 methods on this class. - **T-008r-4-4** (Files: `packages/client-sdk/src/relay/relay-crypto-session.ts` (EXTEND); Verifies invariant: I-008-7 (substrate — produces the signed bundle R3 admits; the client also re-verifies peers' fetched bundles, distinct from R3 control-plane admission), I-008-6; Spec coverage: `Spec-008 §Relay Negotiation` + `Spec-008 §Relay Connection Lifecycle` (step 3), substrate — supports AC2's client half) — Add `assembleSessionKeyBundle()` and `verifyPeerBundle(bundle)` methods. `assembleSessionKeyBundle()` signs `session_id ‖ ephemeral_x25519_public` via the injected signer and returns a `SessionKeyBundle` (consume `SessionKeyBundleSchema` from `packages/contracts/src/session-join.ts` — R1 T-008r-1-2; do **not** re-author the shape) with the 32-byte `ephemeralX25519Public`, 32-byte `ed25519IdentityPublic`, and 64-byte `signature` (`Spec-008 §Relay Negotiation`). `verifyPeerBundle(bundle)` is **client-side defense-in-depth re-verification**: `ed25519.verify(bundle.signature, session_id ‖ bundle.ephemeralX25519Public, bundle.ed25519IdentityPublic, { zip215: false })` via `@noble/curves` before that peer's key is used in ECDH — **distinct from R3's control-plane admission** (I-008-7 a/b/c is the broker's job; this is the participant re-checking what it fetched). **This is the highest-risk verify site in the plan, and `{ zip215: false }` MUST be passed literally at this call site.** The key it verifies against, `bundle.ed25519IdentityPublic`, is **attacker-supplied** — read straight off a fetched peer bundle, ahead of any control-plane identity binding (every other verify site in this plan checks a key the broker already bound to a registered participant) — so an attacker does not need to find a weak key, it simply **chooses a small-order one**. Under noble's default `zip215: true` the small-order public-key rejection is skipped, the `[8][k]A` term drops out of the cofactored verification equation, and a single `(R, S)` pair verifies for _every_ message: `verifyPeerBundle` would accept an arbitrary forged bundle and feed its `ephemeralX25519Public` directly into the T-008r-4-5 ECDH. `zip215: false` selects strict RFC 8032 / FIPS 186-5 verification, which additionally rejects non-canonical point encodings (`y >= p`). Noble resolves both `{}` and `{ zip215: undefined }` back to the permissive default, so the option must **not** be hoisted into a shared or spread options object — that reinstates the forgery **with no diff at the call site**. Reject a bad-signature peer bundle with `relay.bundle_signature_invalid` (ratified OD-008r-1, registered in `error-contracts.md` at swap). Tests: T-008r-4-T4 round-trip — assemble a bundle with the fixture signer, then `verifyPeerBundle` accepts it; T-008r-4-T5 (Ed25519-verify, Done-Checklist `Plan-008 §Done Checklist`) `verifyPeerBundle` rejects a bundle whose signature was produced over a different `session_id` or a tampered `ephemeralX25519Public`; T-008r-4-T5b `verifyPeerBundle` rejects a peer bundle whose `ed25519IdentityPublic` is a **small-order point** carrying the matching all-message forged `(R, S)` pair, and no pairwise key is derived from that bundle's `ephemeralX25519Public` — the attacker-supplied-key arm of the same client-side re-verification, and the only arm that detects a dropped `{ zip215: false }`; the case carries a **negative control** asserting that the identical `(signature, message, key)` triple verifies under a bare `ed25519.verify` at noble's permissive default, so the test cannot go green with the option removed.

- **T-008r-4-5** (Files: `packages/client-sdk/src/relay/relay-crypto-session.ts` (EXTEND); Verifies invariant: I-008-6; Spec coverage: `Spec-008 §Relay Encryption` (V1 cipher suite) + §Relay Connection Lifecycle (step 3), substrate — supports AC2's client half) — Add `derivePairwiseSessionKey(peerEphemeralX25519Public)`: X25519 ECDH (`x25519.getSharedSecret(ephemeralSecret, peerPublic)` via `@noble/curves`) then `HKDF-SHA256(sharedSecret, salt=session_id, info="ai-sidekicks/v1/pairwise", length=32)` (`hkdf` from `@noble/hashes`, `sha256`; RFC 5869, `Spec-008 §Relay Encryption` / `ADR-010 §Decision`) → a 32-byte symmetric key cached in the per-pair derived-keys map for the session lifetime (`Spec-008 §Relay Connection Lifecycle` step 3). The `session_id` HKDF salt is the `RelayNegotiationResponse` Session-ID field (`Spec-008 §Relay Negotiation`). Tests: T-008r-4-T6 (HKDF, Done-Checklist `Plan-008 §Done Checklist`) two `RelayCryptoSession` peers each derive the **same** 32-byte pairwise key from their mutual ECDH (ECDH symmetry + HKDF determinism), and assert the derived key equals an independently-computed `HKDF-SHA256(ecdh, salt=session_id, info="ai-sidekicks/v1/pairwise", 32)` reference vector.

- **T-008r-4-6** (Files: `packages/client-sdk/src/relay/relay-crypto-session.ts` (EXTEND); Verifies invariant: I-008-8, I-008-6; Spec coverage: `Spec-008 §Relay Encryption` (V1 cipher suite) + §Message Framing, substrate — supports AC2's client half) — Add `encryptForRecipient(recipientId, plaintext)` and `decryptFromSender(envelope)` (the recipient resolves the sender from the wire envelope, not a caller-supplied id). Encrypt: fresh **24-byte random nonce**, XChaCha20-Poly1305 (`xchacha20poly1305` from `@noble/ciphers`) under the cached pairwise key, AEAD **associated data = `recipient_id ‖ per-sender monotonic seq`** (the sender's outbound counter for that recipient, an 8-byte big-endian uint64 incremented per message — I-008-8, `Spec-008 §Relay Encryption`), **writing the `seq` into the envelope in the clear** and yielding a `{sender_ephemeral, recipient_id, seq, nonce, ciphertext+tag}` envelope (`Spec-008 §Relay Encryption`), where `sender_ephemeral` is **this session's own ephemeral X25519 public key** (the recipient's key-selection handle — `Spec-008 §Relay Encryption` + `Spec-008 §Message Framing`). Decrypt: **read `envelope.sender_ephemeral` and resolve the cached pairwise key keyed by that ephemeral public** (the per-pair derived-keys map from T-008r-4-5 is keyed by exactly this value — an O(1) lookup, no trial-decryption); sender authenticity is enforced by key-selection — the tag verifies **only** under the resolved sender's pairwise key (a two-party key, so a valid tag proves the peer sent it: the Olm/Signal pairwise property, **not** Megolm's non-authenticating group-key selection, which needs a separate Ed25519 signature), so a mislabeled `sender_ephemeral` selects the wrong key and fails the tag (`sender_ephemeral` is **not** AEAD-bound; the AAD stays `recipient_id ‖ seq` — the cleartext handle follows the Megolm session-id lookup idiom). Then **read the wire `seq`, reconstruct the associated data `recipient_id ‖ seq`**, verify the AEAD tag (which authenticates the `seq` — a tampered `seq` fails the tag), then **reject any frame whose `seq` is ≤ the last accepted sequence for that resolved sender** (monotonic-seq replay rejection; the per-sender counter is keyed by `sender_ephemeral`, I-008-8, `Spec-008 §Trust Properties`) with `relay.replay_rejected` (ratified OD-008r-1, registered in `error-contracts.md` at swap). The opaque envelope is the `[payload]` the R1 `encodeRelayFrame`/`decodeRelayFrame` codec wraps and the R3 broker forwards uninspected (I-008-5, broker side). Tests: T-008r-4-T7 (round-trip, Done-Checklist `Plan-008 §Done Checklist`) encrypt→decrypt resolving the sender from `envelope.sender_ephemeral` returns byte-identical plaintext and the AAD-bound `recipient_id`+seq match; T-008r-4-T8 (replay, I-008-8) a re-delivered frame at an already-accepted sequence is rejected, and an out-of-order (lower) sequence is rejected, while the next monotonic sequence is accepted; T-008r-4-T19 (seq integrity, I-008-8; re-assigned from the colliding `T9` at the campaign-B12 census re-derivation — see §Decision Log 2026-07-21) a frame whose wire `seq` is tampered (mutated after encryption) fails the AEAD tag on decrypt (the `seq` is authenticated as associated data, not merely transmitted); T-008r-4-T20 (sender-handle resolution, I-008-8; re-assigned from the colliding `T11`, same row) a frame whose `sender_ephemeral` is relabeled to a different peer's ephemeral public selects the wrong pairwise key and fails the AEAD tag — proving sender authenticity is enforced by key-selection, not by trusting the cleartext handle.

- **T-008r-4-7** (Files: `packages/client-sdk/src/relay/relay-crypto-session.ts` (EXTEND); Verifies invariant: I-008-6; Spec coverage: `Spec-008 §Relay Connection Lifecycle` (step 5), substrate — supports AC2's client half) — Add `zeroizeOnSessionClose()`: overwrite the ephemeral X25519 secret key **and** every cached derived pairwise session key in place with zero bytes, then drop the references, on graceful session close (`Spec-008 §Relay Connection Lifecycle` step 5 — "zeroes its ephemeral X25519 secret key and derived session keys in memory"). This is the I-008-6 forward-secrecy guarantee: compromise of the long-term Ed25519 identity key after close must not recover past session keys. Tests: T-008r-4-T9 (zeroization, Done-Checklist `Plan-008 §Done Checklist`) capture references to the ephemeral secret + a derived key, call `zeroizeOnSessionClose()`, and assert the underlying `Uint8Array` bytes are all zero (and that a subsequent encrypt/derive throws rather than operating on zeroed material).

- **T-008r-4-8** (Files: `packages/client-sdk/src/relay/relay-wss-transport.ts` (CREATE); Verifies invariant: I-008-11, I-008-4 (the client-side half — presents the token; the relay validates per R3); Spec coverage: `Spec-008 §Relay Connection Lifecycle` (steps 1-2, 6), substrate — supports AC2's client half) — Implement the `RelayWssTransport` **class**: open a WSS connection to the `RelayNegotiationResponse.relayEndpoint`, presenting the R3-issued `connectionToken` (short-lived PASETO v4.public, TTL 300s, `Spec-008 §Relay Negotiation`) in the WebSocket handshake via two `Sec-WebSocket-Protocol` subprotocol values — `paseto-v4, <base64url(connectionToken)>` (`Spec-008 §Relay Connection Lifecycle`, lifecycle step 2): value[0] `paseto-v4` names the scheme (echoed by the relay as the negotiated subprotocol), value[1] is the base64url-encoded token (browsers cannot set custom WS headers — the Kubernetes apiserver bearer-token-over-subprotocol idiom). The transport sends/receives the binary frames produced by the R1 `encodeRelayFrame`/`decodeRelayFrame` codec (consume from `packages/contracts/src/session-join.ts`; the 1-byte `RelayFrameType` wire enum is declared in contracts at R1 per the NS-19 audit hoist — **consume it, do not re-declare or assign numeric values**). Liveness: **ping every 30s, treat the connection dead after 60s with no pong** (`Spec-008 §Message Framing`). Reconnect re-establishes the **relay TRANSPORT** via a fresh negotiation token and resumes using the still-resident session keys if the ephemeral material survives (`Spec-008 §Relay Connection Lifecycle` step 6) — this is the transport-key-resume half, **distinct from R2's participant-IDENTITY re-association** (handled by `sessionJoinClient`, T-008r-4-10). I-008-11: a relay-setup or WSS failure surfaces as degraded connectivity, never a failed join. Tests: T-008r-4-T10 against a stub WSS server — handshake presents the two-value `paseto-v4, <base64url(connectionToken)>` subprotocol (assert the stub reads value[1], base64url-decodes to the original token, and the client accepts the echoed `paseto-v4`); ping/pong liveness marks the socket dead after the 60s no-pong window (faked timer); a reconnect re-presents a fresh token and re-attaches the transport.

- **T-008r-4-10** (Files: `packages/client-sdk/src/relay/relay-session-coordinator.ts` (CREATE); Verifies invariant: I-008-11, I-008-9, I-008-12 (composition point), I-008-14(a) (the live-path half); Spec coverage: Spec-008 AC2 + AC5 (live half) + §Relay Connection Lifecycle + §Per-Channel Recipient Scoping (V1)) — Implement the `RelaySessionCoordinator` **class** that composes the legs into the lifecycle the brief keeps distinct: it drives `SessionJoinClient.negotiateRelay` → constructs the `RelayWssTransport` (T-008r-4-8) with the returned token → constructs the `RelayCryptoSession` (T-008r-4-3) with the injected signer → posts the assembled `SessionKeyBundle` via the join client → fetches + `verifyPeerBundle`s peers → derives pairwise keys — **and (campaign B12) owns the timeline leg**: it constructs the T-008r-4-13 `ResumingEventClient` (`createResumingEventClient` over the daemon `EventClient` + injected `readSession`) at session attach and drives `resumeAndSubscribe`, the production composition point for I-008-12. Its `onProjectionReset` implements the T-008r-4-13 reset-settlement contract: it resolves only after durably recording the forced-earliest rebuild in the same commit domain as its projection clear, and while that marker stands its injected `readSession` strips `acknowledged` from the response, clearing the marker once the rebuilt projection durably re-acks. A rejection carrying the I-008-12(d) unsupported-responder refusal is surfaced as degraded timeline state (the I-008-11 posture) and re-probed on the next reconnect, never hot-loop re-invoked. On reconnect it calls `SessionJoinClient.resumeAfterReconnect` (R2 **identity** re-association, I-008-9), re-establishes the `RelayWssTransport` (R3 **transport** key-resume), **and re-invokes `resumeAndSubscribe` (B12 timeline resume), serialized to at most one live resume cycle per session: re-invocation happens when the cycle has terminated (a rejected `done` — the coordinator owns the retry policy the decorator deliberately does not), and a reconnect-driven re-drive of a still-live cycle first closes the prior handle and awaits its `done` settlement before re-invoking (two concurrent cycles would interleave resets and applies against one projection; a healthy cycle rides the daemon transport and survives a relay blip undisturbed, so close-then-await-then-reopen is the only safe redundant re-drive)** as three separate steps — never conflating them — so a surface riding the coordinator can never complete a reconnect with a stale timeline. **It also owns the outbound recipient set (I-008-14(a)).** Publishing a session event, the coordinator derives _which_ recipients get an envelope before calling `RelayCryptoSession.encryptForRecipient` per recipient (T-008r-4-6 encrypts for one recipient and deliberately decides nothing about the set): the default is the session's current recipient set, and for an event whose `payload` carries a `channelId` binding that resolves to a **membership-restricted** channel the set is that channel's member set **intersected** with the current recipient set (`Spec-008 §Per-Channel Recipient Scoping (V1)`). Resolution goes through a constructor-**injected** `ChannelRecipientScopeResolver` — a single total async operation `resolveChannelRecipientScope(channelId)` returning `{ kind: "unrestricted" }` or `{ kind: "restricted"; members: readonly ParticipantId[] }` and **rejecting** on a binding it cannot resolve — because the channel record (kind + immutable pair) is Tier-6 [Plan-016](./016-multi-agent-channels-and-orchestration.md)-owned: **author the interface plus a `FixtureChannelRecipientScopeResolver` test double only**; do **not** author the channel record, its projection, or the concrete provider wiring (forward-declared, CP-008-12 — the CP-008-8 injected-signer shape). Keep the predicate **structural**: branch on the resolved scope of a payload-carried `channelId`, never on an enumerated set of event `type` values (the envelope's top-level member set is closed, so the binding is always a payload member, and a type list fails open on the first taxonomy addition it misses). An unresolvable binding **fails closed** — the publication is refused and no envelope is emitted; it MUST NOT fall back to the unscoped session set, and it mints no wire error code (it is a local producer-invariant violation, surfaced like any other local publish failure). The resolver itself is a **required** constructor parameter and construction **without** one is refused at the constructor — never defaulted to an admit-all resolver, which here would mean full-session fan-out for exactly the channels this derivation protects (CP-008-12). An event carrying no `channelId`, or one bound to an unrestricted channel — including the bootstrap `main` channel, which has no channel row and resolves unrestricted by construction — takes the full recipient set unchanged. Comments and error text on this path describe the property as **daemon** scoping, never as agent exclusion (I-008-14(c)). **It also owns inbound dispatch (2026-08-11 round-2 fold, CP-008-14):** the coordinator is the surface where relay ciphertext becomes plaintext (bundle custody is client-side), and verification + append are daemon authority, so each decrypted application payload is forwarded over the daemon JSON-RPC bridge by kind — a live relayed session event (the origin triple: origin `NodeId`, canonical bytes, origin signature) to `session.relayEventDeliver`, a `HistoryBackfillRequest` from a peer to `session.historyBackfillServe` (whose streamed chunks the coordinator seals and publishes in ordinal order), and a `HistoryBackfillChunk` to `session.historyBackfillDeliver`; at its own relay admission it opens backfill via `session.historyBackfillStart` and seals the returned request to each other current member. The coordinator never verifies, never appends, and never inspects entry contents — decrypt, classify, forward; the daemon engines own everything evidential (methods and semantics at the api-payload §History-Backfill Bridge Method Registry; engine behavior and tests on T-008r-4-14, the bridge integration arm `T-008r-4-T49`). On graceful close it invokes `RelayCryptoSession.zeroizeOnSessionClose()` (I-008-6) and `close()` on the resume handle. I-008-11: if relay negotiation/WSS fails, the coordinator leaves the prior `SessionJoinClient.join` membership valid and reports degraded connectivity. Tests: T-008r-4-T13 (AC2, I-008-9, I-008-12) a simulated reconnect drives all three legs as distinct operations — the R2 identity-resume call, a fresh R3 transport re-attach, and a `resumeAndSubscribe` re-invocation on the composed `ResumingEventClient` that first closes the prior handle and awaits its `done` settlement (at most one live cycle — the serialization arm) — and the timeline projection converges with redelivered overlap deduped (the three-leg integration arm), with the coordinator's `onProjectionReset` on a forced reset stripping `acknowledged` from the injected `readSession` until the rebuilt projection re-acks (the forced-earliest marker arm); T-008r-4-T14 (I-008-11) a forced relay-negotiation failure leaves the membership intact and surfaces degraded-connectivity state rather than throwing a join failure; T-008r-4-T30 (I-008-14a, AC5 live half) in a session whose current recipient set holds four peers, publishing an event whose `payload.channelId` resolves through the fixture resolver to a **restricted** two-member scope emits envelopes addressed to exactly those two `recipient_id`s and to no other peer — driving the **production** derivation, not a re-implemented predicate — while the same coordinator publishing an event that carries no `channelId` and one bound to an **unrestricted** channel each emit envelopes to all four (the firing negative control: a derivation stuck at narrow-everything or narrow-nothing fails one arm or the other); T-008r-4-T31 (I-008-14a) a `payload.channelId` the injected resolver rejects as unresolvable **refuses the publication and emits zero envelopes** — asserted against the encrypt spy, so a fallback to the unscoped four-peer set is caught rather than passing as "some envelopes were sent"; T-008r-4-T32 (I-008-14a, CP-008-12) constructing the coordinator with **no** `ChannelRecipientScopeResolver` bound **throws at construction** rather than yielding a publishable coordinator — the enforcement point for CP-008-12's build-incomplete rule, paired with a positive control that the same construction with the fixture bound succeeds and publishes, since every other arm here binds a resolver and would therefore pass unchanged against an admit-all default.

Consume the Plan-023 Tier-1-Partial renderer substrate (`main.tsx` shell + bridge) already on disk; do \*\*not\*\* re-create the substrate. Tests: T-008r-4-T15 (AC1) the view renders the joined-session timeline after a bridge-mocked join resolves; T-008r-4-T16 (I-008-11) a degraded relay state renders the degraded indicator while the joined membership stays rendered.

- **T-008r-4-13** (Files: `packages/client-sdk/src/resumingEventClient.ts` (CREATE) + `packages/client-sdk/src/index.ts` (EXTEND — append `export * from "./resumingEventClient.js"`, the [cross-plan-dependencies.md §2 `packages/client-sdk/src/` row](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#2-package-path-ownership-map) barrel-line seam); Verifies invariant: I-008-12, I-008-9 (the event-stream leg of reconnect — identity re-association is T-008r-4-9's, transport re-attach T-008r-4-8's; this task restores the timeline); Spec coverage: `Spec-008 §Default Behavior` (join returns live replay cursors) + `Spec-008 §Client SDK Implementation Guidance` (SSE `Last-Event-ID` resumption)) — **Campaign B12** ([design §4 W2 B12 row](../superpowers/specs/2026-07-01-capability-enhancements-design.md)): Implement the **`ResumingEventClient` decorator**: `createResumingEventClient(eventClient: EventClient, readSession: (sessionId: SessionId) => Promise<SessionReadResponse>): ResumingEventClient`, where `interface ResumingEventClient extends EventClient` (delegating `readAfterCursor` / `readWindow` / `subscribe` unchanged) adds one composite entry point `resumeAndSubscribe(params: { sessionId: SessionId; onEvent: (envelope: EventEnvelope) => void | Promise<void>; onProjectionReset: () => void | Promise<void> }): Promise<{ close(): void; done: Promise<void> }>` (the promise resolves once the cycle is armed — subscription open, drain begun — so `close()` is available during a long catch-up; both callbacks are promise-aware and each invocation is awaited to settlement before the cycle proceeds) — the catch-up-subscription consumer that operationalizes [Plan-006 T4.3](./006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#t43--replay-servicets-for-eventreadaftercursor--eventreadwindow)'s consumer rule (I-008-12). **(1) Floor selection:** `readSession(sessionId)` → `timelineCursors { earliest?, latest, acknowledged? }`; floor = `acknowledged ?? earliest`; the gap predicate `decodeEventCursor(acknowledged) < decodeEventCursor(earliest)` ⇒ events lost below the surviving floor ⇒ invoke `onProjectionReset()` exactly once and re-floor to `earliest`; an unresolvable `acknowledged` (`decodeEventCursor` raises the typed `event.cursor_unresolvable`) takes the same reset path; a clean floor (`decode(acknowledged) === decode(earliest)`) is NOT a gap (T4.3's no-false-resync rule); `earliest` absent (pre-widening responder) ⇒ cannot gap-detect — and cannot resume through this surface at all: a responder predating the T4.3 `earliest` widening equally predates T4.5's subscription and T4.7's `event.*` entry points (they land together in Plan-006 Phase 4), so the conservative cursorless full replay T4.3 sketches would method-not-found on the very calls it needs — the branch REFUSES before any destruction or subsequent replay/subscribe wire call (the already-resolved `readSession` snapshot is the cycle's sole wire traffic): no `onProjectionReset`, no `subscribe`, no decode of the responder's `latest` (which may still carry the legacy opaque event-id form the T4.7 correction retired), the arming promise rejecting with the decorator's typed SDK-local unsupported-responder error (no wire error code minted — no subsequent replay/subscribe wire call is made), the projection and `acknowledged` floor left intact; the caller surfaces degraded timeline state and a later re-invocation re-probes (the daemon may have upgraded). **(2) Subscribe-before-drain (bounded; the skew branch refuses in step 1 and never reaches this step):** open `eventClient.subscribe({ sessionId, afterCursor: snapshot.latest })` FIRST — positioned at the same `readSession` snapshot's `latest`, NOT at the floor, so its replay half ([Plan-006 T4.5](./006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#t45--eventsubscription-ssejson-rpc-notification-stream) replay-then-live, I-006-4-05) covers only the post-snapshot tail and the catch-up buffer holds live-tail traffic arriving during the drain, bounded by append rate × drain duration, never O(retained-history) (a floor-positioned subscribe would double-replay the whole retained span into memory) — and buffer its deliveries; then drain `eventClient.readAfterCursor` pages from the floor, **stopping at the captured snapshot's `latest`** — the loop ends when a page reaches `decodeEventCursor(latest)`, or earlier when `hasMore` exhausts on a quiet log, never by chasing the moving head, so a writer appending as fast as pages are consumed cannot extend the drain or indefinitely defer the live switch (rows beyond `latest` in the final page apply normally; the mark dedupes their buffered subscription copies — a ≤ one-page overlap, the bounded seam), and an `hasMore: false` exhaustion ending **below** `decodeEventCursor(latest)` — the empty or short drain a total-purge race leaves — is itself a discontinuity: the snapshot's `latest` witnesses that a row existed at that position, so a drain falling short of it proves rows were deleted after the floor check, and it takes the step-4 reset path (a clean floor-equals-latest snapshot exhausts AT `latest` and never false-resets) — applying each envelope through `onEvent` (awaited to settlement) and advancing the high-water-mark to its `sequence` only after that settlement, with **every applied sequence contiguity-checked** against the floor-initialized expected-next, drained pages and live deliveries alike (Plan-006's surviving log is dense — compaction stubs rows in place and gap-inverts, so a non-contiguous drained page means a retention pass deleted rows between the floor check and the read, and it takes the step-4 reset path exactly like a live gap); then switch to the subscription — flush the buffered deliveries in order, then continue live — applying only envelopes with `sequence >` high-water-mark. Coverage is exact by construction: the drain owns `(floor, latest]`, the subscription owns everything after `latest` with no append window between them (subscribe-first ordering + replay-then-live from `latest`). **(3) At-most-once, settlement-gated apply:** every envelope applies through `onEvent`, is awaited to settlement, and only then advances the high-water-mark; a redelivered sequence (at-least-once delivery across reconnects — SSE `Last-Event-ID` floors replay at the last-delivered cursor, so redelivery of already-applied events is the expected steady state) is discarded, never re-applied; a rejected apply is terminal — the mark does not advance, the cycle tears down, and the failure surfaces via `done` (step 5) — so a delayed or failed apply is never overtaken by later events and never falsely marked applied (the at-most-once guarantee is epoch-scoped per I-008-12(c) — a settled `onProjectionReset` clears the projection and begins a new epoch, so the step-4 re-drain re-applies the pre-gap prefix into the new epoch rather than violating the guarantee; across invocations the caller's persisted `acknowledged` floor governs — events applied-but-unacknowledged when a cycle dies re-deliver on the next invocation, the deliberate ack-cadence-bounded residual — and the decorator persists no second position, keeping the ack floor the single durable authority). **(4) Gap reset (drained or live):** a sequence discontinuity caught by the step-2 contiguity check — a non-contiguous drained page (the retention check-to-read race surfaces here) or a non-monotonic live delivery (the decorator's own check is this consumer's live-gap detector, with the offending envelope in hand: [Plan-006 T4.7](./006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#t47--client-sdk-eventclientts-typed-entry-points)'s iterator-side monotonicity rejection is untyped on the `LocalSubscriptionConsumer.next()` channel — indistinguishable from a transport failure to a compose-only consumer — so any iterator rejection is step-5 terminal, never a reset trigger, and a gap the iterator rejects before yielding the envelope still converges to safety: the cycle terminates, the coordinator re-invokes, and the fresh cycle's floor/contiguity/witness checks re-derive the state from the durable log, healing a mere delivery artifact by re-drain with no reset while genuine loss still resets) — triggers, in order: `onProjectionReset()` awaited to settlement, high-water-mark reinitialization (atomic with the reset with respect to application — no envelope applies between them; a stale mark would discard the entire re-drained prefix as already-applied), then re-resume from a fresh `readSession`'s `earliest` + re-drain — the reset invalidates the `acknowledged` floor (the re-fetch exists to pick up a moved surviving floor, never to re-trust `acknowledged` over a now-empty projection), so the third of T4.3's three converging failure modes resolves, like the other two, to the one reset-and-resume-from-`earliest` path. **Reset settlement binds the caller's floor:** `onProjectionReset` MUST NOT resolve until the caller has durably recorded the rebuild in the same commit domain as its projection clear — including invalidating its `acknowledged` floor, since an ack may never exceed the durably-applied projection; no ack-write wire op exists to lower the daemon-held cursor, so the caller honors the recorded rebuild through the **injected `readSession` seam**: while its forced-earliest marker stands it strips `acknowledged` from the injected response (the decorator then floors at `earliest` by its ordinary step-1 rule), clearing the marker only once the rebuilt projection durably re-acks — so a cycle that dies between reset settlement and re-drain completion re-floors at `earliest` on the next invocation, never at the stale ack past the cleared prefix. This binds **every** reset path — the step-1 floor-selection resets ((b)'s gap predicate, the unresolvable `acknowledged`) exactly as the step-4 discontinuity resets ((d)'s skew branch resets nothing — it refuses): the predicate and unresolvable paths would re-fire on re-invocation anyway, so the marker's load-bearing case is the cycle that dies between reset settlement and re-drain completion. **(5) Terminal-failure surface:** `done` resolves ONLY on locally-requested `close()` — a closing flag set before the underlying subscription is cancelled is the discriminand — and rejects on any terminal failure: the live consumer loop erroring after catch-up (the `LocalSubscriptionConsumer.next()` rejection path — including T4.7's own monotonicity rejection, which is untyped on that channel and therefore terminal here, never a reset trigger per step 4), an **unsolicited stream end** (the iterator completing without the local closing flag — Plan-007's consumer contract carries a normal completion path, e.g. a daemon-side subscription cancel, and a normally-ending stream nobody asked to end is a dead timeline, not a success), a rejected `onEvent`/`onProjectionReset` settlement, or a failed re-resume — so a post-resolution stream death is observable, never a silently stopped projection. The decorator performs no automatic transport reconnect: re-invoking `resumeAndSubscribe` IS the reconnect path — one live cycle at a time: the caller closes and awaits the prior handle's `done` before re-invoking (T-008r-4-10's serialization rule) — correct by construction (the floor re-selects from the caller's persisted `acknowledged`; redelivered overlap is bounded by ack cadence under the at-least-once contract), and retry/backoff policy stays with the application per the SDK marshal-don't-derive posture. **Composition point (production caller):** the T-008r-4-10 `RelaySessionCoordinator` composes this decorator as the reconnect's third leg — it constructs `createResumingEventClient` at session attach, drives `resumeAndSubscribe`, and re-invokes it on a rejected `done` and on reconnect — always closing the prior handle and awaiting its `done` settlement first, at most one live cycle per session (the coordinator owns the retry policy the decorator deliberately does not) — so the desktop/CLI surfaces riding the coordinator inherit a live timeline on reconnect, never a stale one (the capability is wired, not shelf-ware; three-leg integration arm on T-008r-4-T13). **Scope guards:** (a) compose-only — consumes Plan-006's `EventClient` + cursor codec + `timelineCursors.earliest`; MUST NOT edit `eventClient.ts` (CP-008-10; the §2 sole-in-place-edit rule). (b) **Disjoint from [BL-144](../backlog.md):** the server-side `event.subscribe` concurrency cap (5 concurrent/participant, [Spec-021 §Canonical Endpoint Group Registry](../specs/021-rate-limiting-policy.md#canonical-endpoint-group-registry)) is enforced at the SSE subscription registry by the substrate owner — this task authors NO cap logic and NO subscription counting. (c) Transport: the composite rides the daemon `EventClient` (event reads are daemon-side per [ADR-017](../decisions/017-shared-event-sourcing-scope.md)); the SSE `Last-Event-ID` mechanics cited here are the delivery-semantics rationale (at-least-once), not a control-plane read path. **Consumes:** Plan-006 T4.7 `EventClient` + `createDaemonEventClient`; Plan-006 T4.3 cursor codec + `timelineCursors.earliest` + `event.cursor_unresolvable`; Plan-006 T4.5 replay-then-live handoff (I-006-4-05); Plan-001 T5.1 daemon `session.read` (the injected `readSession`) — all path-c lower-tier Preconditions (CP-008-10). Tests (nine, per the design §4 B12 row): T-008r-4-T21 (I-008-12a) acknowledged-present happy path — floor = `acknowledged`, no reset, drain + live applied in sequence order; T-008r-4-T22 (I-008-12a) acknowledged-absent first connect — floor = `earliest`, no reset; T-008r-4-T23 (I-008-12b) gap predicate fires — `decode(acknowledged) < decode(earliest)` ⇒ exactly one `onProjectionReset` + resume from `earliest`; T-008r-4-T24 (I-008-12b) clean-floor equality — `acknowledged == earliest == encodeEventCursor(N-1)` ⇒ NO reset (no-false-resync guard); T-008r-4-T25 (I-008-12b) unresolvable acknowledged — decode raises `event.cursor_unresolvable` ⇒ reset path, resume from `earliest`; T-008r-4-T26 (I-008-12d) earliest-absent version skew ⇒ refusal before destruction — the arming promise rejects with the SDK-local unsupported-responder error, `onProjectionReset` is NEVER invoked, no subsequent replay/subscribe wire call is issued, the gap predicate is never evaluated, and the responder's legacy opaque `latest` (an event-id-form value) is never decoded — with the projection and `acknowledged` floor intact after the rejection; T-008r-4-T27 (I-008-12c) subscribe-before-drain race closure + buffer bound — an envelope appended after the `readSession` snapshot and before the live switch-over is applied exactly once (no loss, no duplicate — the `latest`-positioned subscription's replay half owns the post-snapshot tail), and the catch-up buffer holds only post-snapshot deliveries, never the retained history, while a writer appending as fast as pages are consumed cannot extend the drain — it stops at the captured `latest` and the live switch occurs (bounded-drain arm); T-008r-4-T28 (I-008-12c) settlement-gated high-water-mark dedupe + terminal surface — the subscription's replay half redelivers the drain's tail overlap beyond the snapshot `latest` and a reconnect redelivers applied sequences, each applying exactly once with the mark advancing only after each (async) `onEvent` settles — a slow apply is never overtaken; a rejected apply advances no mark and rejects `done`, while graceful `close()` resolves it, and an untyped iterator rejection (a simulated transport failure on the `next()` channel — the same channel T4.7's own monotonicity rejection rides) rejects `done` WITHOUT invoking `onProjectionReset` (terminal, never destructive — the channel-discrimination rule), and an unsolicited stream end (the subscription iterator completes with no local `close()` — a simulated daemon-side cancel) likewise rejects `done` rather than resolving it (the locally-requested-shutdown flag is the discriminand); T-008r-4-T29 (I-008-12b) gap reset, drained and live — a non-monotonic live envelope delivered by `subscribe` (caught by the decorator's own contiguity check pre-apply), separately a non-contiguous drained page (retention advanced `earliest` between the `readSession` floor check and the first read — the check-to-read race), and separately a total-purge empty/short drain (`hasMore: false` exhaustion ending below the snapshot `latest` — the witness rule), each ⇒ `onProjectionReset` + resume from the fresh `readSession`'s `earliest`, asserting the post-reset floor is `earliest` even while `acknowledged` is still populated AND that the high-water-mark was reinitialized with the reset — the re-drained pre-gap prefix re-applies into the new epoch rather than being discarded as already-applied.
