the T-008r-4-10 coordinator is the relay carriage, and the decrypted request reaches this engine over the CP-008-14 `session.historyBackfillServe` bridge method carrying the pairwise-authenticated requester `ParticipantId` — round-2 fold), select the requester's entitled set from the local `session_events` log under the **possession bound** — `received_from_node_id IS NULL` (origin-authored rows, including a predecessor `NodeId`'s rows where the member's store was carried forward, served as transport under the origin's roster key — **each `NULL`-marker row's origin labeled by the CP-008-15 lineage lookup, never guessed** (round-3 fold): the row's `sequence` against the `daemon_signing_keys` watermark windows, the open tail belonging to the active `NodeId`, which is also how `servesForOrigins` derives) — with all four I-008-13(b) exclusions applied **at selection time** (sentinel-bound daemon-scope / participant-scope rows; the `pii_payload` column never carried in any chunk — a **column** strip, never a row drop: the pii-bearing row itself is served with its signed digest and owner-claim members intact; no other session's rows; no membership-restricted-channel row outside the requester's channel membership — the I-008-14(b) structural predicate, fail-closed on an unresolvable binding and **total over both retention classes**, a compacted row's binding read from the `channelId` the signed stub projection preserves). Serve one representation per `id` — the origin-signed full row if held, else the audit stub with `stub: true` and its origin's **covering anchor record carried on the entry** (`coveringAnchor` — the anchor-plus-`stub_signature` verification path needs both halves and the anchor's control-plane upload is asynchronous, so the origin-signed anchor rides the payload rather than being looked up; carriable-not-forgeable now extends to the coordinates, whose relabeling a root-only signature could not catch — `root_signature` covers the full anchor claim per `Spec-006 §Anchoring Cadence`'s 2026-08-11 amendment, the round-2 fold; `Spec-008 §Peer History Backfill On Join (V1)`) — chunked at the application layer under the 64 KB frame ceiling (entry-total by construction: `Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules`' `EVENT_CANONICAL_BYTES_MAX` append ceiling — and its `Spec-006 §Compacted Event Format` stub-construction companion, which truncates the compactor-minted `summary` before signing so the stub representation is bounded by the same constant (round-3 fold) — guarantees every single entry, full or stub, rides one frame — no fragmentation or reassembly protocol exists; round-2 fold) with the requester's `correlationId` echoed, contiguous `ordinal`s, and a `terminal` chunk carrying `totalEntries` plus `servesForOrigins` (self-claim on answer; a foreign origin claimed only when at least one served row verifies under that origin's key — an empty foreign claim is never credited, I-008-13(b)'s cause-opaque asymmetry) plus `responderNodeId` and `responderSignature` — Ed25519 under this daemon's own session signing key (the roster key's private half, held exactly here) over the RFC 8785 canonicalization of the terminal chunk's members excluding the signature itself, the proof-of-node-identity the pairwise channel's `ParticipantId`-grained authentication cannot supply (round-2 fold: without it an empty self-claim is an unauthenticated assertion any admitted participant could mint for a `NodeId` it never held). An entitled-empty set answers as a single zero-entry terminal chunk (credited answered); a source that cannot currently serve refuses with `session.history_backfill_unavailable` ([Error Contracts §Session](../architecture/contracts/error-contracts.md#session), HTTP 503 — the OD-008r-1 registration shape, landed with this task; the transient response-level arm, never the coverage state). **Joiner half** (`history-backfill-joiner.ts`): mint one request per **other** current-member source — the joiner is its own source by construction: its origin-signed holdings are already local, its own origin `NodeId` is credited claimed from the local log, and the pairwise layer derives keys with other members' bundles only, so no self endpoint exists to request over (round-2 fold) — (participant-grained, matching relay addressing — one admitted `SessionKeyBundle` per participant per session, so each member serves through exactly one live daemon; origins held only on a member's other, detached machines resolve on the origin axis's unclaimed arms rather than on extra requests, per `Spec-008 §Peer History Backfill On Join (V1)`); credit a response's terminal declarations — `totalEntries`, `servesForOrigins`, the self-claim — only after verifying `responderSignature` under the claimed `responderNodeId`'s roster key via the CP-008-11 read **and reconciling the response: distinct verified entry `id`s across its chunks equal to the signed `totalEntries`, every declared ordinal held** (round-3 fold — the signature covers the terminal members, not the unsigned data chunks, so a tampering coordinator that drops an entry and pads with a duplicate fails reconciliation rather than earning credit; an already-held `id` counts when its entry verifies, since first-delivery-wins discards the append, never the receipt), an unsigned or wrong-key terminal marker leaving the source outstanding and the self-claim uncredited (fail-closed); for each received full-row entry drive **verify-before-append through the per-row origin-bytes check of Plan-006's production verifier** (T4.1 — imported, never re-implemented: the rule-2 Ed25519 verification over the origin's own canonical bytes under the origin `NodeId`'s roster key resolved through the CP-008-11 `runtimenode.signingkeyroster` read; the chain half is deliberately **not** invoked at this boundary — the origin's `prev_hash` / `row_hash` chain runs over the origin's own `sequence`, which does not survive the seam, and the served set is non-contiguous under the entitlement exclusions, so the joiner's own chain over appended rows is what binds them locally, exactly as ADR-017's live-relay receive semantics already provide); for each stub entry drive the same verifier's anchor-plus-stub checks over the carried `coveringAnchor` — the anchor-claim signature (coordinates and root together, `Spec-006 §Anchoring Cadence` 2026-08-11) under the same origin roster key, span coverage of the stubbed row's `sequence`, then `stub_signature` over the served stub bytes, an anchor-less or anchor-failing stub refused on the existing `anchor_missing_for_compacted_range` / `anchor_signature_invalid` modes — a failure on either path REFUSES the row, surfaces `audit_integrity_failed`, and never appends — then append: a full row under ADR-017's unchanged receive semantics through Plan-006's append seam **with the verified origin `NodeId` supplied** (CP-008-13), stamping `received_from_node_id`, emitting the conditional `receivedFromNodeId` member inside the receiver-signed bytes; **a stub entry as the received-stub row** (round-3 fold, `Spec-008 §Peer History Backfill On Join (V1)` / `Spec-006 §Post-Compaction Integrity`'s received-as-stub dispatch): `retention_class = 'audit_stub'`, the origin's projection bytes stored verbatim as `payload`, the origin's `stub_signature` retained, the marker stamped, a fresh local `sequence` — the carried anchor consumed at the boundary, the at-rest checks thereafter dispatching on the self-discriminating class (marker present, projection member absent: origin-key stub verification, `sequence` and provenance bindings exempt, every other scalar binding enforced) — and in both representations writing **neither PII column ever** (the write-side half of the Spec-006 amendment's require-absent arm — a backfill-appended row holds `pii_payload` NULL and `pii_participant_id` NULL by construction). Converge idempotently by canonical `id` — first delivery wins, and no appended row is ever rewritten in either direction (stub-after-full and full-after-stub are both discarded). Track coverage on the two axes (current-member sources answered/outstanding by terminal-marker + ordinal + distinct-`id` reconciliation; roster origins claimed/unclaimed) and surface `pending_backfill` naming every unanswered source and unclaimed origin — a departed (`revoked`) member is never a requestable source while its origin stays coverage-tracked, **and the member axis is live, never a join-time snapshot** (round-3 fold, `Spec-008 §Peer History Backfill On Join (V1)`): a membership transition re-evaluates coverage — a mid-backfill revocation cancels the outstanding request, drops the source, and re-tracks its origins on the unclaimed arms; a mid-backfill admission adds a requestable source; the terminal determination quantifies over members current at evaluation time. **Migration:** the additive nullable `session_events.received_from_node_id TEXT` column — Plan-008-owned ([cross-plan-dependencies.md §1](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#1-table-ownership-map)), Spec-006-owned semantics; NULL = origin-authored (the serving selector's predicate), non-NULL = the origin `NodeId` whose roster key verified the bytes at receive. **Second migration (CP-008-15, round-3 fold):** the additive `daemon_signing_keys` lineage rebuild — PK `(session_id)` → `(session_id, node_id)`, `superseded_at_sequence INTEGER` (NULL = active), `node_id` backfilled to the current `NodeId` — with the succession watermark stamped by the admission-time registration hook when a fresh `NodeId` takes over a carried store. **Bridge (CP-008-14, round-2 fold):** both engines are reachable only through the daemon JSON-RPC bridge — handler files under `packages/runtime-daemon/src/ipc/handlers/` registering `session.historyBackfillStart` (joiner-side: mints the `correlationId`, registers the two-axis expectations excluding self, credits the joiner's own origin, returns the request the coordinator seals per other member), `session.historyBackfillServe` (source-side, streaming over Plan-007's `streaming-primitive.ts`: runs this task's selection and signs the terminal marker with the daemon-held key), and `session.historyBackfillDeliver` (joiner-side: per-chunk verify-and-append with coverage accounting and per-chunk disposition) — an EXTEND of the Tier-1 `session.*` namespace root per the §2 root-extension class; the fourth method, `session.relayEventDeliver`, is T-008r-4-10's inbound live sibling into this same joiner engine (methods canonical at [api-payload-contracts.md §History-Backfill Bridge Method Registry](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md#history-backfill-bridge-method-registry-tier-5-plan-008-t-008r-4-14)). **Contracts:** the `HistoryBackfill*` schemas — request, entry, chunk, and the entry's `coveringAnchor` record — EXTEND this plan's own `session-join.ts` (canonical bytes base64; `daemonSignature` and the terminal `responderSignature` lowercase hex per the Plan-006 T2.3 wire convention; the anchor's `merkleRoot` / `rootSignature` base64, the anchor-family convention; the chunk's terminal arm carries `responderNodeId` / `responderSignature` per the round-2 fold). Tests: T-008r-4-T33 (I-008-13a) a valid origin-signed entry verifies through the **production** verifier and appends with `received_from_node_id` stamped to the origin `NodeId`; T-008r-4-T34 (I-008-13a) a tampered-bytes entry is refused — never appended — and surfaces `audit_integrity_failed`; T-008r-4-T35 (I-008-13a) a **validly re-signed** copy of another daemon's event (correct bytes, a legitimate non-origin key) is refused for resolving under the wrong `NodeId` — the arm a signature-only fixture passes vacuously; T-008r-4-T36 (I-008-13b, I-008-14b, AC5 serving half) the **source-side** selector fed a sentinel-bound row, another session's row, and a `direct`-channel row for a requester outside the pair emits none of the three, while the same selector call emits a `pii_payload`-bearing row as an entry whose canonical bytes carry the signed `pii_ciphertext_digest` + `pii_participant_id` members with no ciphertext anywhere in the chunk (the exclusion strips the **column**, which was never inside the canonical bytes — never the row) and emits the `direct`-channel row for a requester in the pair (firing negative controls plus positive controls); T-008r-4-T37 (I-008-13c) re-delivery converges idempotently by `id`, a stub offered after an appended full row is discarded, and a late pre-compaction full row offered after an appended stub is discarded (the compaction-race order); T-008r-4-T38 (I-008-13a/c) a predecessor-`NodeId` row served from a carried-forward store verifies under the **origin's** roster key — **labeled by the CP-008-15 lineage lookup** (round-3 arm: a two-`NodeId` lineage store labels rows on both sides of the succession watermark correctly, and `servesForOrigins` derives from the lineage rows) — and an empty foreign claim is not credited — the origin stays unclaimed; T-008r-4-T39 (I-008-13c) `pending_backfill` names every unanswered source on an all-peers-offline join (transient arm), and an unclaimed origin stays named after every other current member has answered (the terminal arm a transient-only fixture never reaches); T-008r-4-T40 (I-008-13c) a dropped terminal chunk — and separately a dropped declared ordinal — leaves the source outstanding until both arrive, an entitled-empty source answering as a zero-entry terminal chunk is credited, and a `revoked` member is never enumerated as a source while its origin stays coverage-tracked; T-008r-4-T41 (I-008-13a, I-008-14b) the serving selector never serves a row whose `received_from_node_id` is non-NULL — a received copy is re-signed bytes, the possession bound driven from the durable marker — with the positive control that the same daemon's origin-authored rows serve; T-008r-4-T42 (I-008-13a) a backfill-appended row lands with `received_from_node_id` = origin `NodeId` and its receiver-signed canonical bytes carry the conditional `receivedFromNodeId` member, while an origin-authored append stays NULL and emits no member — each checked through the production verifier, so forging the marker in either direction fails `signature_mismatch`; T-008r-4-T43 the `0NNN-received-row-provenance.ts` migration applies, is idempotent, and is registered in `migration-runner.ts` (the §2 mirror-seam arm); T-008r-4-T44 (I-008-13c) a source refusing with `session.history_backfill_unavailable` stays outstanding — the response-level refusal changes no coverage state — and a subsequent successful retry converges the same rows idempotently; T-008r-4-T45 (I-008-13a) a stub entry carrying its origin's `coveringAnchor` verifies on the full anchor-plus-stub path — the anchor-claim signature (coordinates and root, `Spec-006 §Anchoring Cadence` 2026-08-11) under the origin roster key, span coverage of the stubbed row's `sequence`, `stub_signature` over the served stub bytes — and appends, while a stub entry with **no** covering anchor, one whose anchor fails signature verification, one whose anchor's span does not cover the row, and one whose anchor coordinates were **relabeled after signing** to cover the row (the round-2 negative control a root-only preimage would pass) are each refused — never appended — surfacing `audit_integrity_failed` on the existing `anchor_missing_for_compacted_range` / `anchor_signature_invalid` modes; T-008r-4-T46 (I-008-13a) the joiner's append path writes neither PII column on any backfill-appended row — the T36 serving arm's digest-and-claim-bearing entry included — landing `pii_payload` NULL and `pii_participant_id` NULL (the write-side half of the Spec-006 amendment's require-absent arm, whose verifier half rides Plan-006 T4.1); T-008r-4-T47 (I-008-13b, I-008-14b) the entitlement and possession predicates are total over both retention classes — a compacted `direct`-channel stub, its binding read from the signed stub projection's `channelId`, is withheld from an out-of-pair requester and served to an in-pair one; a compacted row whose projection carries no `channelId` serves unrestricted; and a compacted **received** row is never served (the possession bound driven from the durable marker rule 4's scalar binding holds tamper-evident); T-008r-4-T48 (I-008-13c — round-2 fold) terminal-marker authentication and self-exclusion: a terminal chunk signed under the responder's roster key is credited with its self-claim, while an unsigned terminal marker, one signed under a wrong key, and one whose signed member set was altered after signing (a stripped or padded `servesForOrigins`) each leave the source outstanding and credit nothing — driven through the production roster-read verification, fail-closed — and the joiner mints no self-request while its own origin lands credited from the local log, **plus the round-3 arms**: a response whose coordinator drops one entry and pads with a duplicate fails distinct-`id` reconciliation against the signed `totalEntries` (no credit), a mid-backfill revocation cancels the outstanding request and re-tracks the departed member's origins unclaimed, and a mid-backfill admission adds a requestable source the terminal determination then waits on; T-008r-4-T49 (I-008-13a/c — round-2 fold) bridge integration and frame fit: the engines are driven end-to-end through the production CP-008-14 methods — `session.historyBackfillStart` registering expectations, `session.historyBackfillServe` streaming chunks whose terminal marker carries the daemon-held-key signature, `session.historyBackfillDeliver` appending with coverage accounting, and a live relayed event appending through `session.relayEventDeliver` into the same engine with `received_from_node_id` stamped — with a maximal-canonical-size entry (at the `Spec-006` `EVENT_CANONICAL_BYTES_MAX` bound) asserted to encode into a single legal 64 KB frame.

## Parallelization Notes

- Join-service work and relay-broker work can proceed in parallel once shared identity and presence contracts are stable.
- Client join surfaces should wait for reconnect-association semantics to stabilize.

## Test And Verification Plan

Tests are scoped per execution window. Tier 1 tests gate Tier 1 Phase 1 PR; Tier 5 tests gate the Plan-008-remainder PRs.

### [Tier 1] Plan-008-Bootstrap Tests (T-008b-1-T1..T12)

Substrate-only scope; no Spec-008 AC coverage at this tier (per F-008b-1-06 disclaimer in Phase 1 §Goal).

- **T-008b-1-T1** (Verifies I-008-1 gate #1) `packages/control-plane/test/server/feature-flag-gate.test.ts`: handler refuses to serve (HTTP 503) without `env.CONTROL_PLANE_BOOTSTRAP_ENABLED === '1'`, even when `env.ENVIRONMENT === 'development'` would satisfy gate #2 — isolates gate #1 refusal independently of gate #2; refusal logged + 503 returned before router dispatch.
- **T-008b-1-T2** (Verifies I-008-1 gate #2 allow-list + F-008b-1-04 + Codex PR #20 round 4 pivot) `packages/control-plane/test/server/dev-environment-gate.test.ts`: table-driven test asserts handler refuses to serve (HTTP 503) when `env.ENVIRONMENT !== 'development'` even with the bootstrap flag set, across rows `undefined`, `'production'`, `'staging'`, `'test'`, `''`. Each refusal logged before router dispatch. The default-deploy threat path (Worker published via `wrangler deploy` without `--env`, then `wrangler secret put CONTROL_PLANE_BOOTSTRAP_ENABLED 1` against that Worker, leaving `ENVIRONMENT` unset) is exercised via the `undefined` row.
- **T-008b-1-T3** Handler serves successfully with flag set in dev environment under `wrangler dev` (workerd local; `env.ENVIRONMENT === 'development'`).
- **T-008b-1-T4..T6** (Verifies I-008-3 + CP-008-1) `packages/control-plane/test/sessions/session-router.test.ts`: end-to-end tRPC integration tests for `session.create` / `session.read` / `session.join` against a `pg.Pool`-backed `Querier`. Lock-ordering (I-001-1) inherited via directory-service routing.
- **T-008b-1-T7** (Verifies CP-008-1 SSE substrate) SSE connection lifecycle: open + send synthetic `EventEnvelope` + close on disconnect. Frame shape canonical at [api-payload-contracts.md §SSE Wire Frame (Tier 1 Ratified)](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md#sse-wire-frame-tier-1-ratified) (closed 2026-04-30).
- **T-008b-1-T8** `Last-Event-ID` resumption: reconnect with header → server emits events strictly after the cursor.
- **T-008b-1-T9** Heartbeat cadence: `event: heartbeat\ndata: {}\n\n` every 15s in absence of data.
- **T-008b-1-T10** (Verifies I-008-3 enforcement #2) ESLint `no-restricted-imports` trips on direct `pg` import in `session-router.ts` or `session-subscribe-sse.ts`.
- **T-008b-1-T11** (Verifies I-008-3 enforcement #3) Unit-test introspection catches new SQL via direct `Querier` instantiation in router file.
- **T-008b-1-T12** (Verifies CP-008-1 + F-008b-1-09 round-trip) `packages/client-sdk/test/transport/sse-roundtrip.test.ts`: stub `sessionClient.subscribe` connects to the Phase 1 SSE substrate, receives synthetic `EventEnvelope`, verifies cursor-based resumption via `Last-Event-ID`. This is the highest-value Phase 1 test — proves the unblock for Plan-001 Phase 5 is real.

### [Tier 5] Plan-008-Remainder Tests

The Tier-5 tests are authored inline per task in the four `#### Tasks` blocks above (`T-008r-{P}-T{n}`), **117 tests total** (base ID spans plus the letter-suffixed review-round siblings; re-derived at campaign B12 — the prior "75" predated the PR-#129-round letter-suffix additions and the R4 id collisions resolved below — again at the 2026-07-26 strict-verification defect repair, which added `T-008r-3-T2b` + `T-008r-4-T5b`, 95 → 97; and again at the 2026-08-03 vision-reconciliation amendment, which added `T-008r-4-T30` + `T-008r-4-T31` for I-008-14(a)'s live-path recipient scoping and `T-008r-4-T32` for CP-008-12's build-incomplete resolver seam, 97 → 100; and again at the 2026-08-11 restoring readiness-audit delta (PR #323), whose T-008r-4-14 carries I-008-13's battery, I-008-14(b)'s serving-side tests, and the provenance-marker + migration arms as `T-008r-4-T33`..`T-008r-4-T49`, 100 → 117 — `T45`..`T47` (the stub anchor-carriage, PII require-absent, and stub-class arms) added by the same delta's Codex round-1 fold, `T48`/`T49` (the terminal-authentication + self-exclusion and bridge-integration + frame-fit arms) by its round-2 fold):

- **R1** `T-008r-1-T1..T14` + round-added `T4b` / `T11b` / `T12b` (17) — contract round-trips + `SessionKeyBundle` 32/32/64-byte length enforcement, relay wire-frame codec + 64 KB cap, relay migration apply / idempotency / CHECK-constraint, foundational PASETO-validation middleware (both surfaces).
- **R2** `T-008r-2-T1..T18` (18) — PASETO-gated membership join, principal-mismatch rejection, inherited ≤10 participant cap + I-001-1 lock-ordering, invite handoff, relay-degraded-join-still-valid, `SessionResumeAfterReconnect` identity re-association, cursor-only resume response, presence fan-out with no durable write.
- **R3** `T-008r-3-T1..T25` + round-added `T2b` / `T3b` / `T4c` / `T4d` / `T5b` / `T9b` / `T13b` (32) — `SessionKeyBundle` admission (a/b/c) incl. the strict-verification small-order rejection (`T2b`, 2026-07-26 defect repair), opaque-forward zero-knowledge spy assertion, discard-on-close, WSS PASETO handshake, RelayNegotiation, ≤25 DO-sharding vs ≤10 cap independence, 1-byte discriminator dispatch, `session_directory` / `relay_connections` writes.
- **R4** `T-008r-4-T1..T49` + round-added `T5b` (50; `T19` / `T20` re-assigned from the review-round `T9` / `T11` id collisions — see §Decision Log 2026-07-21; `T5b` added at the 2026-07-26 strict-verification defect repair; `T30` / `T31` / `T32` added at the 2026-08-03 vision-reconciliation amendment; `T33`..`T47` added at the 2026-08-11 restoring delta, T-008r-4-14, with `T48` / `T49` at its round-2 fold) — participant pairwise crypto + join / presence / reconnect client surfaces + the campaign-B12 `ResumingEventClient` resume-and-subscribe consumer (`T21..T29`, I-008-12) + the coordinator-owned scoped fan-out (`T30` / `T31`, I-008-14(a)) and its resolver-seam construction guard (`T32`, CP-008-12) + the peer-history backfill engine (`T33..T49`, I-008-13 + I-008-14(b), T-008r-4-14).

**The four [Plan-008 §Done Checklist](#done-checklist) Done-Checklist crypto tests** are authored at R4: **`T-008r-4-T7`** (X25519 + XChaCha20-Poly1305 encrypt→decrypt round-trip), **`T-008r-4-T9`** (ephemeral X25519 secret + derived pairwise keys zeroized on session close — I-008-6 forward secrecy), **`T-008r-4-T6`** (HKDF-SHA256 pairwise derivation), and **`T-008r-4-T5`** (Ed25519 bundle-signature verification).

**Spec-008 AC coverage** (`Spec-008 §Acceptance Criteria`): **AC1** authenticated session join — R4 `T-008r-4-T11` / `T-008r-4-T15` / `T-008r-4-T17` (join valid even with `relayEndpoint` omitted), over the R2 PASETO-gated membership join; **AC2** direct↔relay switching without a second identity — R4 `T-008r-4-T13` (reconnect re-associates the same identity, transport re-attach kept distinct), over R3 RelayNegotiation; **AC3** control-plane-is-not-execution-authority — R3 `T-008r-3-T8` (zero-knowledge forward: broker runs no decrypt / HKDF / ECDH path — I-008-5) + `T-008r-3-T15` (RelayNegotiation asserts no execution authority — I-008-10); **AC4** entitled-history recovery on join (I-008-13) — R4 `T-008r-4-T33`..`T-008r-4-T42` plus `T-008r-4-T44`..`T-008r-4-T49`, every I-008-13-tagged test (T-008r-4-14, tasked 2026-08-11 by the restoring readiness-audit delta, `T48` / `T49` at its round-2 fold; `T-008r-4-T43`, the migration-mechanics arm, carries no invariant tag); **AC5** membership-restricted channel scoping (I-008-14) — live half covered by R4 `T-008r-4-T30` / `T-008r-4-T31` / `T-008r-4-T32` (scoped fan-out, the fail-closed refusal, and the unbound-resolver construction refusal), serving half by R4 `T-008r-4-T36` / `T-008r-4-T41` / `T-008r-4-T47` (the source-side channel exclusion, the possession-bound serving selector, and the stub-class arms, same task).

## Rollout Order

1. Ship authenticated join and presence registration
2. Enable reconnect association and replay-oriented join responses
3. Enable relay negotiation as a secondary connectivity path

## Rollback Or Fallback

- Disable relay negotiation and keep direct control-plane join only if relay rollout regresses while preserving session-join correctness.

## Risks And Blockers

- Session-join traffic requirements for admin or recovery flows remain unresolved (operational sizing deferred; see parent [Spec-008](../specs/008-control-plane-relay-and-session-join.md))
- Presence duplication is likely if reconnect association is not made authoritative early

## Decision Log

| Date | Event | Notes |

| 2026-04-14 | Plan drafted | Initial Plan-008 authored by Codex |
| 2026-04-30 | Runtime amended (BL-104 / C-4 resolved) | Tier 1 bootstrap and Tier 5 production runtime confirmed as Cloudflare Workers via `@trpc/server/adapters/fetch` per [ADR-014](../decisions/014-trpc-control-plane-api.md). Resolution path (b) chosen: Plan-008 amended; ADR-014 honored verbatim — no ADR amendment. Local development uses workerd via `wrangler dev` (Miniflare v3) per [ADR-014 Assumption #1](../decisions/014-trpc-control-plane-api.md#assumptions-audit). I-008-1 gate #2 reformulated from "loopback-bind gate" to environment-scoped deployment refusal since Cloudflare Workers do not expose a host-bind surface; gate-semantics specifics evolved across PR-#20 review rounds — **see the round-4 row below for the current canonical gate semantics (allow-list `env.ENVIRONMENT === 'development'`) and the round-3 row for the intermediate deny-list draft**. Implementers MUST follow the current plan body (I-008-1, T-008b-1-1, T-008b-1-T2) and the round-4 Decision Log row, NOT the intermediate semantics that the BL-104 amendment first proposed. SSE substrate selection settled: tRPC v11's shared HTTP resolver (`resolveResponse.ts` upstream) detects subscription procedures and produces SSE-streaming `Response` natively when invoked via `fetchRequestHandler`; no separate adapter required. Primary-source verification: [resolveResponse.ts on tRPC main branch](https://github.com/trpc/trpc/blob/main/packages/server/src/unstable-core-do-not-import/http/resolveResponse.ts). Free-tier capacity verified for V1: Workers Free supplies 100k requests/day with no effective limit on SSE response duration per [Cloudflare Agents HTTP and SSE docs](https://developers.cloudflare.com/agents/api-reference/http-sse/), comfortably covering a 50-developer team's projected 3-12k req/day control-plane workload. | 2026-05-31 | Tier 5 audit Codex review (PR #129) — relay-envelope `seq` on the wire + WSS token carriage (two ratified design decisions, Spec-008 amended at swap) | Codex flagged two real gaps where the plan/spec described a mechanism that could not be built as written. Both resolved by **reusing an existing primitive** (not inventing one), and the consequent [Spec-008](../specs/008-control-plane-relay-and-session-join.md) amendments are executed at this swap (content-only; Spec-008 stays `approved` — the same plan-scoped amendment-at-swap bucket as the Tier-4 Spec-006 enum bump and D-022-5). **(1) Relay envelope omitted the per-sender `seq` (I-008-8).** The envelope was `{recipient_id, nonce, ciphertext+tag}` with the `seq` described as living only in the AEAD associated data — but the receiver cannot reconstruct the associated data (and therefore cannot decrypt or replay-check) without the `seq` on the wire. **Resolution:** the `seq` (8-byte big-endian uint64) is carried in the envelope **in the clear** and bound into the AAD (`recipient_id ‖ seq`); a tampered `seq` fails the AEAD tag — the standard DTLS/QUIC/Noise record-header idiom (sequence-in-clear, authenticated-as-AAD). Envelope became `{recipient_id, seq, nonce, ciphertext+tag}` at this round (round-7 then prepended `sender_ephemeral` — see the row below) across `Spec-008 §Relay Encryption` / `Spec-008 §Message Framing` / `Spec-008 §Trust Properties` + Plan-008 I-008-5/I-008-8 + T-008r-3-3 / T-008r-4-6 (the broker forwards `seq` opaquely; the R1 frame codec is unaffected — `seq` lives inside the opaque `[payload]`). Test added: T-008r-4-T9 (tampered wire `seq` fails the tag). **[Test id re-assigned 2026-07-21, campaign B12: this round's seq-integrity test is now T-008r-4-T19 — the `T9` id had collided with T-008r-4-7's pre-existing zeroization T9.]** **(2) WSS handshake presented the scheme name, not the token.** The handshake was `Sec-WebSocket-Protocol: paseto-v4` — the literal scheme string, carrying no token. **Resolution:** the two-value subprotocol carriage `paseto-v4, <base64url(connectionToken)>` — value[0] names the scheme (echoed as the negotiated subprotocol), value[1] is the base64url-encoded token; the established Kubernetes apiserver bearer-token-over-subprotocol idiom ([kubernetes/kubernetes#47740](https://github.com/kubernetes/kubernetes/pull/47740)), since browsers cannot set custom WebSocket headers. base64url-encoding keeps the value within the RFC 7230 `token` grammar the subprotocol field requires, independent of token format (websearch-verified 2026-05-31). Applied across `Spec-008 §Relay Connection Lifecycle` + Plan-008 I-008-4 / T-008r-1-5 / T-008r-3-5 / T-008r-4-8. |
| 2026-05-31 | Tier 5 audit Codex review (PR #129) **round 7** — relay envelope sender-resolution handle (ratified design decision, Spec-008 amended at swap) | Codex flagged that `decryptFromSender(senderId, …)` needs a `senderId` the recipient has no wire source for in a ≥3-party session: the round-6 envelope `{recipient_id, seq, nonce, ciphertext+tag}` carries no sender identifier, so a recipient cannot select which cached pairwise key to verify under (or which per-sender replay counter applies). **Resolution (reusing an existing value, not inventing one):** the envelope carries `sender_ephemeral` — the sender's session ephemeral X25519 public key, already exchanged in its `SessionKeyBundle` and already the key of the recipient's per-pair derived-keys map (T-008r-4-5) — as a **cleartext O(1) key-selection handle**. Sender authenticity comes from **key-selection**: the AEAD tag verifies only under the pairwise key the handle selects, so a relay-mislabeled `sender_ephemeral` picks the wrong key and fails the tag. `sender_ephemeral` is therefore **not** AEAD-bound — the AAD stays `recipient_id ‖ seq`. The cleartext handle follows the **Matrix Megolm session-id lookup idiom** (lookup id in the clear, never trusted as a sender claim — MSC3700 deprecated _trusting_ a cleartext sender claim); authenticity, separately, comes from the **pairwise key** — the Olm/Signal Double Ratchet property that a valid tag under a two-party key proves the peer sent it, **not** from Megolm-style key-selection, which is non-authenticating in Megolm's group-key setting and is exactly why Megolm carries a separate Ed25519 message signature. Distinct from MLS's AAD-bound SenderData and from trial-decryption (the mixnet pattern: O(N), no cached key set). The per-session ephemeral is unlinkable across sessions and reveals nothing to the relay the connection does not. Envelope is now `{sender_ephemeral, recipient_id, seq, nonce, ciphertext+tag}` across `Spec-008 §Relay Encryption` / `Spec-008 §Message Framing` / `Spec-008 §Trust Properties` + `Plan-008 §I-008-5 — Relay is zero-knowledge (E2EE; never plaintext)` / T-008r-3-3 / T-008r-4-6; the stale `(recipient_id, nonce, ciphertext+tag)` at `docs/architecture/security-architecture.md §V1 Relay Encryption: Pairwise X25519 + XChaCha20-Poly1305` (a round-6 sweep miss — it was also missing `seq`) is corrected in the same pass. R1 frame codec unaffected (`sender_ephemeral` rides inside the opaque `[payload]`). `decryptFromSender(senderId, …)` → `decryptFromSender(envelope)`. Test added: T-008r-4-T11 (a relabeled `sender_ephemeral` selects the wrong pairwise key and fails the tag). **[Test id re-assigned 2026-07-21, campaign B12: now T-008r-4-T20 — the `T11` id had collided with T-008r-4-9's pre-existing join T11.]** Websearch-verified 2026-05-31 against MLS RFC 9420 SenderData (AAD-bound), Olm/Signal Double Ratchet (the pairwise key authenticates the sender implicitly), and Matrix Megolm (group key → cleartext session-id lookup **plus** a separate Ed25519 signature for authenticity). |

Importing it there drags a wrong-direction dependency on the entire control-plane into the self-host relay bundle, violating [Spec-025](./025-self-hostable-node-relay.md) AC-180 ("the hosted backend is a separate build target"). Three surfaces consume it: the R3 broker, the R4 client-SDK transport (T-008r-4-8), and the Plan-025 self-host relay. **Resolution:** the `RelayFrameType` declaration moves to `packages/contracts/src/session-join.ts` (R1 T-008r-1-3) as a contracts-owned wire-protocol symbol all three import. Authored as a cross-package-safe `as const` object + derived union, **not** a `const enum` (which transpile-only / `isolatedModules` builds cannot inline across a package boundary — the modern TS guidance for shared wire constants). This **supersedes** the original author-resolved R1 decision (§Decision Log NS-17 R1) that deferred the numeric assignment to R3: the values are a wire fact, so contracts owns them. `encodeRelayFrame`/`decodeRelayFrame` keep the opaque `number` at the wire boundary; consumers map `number` ↔ `RelayFrameType`. [cross-plan-dependencies.md §1](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#1-table-ownership-map) records the contracts ownership; the R3 routing tests (T-008r-3-T21/22/23) are unchanged. Doc-only amendment (no relay code shipped yet); Plan-008 stays `approved`. |

Classification per the [runbook §Status Flip Rule](../operations/plan-implementation-readiness-audit-runbook.md#status-flip-rule): the bundle carries row-3-class content (new invariant + CP), but Plan-008 already sits at `review` (NS-17 flip, 2026-05-30), so no status transition occurs — the design §4 row's declared "Already `review`; promoted in W2.5" classification (campaign plan Task 23). Dep-map registrations executed in-cell in the same PR: §2 client-sdk extender cell (+ `resumingEventClient.ts`) and §3 Plan-008 row (the Plan-006 T4.7/T4.3/T4.5 consume edge). **Codex round 1 (PR #236, four P1s folded):** the apply callbacks made promise-aware with settlement-gated mark advance (a delayed or rejected apply is never overtaken and never falsely marked applied), the subscription repositioned from the floor to the snapshot `latest` (the catch-up buffer bounds to live-tail traffic during the drain — a floor-positioned subscribe would double-replay O(retained-history) into memory), the handle widened to `{ close(), done: Promise<void> }` (terminal live-loop failures reject `done`; re-invocation is the reconnect path — no automatic transport reconnect, per the SDK marshal-don't-derive posture), and every projection reset now reinitializes the high-water-mark atomically with the reset (T-008r-4-T29 asserts the re-drained pre-gap prefix re-applies). **Codex round 2 (PR #236, three P1 + one P2 folded):** the drain bounded to the captured snapshot's `latest` (stop condition = snapshot, never moving-head `hasMore` exhaustion — a sustained writer could otherwise extend the drain and grow the buffer without bound); I-008-12(c) honestly scoped — at-most-once is per resume cycle, the cross-cycle residual is at-least-once bounded by ack cadence with the caller's `acknowledged` as the single durable authority (a decorator-persisted watermark would mint a competing source of truth; effective exactly-once = ack-after-durable-apply, the caller's discipline); the gap-reset path generalized to drained pages via a floor-initialized contiguity check (sound because Plan-006's surviving log is dense — compaction gap-inverts — so a non-contiguous drained page can only mean the retention check-to-read race; closes the stale-floor TOCTOU as the P2 asked, without adding a Plan-006 read-side floor revalidation surface); and the unwired-capability hole closed — T-008r-4-10's coordinator now owns the timeline leg as the production composition point (constructs the decorator, drives + re-invokes `resumeAndSubscribe`; T-008r-4-T13 became the three-leg reconnect integration arm). **Codex round 3 (PR #236, two P1 + two P2 folded):** the version-skew branch hardened — a pre-widening responder's `latest` may still carry the legacy opaque event-id cursor form the T4.7 correction retired, so the skew branch never decodes or reuses it and runs as a single cursorless `subscribe` full replay (no separate drain, no snapshot stop condition); a `hasMore: false` drain exhaustion ending below the snapshot `latest` is a discontinuity by the witness rule (the snapshot's `latest` proves a row existed at that position, so a short drain means rows were deleted after the floor check — the total-purge race resets, never a silent partial resume); the coordinator's re-invocation serialized to at most one live resume cycle per session (close the prior handle + await its `done` settlement before re-invoking — two concurrent cycles would interleave resets and applies against one projection); and the reset-settlement contract authored — `onProjectionReset` resolves only after the caller durably records the forced-earliest rebuild in its projection's commit domain, honored by stripping `acknowledged` through the injected `readSession` seam until the rebuilt projection re-acks (no ack-write wire op exists to lower the daemon-held cursor), so a cycle dying between reset settlement and re-drain completion re-floors at `earliest`, never at the stale ack past the cleared prefix. **Lead self-audit (round-4 window):** the reset-settlement contract bound explicitly to **every** reset path — the step-1 floor-selection resets (gap predicate, unresolvable ack, version skew) as well as the step-4 discontinuity resets — because the predicate and unresolvable paths self-correct on re-invocation (the same condition re-fires) but the skew path does not: a skew cycle dying mid-full-replay whose next invocation lands on an upgraded responder (`earliest` now present) would trust the populated pre-skew ack over the partial rebuild without the standing marker (I-008-12(d) carries the cross-reference); and I-008-12(b)'s discontinuity enumeration extended with the total-purge witness shape its own T-008r-4-T29 third arm already tests. **Codex round 4 (PR #236, one P1 folded):** the live-gap reset trigger re-attributed from T4.7's iterator-side monotonicity rejection — untyped on the `LocalSubscriptionConsumer.next()` channel, indistinguishable from a transport failure to a compose-only consumer — to the decorator's own step-2 contiguity check, which sees every live envelope pre-apply (the offending envelope in hand); any iterator rejection is uniformly step-5 terminal (`done` rejects — never a destructive reset on an ambiguous signal), and a gap the iterator rejects before yielding the envelope still converges to safety: the coordinator re-invokes and the fresh cycle's floor/contiguity/witness checks re-derive the state from the durable log, healing a mere delivery artifact by re-drain with no reset while genuine loss still resets. T4.3's convergence rule is honored without a Plan-006 surface change; T-008r-4-T28 gains the channel-discrimination arm. **Codex round 5 (PR #236, three P1s folded — final round):** (1) the version-skew fallback was unreachable as written — a responder lacking `earliest` necessarily predates Plan-006 Phase 4's entire event-replay surface (T4.3, T4.5, T4.7 land together), so the promised cursorless `subscribe` would method-not-found after a destructive reset; the branch now REFUSES before any destruction or wire call (typed SDK-local unsupported-responder rejection; projection and `acknowledged` intact; the coordinator surfaces degraded timeline state and re-probes on a later reconnect) — T4.3's conservative-full-replay consumer sentence presumes a read surface the same responder cannot expose, so the refusal is recorded here as the consumer-side resolution with no Plan-006 edit (compose-only); this supersedes the round-3 single-cursorless-subscribe shape and the round-4-window skew-marker rationale (the skew path resets nothing now). (2) The at-most-once guarantee re-scoped from resume cycle to **projection epoch** — a settled `onProjectionReset` clears the projection and begins a new epoch the re-drained prefix re-applies into, resolving the (c)-vs-T29 contradiction the round-1 watermark-reset fix introduced (a caller with non-resettable external side effects anchors to the reset callback, the epoch boundary). (3) `done` resolves only on locally-requested `close()` — an unsolicited stream end (Plan-007's consumer contract carries a normal completion path, e.g. a daemon-side cancel) now rejects `done`, closing the silent-stop hole the coordinator's rejected-`done`-only re-invocation would otherwise leave; T26 re-authored and T28 extended accordingly. |
| 2026-07-26 | Ed25519 bundle-admission verification pinned strict (`{ zip215: false }`) — defect repair, no status transition | **Three prescriptive verify sites corrected.** Each specified the noble call shape without its verification-mode option, and `@noble/curves` defaults its `ed25519` wrapper to `zip215: true` — the consensus-friendly ZIP-215 decode rules, which **skip the small-order public-key rejection**. Against a small-order public key the `[8][k]A` term drops out of the cofactored verification equation, so a single `(R, S)` pair verifies for _every_ message: **universal forgery** of `SessionKeyBundle` admission. The corrected sites are **T-008r-3-1** (the broker's direct `@noble/curves` admission call), **T-008r-4-2** (the fixture-signer test oracle, now asserted under strict verification so a fixture cannot pass on wrapper leniency), and **T-008r-4-4** (`verifyPeerBundle`). **T-008r-4-4 is the highest-risk of the three**: it verifies against `bundle.ed25519IdentityPublic`, an **attacker-supplied** key read off a fetched peer bundle before any control-plane identity binding has run and before that peer's `ephemeralX25519Public` enters ECDH — so the attacker need not find a weak key, it simply chooses a small-order one. `zip215: false` selects strict RFC 8032 / FIPS 186-5 verification, which additionally rejects non-canonical point encodings (`y >= p`). **The option MUST be passed literally at each call site:** noble resolves both `{}` and `{ zip215: undefined }` back to the permissive wrapper default, so hoisting it into a shared or spread options object silently reinstates the forgery **with no diff at the call site** — this is why the plan prescribes the literal option rather than a shared verify helper. The same pin and its regression guard are in evidence at `packages/crypto-paseto/src/v4-public.ts` + `packages/crypto-paseto/src/__tests__/ed25519-strict-verification.test.ts`; the `docs/superpowers/` crypto-paseto substrate plan + design spec were corrected in the same pass so their mirrored reference signature cannot re-seed the defect. **Two tests added** so the corrected prescription is detectable rather than merely stated — a prescription to verify strictly whose test list cannot distinguish strict from permissive would ship the corrected behavior untested: `T-008r-3-T2b` (broker rejects a small-order `ed25519_identity_public`) and `T-008r-4-T5b` (`verifyPeerBundle` rejects a small-order `ed25519IdentityPublic` and derives no pairwise key from it), each carrying a bare-noble **negative control** that fails if the option is dropped. Test census re-derived **95 → 97** (R1 17 / R2 18 / R3 31 → 32 / R4 29 → 30), counted from the task bodies rather than incremented. **Tier-5 Done Checklist ranges repaired in the same pass:** the R1 and R3 rows claimed `T-008r-1-T1..T14` / `T-008r-3-T1..T25` green while silently omitting their round-added letter-suffixed siblings (R1 `T4b` / `T11b` / `T12b`; R3 `T3b` / `T4c` / `T4d` / `T5b` / `T9b` / `T13b`) — a false completeness claim in the checklist that gates each phase, and the bare-range notation is what let the drift hide. All four Tier-5 rows now enumerate their suffixed siblings and carry the per-phase count (17 / 18 / 32 / 30 = 97). The R1 omission predates this repair and is unrelated to the `zip215` defect; it is corrected here rather than left standing in a checklist this pass already touches. The three **descriptive** `ed25519.verify` mentions — the Tier-5-remainder dependency bullet, the R3 phase Goal, and the R3 `Consumes:` line — name the symbol without a call shape, do not contradict the corrected rule, and are deliberately left unedited. **Defect repair, not a scope change: Plan-008 Status deliberately remains `approved`.** This changes _how_ three tasks verify, not _what_ they build: **no new invariant** (deliberately declined — strict verification is the existing I-008-7a "verify the Ed25519 signature" obligation read correctly, not a new obligation), no new table, no new error code, no new cross-plan dependency; scope, schema, and dependencies unchanged. |
| 2026-08-03 | V1 product-vision reconciliation — peer-to-joiner history backfill + per-channel relay recipient scoping contracted (Spec-008 amendment) — Status flipped `approved → review` | [Spec-008](../specs/008-control-plane-relay-and-session-join.md) gained §Peer History Backfill On Join (V1), contracting the `vision.md §The Collaboration Model` late-joiner claim that previously had **no** transport: [ADR-017](../decisions/017-shared-event-sourcing-scope.md) Option B leaves the control plane with no session events to serve, the relay zero-knowledge, and pre-join ciphertext undecryptable to a new member. The mechanism is [Spec-014 §Cross-Node Artifact Relay (V1)](../specs/014-artifacts-files-and-attachments.md#cross-node-artifact-relay-v1)'s membership-epoch re-publication generalized from artifacts to the event stream — peers re-publish the joiner's entitled history over the existing pairwise envelope as an application-level payload (the Spec-014 artifact-key-attestation carriage precedent: **no new frame message type**, no `SessionKeyBundle` change), the joiner verifies each event against its **originating** daemon's roster-resolved key before appending, then appends under ADR-017's unchanged receive semantics. **Adds:** invariants **I-008-13** (origin-verified / entitlement-scoped / never-silently-empty) and **I-008-14** (channel-scoped traffic is member-scoped on the live path and the serving path), plus obligations **CP-008-11** (read-only consume of Plan-006 T4.10's `runtimenode.signingkeyroster`, whose reciprocal already exists in [Security Architecture §Per-Event Daemon Signature](../architecture/security-architecture.md#per-event-daemon-signature)) and **CP-008-12** (recipient scoping provided **forward** to Plan-016/Spec-016). **Adds no** table, transport, relay frame type, envelope member, or error code; the degraded state's `session.history_backfill_unavailable` code is reserved in the spec and registers in `error-contracts.md` with the backfill task (OD-008r-1 shape). New normative behavior on an `approved` plan is the same new-contract-surface shape that flipped Plan-006 (2026-07-29, 2026-08-02) and Plan-024 (2026-08-02), so §Preconditions' paired-spec box re-opens and Status flips `review`; the restoring targeted readiness-audit delta authors the R4 backfill task, re-checks the box, and restores `approved`. **Second leg — per-channel relay recipient scoping.** [Spec-016](../specs/016-multi-agent-channels-and-orchestration.md)'s same-day `direct` two-human channel named confidentiality from non-member humans as a relay-layer property it consumes but does not define; `Spec-008 §Per-Channel Recipient Scoping (V1)` defines it, and I-008-14 binds it here. An event whose `payload` carries a `channelId` resolving to a membership-restricted channel is sealed to that channel's member set **intersected** with the session's current recipient set, and is never served on backfill outside that set (I-008-13(b)'s exclusion count grows three → four). The predicate is structural rather than an event-type list (the canonical envelope member set is closed, so the binding is always a payload member, and a type list fails open on the first taxonomy addition it misses); `main` resolves unrestricted by construction, so the predicate is total; an unresolvable binding fails **closed** on both paths. Unlike CP-008-11 this obligation is **not** free of reciprocal debt: the [cross-plan-dependencies.md §3](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#3-inter-plan-dependency-graph) Plan-016 row owes a new Plan-016 → Plan-008 edge, and Plan-016's D-016-21 / CP-016-14 surfaces still describe the property as unresolved — both fix-in-place at swap on the owning surfaces (F-008r-C convention), neither made from this plan. Tasked growth: T-008r-4-10 gains the coordinator-owned recipient-set derivation over a forward-declared injected `ChannelRecipientScopeResolver` (CP-008-12, the CP-008-8 own-the-operation-never-the-custody shape — an unbound resolver is build-incomplete, never admit-all, because admit-all here means full-session fan-out) plus tests `T-008r-4-T30` / `T-008r-4-T31` and `T-008r-4-T32` (the construction-refusal arm that makes the build-incomplete rule detectable rather than merely prescribed — the 2026-07-26 `zip215` row's lesson applied), re-deriving the Tier-5 census 97 → 100 (R4 30 → 33). Two ACs were added to Spec-008 (AC4 backfill, AC5 scoping); the Done Checklist's "all verified" AC row — which had gone stale at AC4 in this same amendment — is re-derived to enumerate all five. **Explicitly not claimed:** scoping bounds which daemons hold the ciphertext and does not exclude agents; agent exclusion stays Plan-016 CP-016-14's daemon obligation at agent-context assembly (I-008-14(c)). |
